Title: Vampire: The Masquerade- Aeterna Servitus
ALT Title: Vampire: The Masquerade- Eternal Servitude
By: Paddy
Writing Start Date: 16th July, 2026
Writing End Date: ongoing
Fandoms: World Of Darkness (WOD), Vampire: The Masquerade (VTM), Mage: The Ascension (MTA)
Genre(s): Horror, Urban Fantasy, Drama, Thriller, Intrigue, Action-Adventure, Gothic Punk, Gothic Horror, Supernatural, Tragedy, Magical Realism, Modern Gothic, Political Horror, Social Horror, Economic Horror
Summary: A Ghoul by the name of Cassandra is plunged into a vast, secretive, and dark world that she only has a theoretical understanding of. Allegiances will be tested, decisions must be made. The steep price that one pays for life eternal is...eternal servitude. Favors are owed. Favors must now be repaid. Tomorrow may not even come, not in this world of darkness.
Content/Trigger Warnings: Intense Violence, Blood & Gore, Torture, Psychological/Mental Torture, Strong Language, Slurs, Bigotry/Hate, Racism, Queerphobia, Sexual Content/Scenes, Full & Partial Nudity, Alcohol/Substance Consumption, Smoking, Human & Animal Death, Xenophobia, Ableism, and so on.

Act 1: British Autumn
Gloughchester, England (NOT to be confused with Gloucester, or Gloucestershire for that matter).
The year is 201X. The air is crispy and the colors of the autumn season are warm, inviting, and somewhat welcoming. Yet, that jolly ol' British "gray and gloomy" feel persists in the overall atmosphere.
A few birds sing sweetly in the distance. The world seems to be at peace. Yet, a sense of unease still lingers in the air.
Her cabin is located quite a way off from the city proper. So far, in fact, that even its bustling sounds cannot be heard. It's a quaint little place, nestled amongst the sturdy trees and rolling hills of the countryside. A small, cobblestone path leads up to the front door.
The cabin itself is quite old, with a rustic charm that is not easy to come by in the modern age. Yet, it is remarkably well-maintained. Smooth wooden flooring, a solid roof, and an impressive stone fireplace. It's more than enough for her needs, and she is quite happy with it.
She has been here for quite some time now. He...lives not that far away, in the fancier house, the real crowning jewel of this part of Gloughchester.
Inside, she readies herself to go and meet with him. She has a Rubenesque physique and is in her late-20s. Her brown hair is long and cascades down to her shoulders. She has lovely hazel eyes. She is dressed in a comfortable outfit of jeans, a t-shirt, and a light jacket.
The lass is seated in front of her dresser, drying her hair. The sound of her hairdryer dominates the room. She dries her brunette hair whilst smiling at her reflection in a rather cheeky fashion.
After that, she takes a deep breath and closes her eyes. He called on her this evening. Last night, all he told her was to come to his house prepared as soon as the sun had set.
He had that ever-so-familiar distance and withdrawment about him. It's something that she herself has gotten fairly inured to over the years that she has not only known him but also served him. He is not an easy man to understand, that much is for certain.
She rises from her seat with a sort of semi-practiced grace and goes to get her things. She grabs her purse, keys, and nothing much else.
"I don't need anything else, do I?" she asks herself, her accent utterly Northern and thick.
She shakes her head. "No, no. Cor blimey, I got another...eh...hour before he gets up, yeah. May as well do some light reading."
She goes to the bookshelf and picks out a random book. She shrugs, throws herself on the recliner, leans her head back, and begins to read. The time passes by rather quickly.
She breathes out a few times and readies herself. She knows how people like him tend to get soon after waking from a slumber that lasts throughout the whole day.
"Alright, alright. Deep breaths. He--he's not gonna lunge at ya and bite ya, right? Eh. I should just keep me distance."
She makes her way out of the cabin, locking the door behind her. She hugs herself when a light breeze blows through the trees.
"It's bloody cold out tonight, innit?"
The woman makes her way through paths which she has traversed (by foot) so many times before. In the few years that she's lived here, she has gotten to know the surrounding area very well.
Coming here from London was more of his idea- she just had to tag along for the ride. Nevertheless, she has always been quite fond of nature, and living out here has done wonders for her mental health.
Whether it's done the same for him, on the other hand, is hard to determine. Even after all these years, he has been a fairly closed-off person. But then again, he has never been the most social of people. His times were very different from hers.
They are separated by decades of history. Yet, they are Bound by the Blood.
While walking the winding paths, she licks her lips and tries to remember when her next feeding is supposed to be. She stops and gives the sky above her a good, long stare. Shrouded by clouds, the moon's light is barely visible.
"Ah, English weather. Never gets old!" she says and follows it up with a long chuckle.
Soon enough, she arrives at the front of his...Haven.
It's a Bauhaus-inspired (as in the school of design) structure nestled between the thick trees. Sleek and modern, it's definitely quite the contrast to her own humble cottage.
(Not that she complains, mind you! She knows better than to complain.)
She stops and admires the plain white walls, the floor-to-ceiling windows, and the impressive glass door. It's a beautiful piece of architecture, that much is for sure. And the price that he got it for is a story in and of itself.
She raps her knuckles on the glass door and patiently waits for him to answer. A few moments pass by, and she hears his voice coming from within.
His accent is somewhat rough but not necessarily caustic, "It's open, Cassandra." His delivery is dry, lacking in any real emotion.
She nods and opens the door. She happily calls out, "Hiya, Greg! Are y--you awake or are you still feeling a bit tired?"
The lights inside are dimmed. The temperature is cool, almost cold. She shivers slightly and rubs her arms. The flooring of his Haven is made of marble.
The interior is minimalist, stark, and modern. He kept most of the previous owner's furniture and decorations. There are a few paintings on the walls, mostly abstract pieces that he tends to get lost in while staring at them.
She looks around and doesn't see him anywhere.
Cassandra has a look of concern on her face when she calls out, "Greg? You there? Y--you wanted to see me tonight? Remember?"
He emerges behind her, having silently moved through the shadows. His voice is soft yet commanding, "Yes, I remember. I don't need reminding of what I already know."
She turns around and sees him standing there. He is a tall and pale man with thinning hair, scruffy facial hair, and lifeless blue eyes. He is middle-aged but looks far older than that.
He is dressed in a green t-shirt and dark sweatpants. His face is angular and somewhat gaunt.
Cassandra smiles and nervously compliments him, "Looking...nice tonight." Her follow-up smile is just as nervous.
The wrinkles on Gregory's face deepen as he glares at her in a very dry and unimpressed fashion, "Hm. If you say so."
"You...uh...fed yet? Need me to help you, you know...?"
"No. That can wait, Cassandra. I summoned you here for a purpose. Make yourself comfortable. I'll need to retrieve something. After that, we'll sit down and have a talk. It is time, Cassandra."
Gregory wordlessly turns on his heels and strides up the stairs.
Cassandra watches him go with a curious expression on her face. Her cheeks redden slightly, and she feels her heart skip a few beats.
She thinks to herself, "Time for what? Bloody 'ell, he's always so vague with me, yeah? Maybe I should 'ave a talk with him about that. But, uh...I dunno if I'm ready for that, innit?"
For now, Cassandra scratches her head and sits down on his fancy L-shaped couch. She leans back and folds 1 leg over the other.
A few minutes later, Gregory comes back down the stairs, holding a piece of paper in his cold, dead hands. He looks at Cassandra and silently hands it to her.
On it, are listed the names of the Clans of the Kindred, along with a few Bloodlines. Cassandra's face scrunches up in confusion.
Gregory sits down with a groan and anticipates her questions. He dryly begins, "I'm sure that you have many questions. Best to get them all out right away. Ask." He lazily motions for her to begin.
Cassandra blinks a few times, "What's this?"
Gregory answers, "As you are very well aware, over the years, I have been something of an art dealer. I needn't elaborate on what I hope you already have knowledge of, yes?"
"Yeah. What's that got to do with a list of all the Clans...and a few Bloodlines, though? Isn't having something like this lying around considered a Masquerade breach?"
Gregory shrugs, "It makes very little difference to me on nights like these, Cassandra. Put that aside for a moment. During my...time, I have come across an array of Kindred. Certain deals and promises were made- formally, informally, or otherwise. I strongly feel that now is the time to make good on these promises. All of them."
Cassandra furrows her brows, "Why is this the first time that I'm hearing about all of this? Also, all of them? Cor blimey, you owe every single Clan on this list a--a favor of some kind, eh?"
"Some of them owe me. Personally."
"Oh. Wow. That should be mighty useful in getting out of a sticky situation, yeah?"
"Hm. Perhaps. Perhaps it would only serve to thrust me into even greater messes. But I digress. This is where you come in. As my Ghoul, I am authorizing you to not only act but also speak on my behalf."
"Wow. Uhm..." the words fail to form on her lips.
Gregory raises a brow, "I feel that you have something to say. Please, say it now rather than keeping it to yourself. I have never been a cruel Domitor to you and I most certainly don't intend to ever be as such."
Cassandra takes a deep breath, "This is--blimey, this is quite a lot. ALL the Clans? You've been busy! So, that FINALLY explains all those trips you used to take for weeks on end! How do I factor into this equation? Is it permissible for a Domitor to authorize his Ghoul to do what you said? I feel that territory is a tad murky, Greg."
Gregory plainly replies, "It is permissible because I say it is so. Truth be told, I'd rather not deal with some of the...individuals on this list. But I have made promises. Promises that I intend to keep. And many of them owe me, and it's time to, pardon the crudeness, cash in on those favors."
Cassandra takes a deep breath and nods slowly, processing the weight of her immortal master's words.
Gregory continues, "There is a few more things that I must tell you."
Cassandra chuckles, "I'm all ears, Greg!" She tugs on her ears.
Gregory ignores her attempt at humor, "You are free to tackle it in any way that you so please. I will elaborate on who owes me and to whom I owe. Us Kindred deal in something far greater than the fluctuating pettiness of the fiat currency--"
"Favors. I know. I know all too bleedin' well."
"Hm. Yes. You do. Now will be the appropriate time to deploy all of that accumulated knowledge and insight that you have been thought. I expect nothing less than your best efforts."
"Sure."
Gregory insists, "I mean it, Cassandra. You will be representing me. You will also be representing my--our Clan...Toreador. For me to tell you to be on your best behavior would be a disservice to you. But you have always been a rather...clever woman."
The Ghoul smirks lightly, "I suppose so, yeah."
The Aesthete firmly reminds her of her place within the structure of the Camarilla, "It's the insect's lesson: be on your best behavior around someone who could easily crush you like 1."
She gulps as a look of fear washes over her face. She nods and quietly says, "I--I understand, yeah. I get it. Manners. Posh, extra posh, innit?"
"This is not a joke, Cassandra. This is serious. I am owed favors and I owe just as many favors to many, many of Caine's Childer."
Cassandra queries, "Any reason why you're doing it all now? Why not only do a part of it and let the others simmer so when shit really gets hot, you can cash 'em in?"
Gregory's dead eyes flit to the side for a moment. He looks back at her and shakes his head, "I'm afraid that it is not that simple. I have my reasoning. And I surely do not owe you an explanation. But I will say this: I want to put all of my affairs in order."
He ominously tells her, "There's a reason why I have kept you away from most of it for the past 2 or so decades. Many reasons...but now is hardly the time for such things."
Cassandra's curiosity is piqued, "So, you're finally going to tell me what you've been up to, yeah? All those sudden trips to fancy locales and--"
"I've been doing it long before you became my Ghoul, Cassandra. But I strongly believe that now is the time to finally cash in on all of these favors. And it's time for you to represent me. It's time for you to put your wits, whatever insights you may possess, and whatever skills you have acquired over the years to good use."
Cassandra licks her lips and stares at the paper. On it are listed the names of the Clans (and a few Bloodlines) written in block letters. A generous amount of space has been left between each entry.
Gregory tells her, "You will be free to tackle this however you deem fit. I shall explain to whom I owe favors...and who owe me. I intend to see the proverbial slate being wiped clean in a...matter of weeks."
"Weeks? Wow. OK. What kind of...err...favors do you owe these people?"
Gregory dryly answers, "I have been involved in a few dealings over the decades, I have met many a sort of person- if some can even be called people anymore. They vary from eccentric to those in whose presence I shan't want to be in. Through my art-dealing, I have done them favors and they have done me favors. That's usually how it goes in Kindred Society."
Cassandra mumbles, "That's fucking vague. OK, never mind. Sorry. I'll just...take your word for it, yeah?"
"Yes, that would be preferable. Have I ever lied to you? Misled you? Backstabbed you?"
"No, no, and no. I'd hope bloody not! Why? Should I expect any surprises when I run around carrying these errands out for you?"
Gregory flatly answers, "You should be ready for anything. It is simply the nature of things, Cassandra. The nature of our world is one of treachery, manipulation, uncertainty, deceit, and betrayal...and violence."
Cassandra notes, "It's a pretty dark world, yeah. But I try me best to see the good in it, too."
"That's always been something admirable about you, Cassandra. What's that word they throw around? Oh, yes, bubbliness. Hm, yes, that. But be wary, all the same. Some of these people will not hesitate--" the words get caught in his mouth.
The Toreador goes silent for a moment. His eyes glaze over, and he seems to be lost in thought. The Domitor dreamily stares at something over his Ghoul's shoulder.
Cassandra has gotten used to this. She follows his gaze and spots a few falling leaves in the far distance.
Gregory shakes his head and comes back to reality. He clears his throat and blinks, "Oh. Where was I? Never mind. And another thing."
"Yeah?"
"Try not to sound so bloody Northern all the time. Some of the people whom you will find yourself interacting with are rather...peculiar, to say the least. Mildly put--"
Cassandra claps back, "I'll sound as bloody Northern as I damn well like! It's me accent, it's me way of--of speaking and expressing meself! How the fuck am I supposed to change that, eh? I'm not gonna start talking like a posh wanker, Greg!"
She does an imitation of someone who's posh. She elaborately lifts her chin and pouts haughtily, "Oh, look at me. Oh, darling butler, the poors are sooooooo quaint. Look at their council houses and their clothes. Is that a chav I spot in the distance! Oh, we simply must have a picture of it!"
Gregory sighs, "Yes, yes, fine. OK. I get the point loud and clear. You've made your argument. Fine. Be as Northern as you like. Just don't let it get in the way of your duties. It was stupid of me to--to even bring that up."
Cassandra nods, "Hmph, yeah. No shite. I am who I am. And is the other thing going to be an issue? Y'know, the--"
"I don't know. But if I were you, I wouldn't go around telling people. I--I know that times are different now. Everything's more open, but a lot of our kind simply haven't gotten with the modern era just yet. You know how some of us can be."
She ruffles her hair slightly and fixes her gaze to the ground, "Yeah, yeah. I know. So, I 'ave to keep me mouth shut about it, yeah? But it's a--a core part of who I am, Greg. I can't just shut that aspect- that part- of me off, y'know?"
Gregory clasps his hands together and nods, "I know. And, for what it's worth, I am terribly sorry about any...prejudices you may have to encounter in regard to that."
Cassandra winces, "Apologizing in advance is hardly confidence-boosting."
Regardless, she waves her hand and clicks her tongue, "Ah, it's fine. I'll find a way to...manage it. It's not like I'm gonna go around shouting it from the rooftops, innit?"
Gregory's smile doesn't quite reach his eyes, "Yes, yes, of course. But you should be free to be who you are. I've personally never had any problems with it. I've always seen it as something that makes you...well, you."
Cassandra lips curl into a smile, "Aww, thanks, Greg! It's--it's nice to hear that coming from you, yeah. And also, a bit reassuring."
"Just be careful out there. You have a deep and intimate knowledge of our kind. Utilize it to the max. This is something that you'll have to do on your own. Figure things out, improvise where you must. I shan't be accompanying you on any of these...journeys."
"Journeys? Cor blimey? Where are you sending me off to? Ibiza?"
"Nothing as fun or--or exotic as that, I'm afraid. Everything is well within the confines of the United Kingdom of Great Britain, Scotland, Ireland...and Wales, for some reason. Reasons that shall soon become apparent to you."
"Alright. Well, this is gonna be a bloody long night, innit? You sure you don't want--"
Gregory holds his hand out, "I will hunt later. This simply has to be done. The Hunger can wait."
Cassandra notes, "It's never a good idea to deny the Beast for that long."
"It can wait. This is simply of the utmost importance to me."
Cassandra pointedly asks, "Why now? Why all of a sudden? Is this some sort of eccentric Toreador thing that I'm not aware of? Is it something else? Some kind of...err...mid-Unlife crisis?"
Gregory's dead eyes narrow, "Eccentric? Mid-Unlife crisis? Far from it. Needless to say, I do not owe you an explanation as to why I'm doing this now. It is my prerogative to do so. As your Domitor, I am asking you- my Ghoul- to simply carry out these tasks on my behalf. Nothing more, nothing less."
Cassandra's eyebrows shoot up, "Wow. OK. S--sure. So, you either owe favors to- or are owed favors from- each and every single Clan? And a few Bloodlines, eh?"
"Yes." A very plain and emotionless answer.
"That's...uhm...oh, I'm pretty stoked about this, Greg. Any suggestions on how to get started? How to tackle this?" Cassandra taps the piece of paper on her thigh.
Gregory reaches out and grabs it from her. He takes a pen from the nearby glass table, clicks it, and starts making some edits. After 20 seconds or so, he hands it back to Cassandra.
"Cor blimey! I always knew that you were busy, but never THIS busy! And that writing was...fast, like, blazing fast, yeah."
Gregory dully says, "I have been in and around the art world as a dealer. And in those few short years, I have come across all sorts. And it is highly likely that you will cross paths with those same...people. Be on your guard, Cassandra. Watch what they say, how they say it, and always remember your learning."
She nods at him, a tight and uneasy look slowly forming on her otherwise soft features, "Yeah, yeah, OK. You're scaring me a bit, Greg. What's all of this really about?"
Gregory vacantly stares off into the distance. He comes back to reality and dryly explains, "It is simply the way things have to be. I...this needs to be done and there is nobody else in this world I trust enough to accomplish these tasks for me. I hope that I haven't made a mistake by choosing you."
The Ghoul clicks her tongue, "No need to be so condescending! Of bloody course, I'll do it! And I won't let you down!"
"Hmm. We have much to discuss. For now, I need some time to...mull some things over. You're free to go and do whatever it is that you usually do for the next few...hours, Cassandra. When you return, we shall talk about the specifics."
"Sure thing."
Gregory looks right into her hazel eyes and comments, "There's a reason why I'm not coming with you. As such, I'm authorizing you to act on my behalf and represent me. Furthermore, try not to call me up so bloody much. It's not safe to be using the phones, anyway, right?"
Cassandra nods her head up and down, "Mm-hmm. Not that safe, yeah. How about if I tackled it in alphabetical order, eh? Or sort of alphabetical order?"
The Toreador Domitor shrugs indifferently, "It's your choice. I shall simply give you some parting advice and leave you, largely, to your own devices. Know that your words and deeds reflect on me. And there are people- especially in this Domain- who are always watching, waiting for the slightest of slip-ups before they come swooping in."
Gregory's tone gets far more ominous, "And when they come for you, they won't come alone. Please, be on your best behavior. Be discreet, cautious, forge alliances wisely, know when to back away from a situation, and...well, you already know what to do. Keeping you insulated from the broader happenings of Kindred Society was a...choice on my part."
"A bad one or a good one?"
"I don't quite have a satisfactory answer to that, I'm afraid. Don't worry about work; I'll call them and speak with them later. You are to take your vacation now. This may very well drag on for a while."
Cassandra sighs and shakes her head, "Oh, a long one, innit? Ah, fine. I was hoping to use me vacation time to actually, y'know, go on a nice holiday. Sunny Spain or--or France or Italy."
Gregory reassures her, "Worry not, everything is well within the confines of...this country and its most immediate neighbors."
Cassandra softly chuckles, "You still dislike Wales, eh? Whatever happened there, anyway?"
"You might find out. Or you won't. Please be careful, Cassandra. This is not a game. This is not a holiday or a--a thrilling vacation filled with wonder and excitement at every turn, this is serious business. I myself am going to be very busy the next few nights. You have an advantage, however."
The Ghoul smiles widely, "I still get to go out in the sun, eat ice cream, do all that fun stuff. Poor you, hmm?"
"Yes. Woe is me. On your travels, you will come across a myriad of...individuals. Each with their own agendas, desires, and goals. Your journeys will take you to places and people unexpected. Don't take anything at face value and be ready to come across things that could very well challenge your view of the world."
"Wow. Uhm...sure. Yeah. I'm absolutely ready. It's not gonna be violent or--or really, REALLY dangerous, though? Or is it?" Cassandra searches his blank gaze for any sort of answer. None are immediately apparent.
Instead, he simply tells her, "I will give you a bit more of my Vitae if you're worried. Other than that, expect the unexpected. Be ready to adapt- rapidly, if need be- to situations that change at the drop of a hat. I can't promise a smooth and easy ride- it would be foolish and irresponsible of me to do so."
Cassandra nods slowly, "Yeah, no shit. Just don't cast me into a fucking mess without a bit of--of prep. Maybe throw in a pep talk, too. Eh? What about money, supplies, that sort of thing?"
"As your Domitor, it is my responsibility to take care of such things. I'll also transfer some money into your account so that you can make purchases as you see fit. Worry not: you'll always have enough for your needs. Maybe the occasional want, but I leave that sort of thing to you."
The Ghoul gives him a thumbs-up, "Thanks, Greg! Y'know, you don't 'ave to pump a lot of dosh into me account; I got...enough. What about IDs and all that other fun stuff?"
Gregory equably answers, "Again, I shall take care of it. I've been around long enough to know the right people in our circles who fancy themselves experts on matters pertaining to...forged documents." Mild disapproval laces his words.
Cassandra smirks, "Ooh, sounds proper shady! But we should really cover our tracks and...err...watch our backs, yeah? I'd hate for the baddies in the Second Inquisition to find us out."
"Hm. Yes. As a Ghoul, even you are responsible for ensuring that the Masquerade- the most important of all our Traditions- is upheld. Do not take it lightly, Cassandra. You and I are in this together."
"Heh. In more ways than 1. You look a lot paler than usual. Are you sure you don't need me help to--"
Gregory's palm faces her. He shakes his head, "I can handle it myself, Cassandra. I got on fine long before you came along. But...you have been good to me. As have I, I--I hope."
Cassandra smiles warmly, "You 'ave. Can I take the Lexus? Do you need me to take you somewhere? Are we expecting any guests this week?"
"No. And not that I'm aware of. Cassandra, the Lexus is yours- it's always been yours. I...don't like driving vehicles of any kind. None whatsoever."
She rises from her seat and pats him on the shoulder. She briefly flashes him a look of concern, "Are you doing alright? Y--you'll tell me if something's wrong with you, yeah?"
"Hmm. Enjoy your drive."
Cassandra chuckles, "I'm just going to the shops, Greg. I'm a bit low on some...err...necessities. I'll pay with me own cash, don't worry, OK?"
Gregory nods and waves her off, "Yes, yes. Be careful out there. And drive safely. You're going to need to be in peak physical condition for the road ahead."
Cassandra chuckles, "It's not the bloody Olympics, is it?"
Gregory responds, "Don't take these things so lightly, Cassandra. I have already told you what will happen if you let your guard down- many, many times. I shall also take my leave. When you return, we have much to discuss and plan for. We also need to make sure that you have everything that you need for this."
The Ghoul pats the side of her jeans and slowly exits her Domitor's fancy Haven. She stops and takes a wee look at some of the abstract art pieces he has adorning the walls of his living room.
"Very pretty!"
"Hmm. Goodbye, Cassandra. I shall be out, hunting. Are those hikers still near that creek?"
While still staring at the paintings, she nods, "Mm-hmm. A few lads and a single lass. Definitely uni students on a wee adventure or some sort of holiday."
"They will have to do."
"Don't kill them, yeah?"
"Of course. I may be a damned monster condemned to drink blood and hide away from the sun for all eternity, but I still have my Humanity, Cassandra. Some shreds of it left to--to cling to."
She murmurs, "I hope that it's a lot more than a few shreds, Greg. I--I don't like disposing of dead bodies. It's fucking terrible and--and it's bloody traumatic! Don't let 'em die, alright?"
The Toreador Domitor puts on a faded jacket, "I shall strive to do my best." Without saying another word, he slips into a pair of running shoes and starts to walk towards the door.
Cassandra steps outside and takes a moment to appreciate her surroundings on this beautiful autumn evening. Despite the gray gloominess that never quite seems to go away, she finds it to be quite lovely. She always does.
She inhales a lungful of fresh air and smoothly lets it out through her nostrils. A quick check of her pocket reveals that she still has her keys. She decides to stop by her cottage before heading out into the city proper.
Cassandra takes slow and deliberate steps, making sure that she does step on a few of the crunchy fallen leaves that blanket the ground. She lightly giggles, skips, and runs around, kicking some up into the air and watching them flutter to the ground.
Once back in the cozy place that she has called home for the past few years, she grabs her phone. It's a Blackberry device with a QWERTY keyboard; she's been meaning to get an Android smartphone, but she's not quite ready to let go of something that's she's deeply familiar with.
The Ghoul's phone begins to vibrate. Her ringtone is a pop song of some kind. Her brows knit together when she spots an unknown number, but she answers it anyway.
"Yeah? Who's this?"
A man's voice is on the other end of the line, sounding slightly excited and breathless. He sounds young, "Hey, it--it's me. Yeah. Me. D--don't say me full name. They're listening, right? Listen, I just heard about your new job. Congratulations."
Cassandra thinks to herself, "Horace? Horace Grimley? But--?"
Horace chuckles and admonishes her, "Yeah, congratulations on the new job. Listen, watch it out there, love."
"But how do you--"
He hangs up without saying a word. Cassandra stares at her phone in shock, disbelief, and worry. The last time she saw him, he was still a Ghoul in the service of a Malkavian named Damien. That was a decade ago. Now, he somehow has...insight to phone her- just as soon as Greg wrapped up with her- and congratulate her on...getting a new job?
Cassandra wonders to herself, "Is Horace a Malkavian now? But how did he know about--? Ah, shite, I'll have to make time to see him sometime this week."
Yes. He is. His Embrace was quite recent, actually. And the visions that he's been getting have been on the overwhelming side of things. She'll find out soon enough.
Like Cassandra, Horace was a Vassal Ghoul.
Anyways, the Lexus that Cassandra has came along with the house and the nearby cottage that she now resides in. Long story. The car in question has already done a lot of kilometers over the years. Despite being secondhand, it's a sleek and very modern-looking thing.
Gregory gave it to her since he has never been much of a vehicle man. Not even bicycles! In his time, he used to walk to his place of work (since it was so bloody close by). He also liked taking the Tube when he was still back in London. And maybe the occasional ride on the double-decker buses.
The Ghoul slides into the driver's seat, starts the car up, and begins to make her way into the city. The engines of the Lexus purr like kittens. She makes sure to stick to the speed limit.
For a car that came thrown in with a cheap (by any and all standards) house and a nearby cottage, it's quite impressive all things considered.
Cassandra lets her lovely hair hand loose, the wind blowing it around. She elects to keep the radio turned off, instead taking in the sounds of the cityscape that she cruises through.
She softly smiles to herself, feeling pretty damn good about the new "job" that she has, assuming one could even call this set of complex tasks a proper job.
The city of Gloughchester has a population of about 600,000 souls, give or take. It's a decent-sized place with some urban areas and some rural ones, too. Pretty average as far as British metropolises go, really.
For Greg and Cass, the move to this Domain from London was done for a lot of reasons. But perhaps the most important was the fact that it's a relatively quiet and low-key Domain where they could both live, go about what matters the most, and function in the 21st century.
Cass slows down when she passes by the Jobs Center. The line has been rather large as of late. People of all ages, genders, ethnicities, and backgrounds wait for their chance at some form of gainful employment. A sympathetic look flashes across her features as she drives by.
"That bad? Shit! It wasn't always like this, though. I hope that they find something. So sad for them, yeah."
The Jobs Center building itself is a fairly average-looking structure. Limestone walls, 2 stories tall, with the upper floor being slightly smaller than the lower one. Large swathes of youth (and a few older people) congregate outside the building.
Many of them hold cups of coffee, water, or soda in their hands. They talk in very hush-hush voices, some of them casting nervous glances at the small group of suited people that are posted near the front entrance.
Everyone sort of stops and freezes in silence when a police car rolls up, parking not too far away from the building itself. The coppers inside get out, don their caps, and start chatting with the suited people.
Cassandra feels somewhat uneasy when she notices the suited people placing an envelope of some kind in the hands of 1 of the coppers. She immediately slams her foot on the gas pedal and speeds up a bit.
She nervously bites her lower lip. Her heart rate jumps a few ticks as she begins to turn down a street that leads to the ASDA supermarket. She finds a spot in the vast parking lot. A slight downpour begins to fall from the heavens.
"Ah, never change, England. Never change."
Cassandra grabs her umbrella and heads into the supermarket proper. It is disgustingly bright, shiny, and sparkling clean. It's also quite crowded. People are doing their usual weekly shopping. The employees are stocking shelves, moving things about, checking inventory, etc.
The Ghoul grabs a green-and-white basket and starts walking about the gaffe. She pulls out her Blackberry and scans the list that her (somewhat stubby) fingers had typed out earlier in the morning. Some of the stuff here is a tad pricier, so she reckons that the pound shop closer to her cottage would be better for those things.
For the other groceries, she'll just buy them here. If there's something she doesn't want to drive all the way out there for, she can just stop by the local pound shop. But they've had a nasty mold infestation as of late. Before that, it was asbestos.
A lot of buildings in the UK are simply not up to code, safe, or modernized in any way. And some of the buildings in Gloughchester are quite old and are starting to show their wear and tear in various ways.
Cassandra grabs some items off the shelves, taking her time to walk through the many aisles in this giant store. She smiles and nods politely to those who pass her by, as is her way. A few of the posher-looking folk give her funny looks, but she doesn't pay them much mind.
The Ghoul of Gregory is about to cast into a world that she only knows of from vast theoretical learning. This job- if one could even call it that- would take her to places she's never been to, see things she's never seen before, and meet people whom she's never met before.
A world of darkness awaits Cassandra.
Sometime later...
Gregory is back in the comfort of his Haven, the eternally ravenous Hunger of his Beast having been sated...for the time being at least. It can never be truly satisfied, only kept at bay.
He sits back with a dull expression taking up residence on his pale face. The thoughts swirl around in his head like whiskey sloshing around in a glass. His fingers tap against the armrests of the plush chair. From the corner of his eye, he thinks he spots...it.
Again. It has been tormenting him for quite a while now. The Toreador hasn't told a single soul about it. Not even his Ghoul knows- she can't, and it's likely that she won't. Ever.
"Maybe it's better that way," Greg says to himself while rapidly blinking his eyes, the vision (or probable lack thereof) having disappeared as quickly as it came. And there is soon a flicker of motion from the other side of the room.
Gregory's cold fingers dig into the arms of the chair, his nails almost piercing through the soft material. He freezes in unease and becomes as stiff as a statue. He watches the flickering figure. His eyes track something in the darkness as it moves and twitches sporadically, randomly, and in unpredictable patterns.
It's impossible to tell where this figure begins and ends. What even is it?
The ringing of the doorbell snaps the troubled Toreador out of his mini night-terror. He rises from his seat and slowly trudges to the front door, the motion sensors kicking on and bathing him in a bright light.
A decidedly middle-aged man with fair skin stands on the other side of the glass door. He has silver hair and a joyful, easy-going expression. His brown eyes twinkle in the dim light. Gregory opens the door, a small smile barely gracing his lips.
The other man loudly greets, "Gregory, how the hell are you doing, old boy!"
Gregory greets him back, "Alistair...all--all's well. I'm fine. How do you do?"
Like Gregory, Alistair is of the Blood. But of a vastly different Generation, lineage, and Sire (obviously).
Alistair is dressed in a simple black suit with a patterned gray tie. He is shorter than his fellow Aesthete and is of average weight. A thin smile plays at his lips as his eyes rove over the other man's features.
He finally remarks, "You look well, old boy. Finished eating?"
Gregory nods, "Hm. Yes. The nearby trespassers proved...suitable for my needs. After all, the nearby land is also...my property." Those last 2 words are something that a man like him- even after all these years- isn't quite used to uttering. Or even thinking about.
The older man claps him on the back and enters, "If I knew how cheap this entire place was going for, I'd have paid twice of what you paid! Hahaha! Ah, but what is done is done and cannot be undone."
"Come inside. Have a seat."
Alistair removes his bulky coat and hangs it on the nearby rack. He sets his hat aside as well. From there, he walks over to the plush chair and eases into it. Gregory returns to his own chair. His visitor takes a moment to appreciate the artwork that his fellow Toreador has on display.
"Abstract. Wonderful taste."
Gregory dryly states, "It came with the house. I only did the barest of renovations and additions. Nothing too serious, really."
"Oh, I know. I know. I shan't be wasting too much of your time. My visit tonight has purpose."
"Hmm."
"Yes. You withdrew your application. Such sudden and abrupt decisions! It's not the way we do things, old boy. You were almost in. Why? The Gloughchester Bridge Party would've been quite fun. Everyone would've had a smashing good time!"
Gregory doesn't look into his friend's eyes, "My decisions are my own. I am prioritizing something else at the moment. As such, I don't believe that I'd be able to dedicate the time and commitment that the Gloughchester Six would require of me."
"Oh. Oh, please. It's the Gloughchester Bridge Party. 'Gloughchester Six' has always been a misnomer to throw the others off the scent as it were. Our Coterie, it goes without saying, is far more than 6 members."
Gregory huffs a sigh, "Maybe a Coterie consisting solely of Toreadors wouldn't do wonders for me."
Alistair side-eyes his host, "Is that why you withdrew your application at the last possible second? It would've been the perfect opportunity for you to show your support and get in touch with some old friends from London, make some new connections with those of the Blood, and enjoy yourself, too! All while helping out a few good causes."
"My decision is final, Alistair. Other matters have come up and they demand my utmost attention and commitment. Please try to understand."
Alistair shrugs, "It's going to be hard explaining it to them, but I'm sure they'll come around. Might I inquire as to the nature of these other matters that have suddenly caught your eye, old boy? I know how...intriguing us Toreadors can get. Heh."
Cassandra's Domitor vaguely responds, "I intend to get my affairs in order."
"..."
Gregory flatly goes on, "I don't feel comfortable sharing any more than that. Not even with you- someone I consider a--a close friend. But I can tell you this: my Ghoul shall be doing it all- everything- on my behalf."
Alistair raises a thin brow, "Cassandra? Is she truly ready for this sort of thing?"
"She better be. There's still much for us to discuss. Needless to say, I have authorized her to act, speak, and negotiate on my behalf. And with regards to the Gloughchester Bridge Party, you need not concern yourself with me anymore. I'm out. And no, I won't be reapplying."
Alistair chuckles, "Even if you wanted to, you'd have to wait for a full century before they'd even consider your application. Unless, of course, they've changed their minds, but I somehow doubt that. It's a very exclusive Coterie. Your sudden and abrupt exit is...unusual, old boy. It's raised its fair share of eyebrows and given them reason enough to suspect something's amiss."
Gregory waves a hand dismissively, "That's no longer my problem."
Alistair leans forward and clasps his icy hands together, "Is there anything else that you wish to talk about, Gregory? About what you're sending Cassandra on, maybe? These aren't simply errands. And it's not something that Domitors usually send their Ghouls on, either."
Gregory acknowledges that, "I know. But I see no other choice. She's probably had it easy for these past 2 decades in which she has been serving me. But I wouldn't necessarily say that in front of her, though."
Alistair's tone turns concerned, "Are you not well, old boy? Still concerned about what happened back in London, eh? I can imagine how hard it must've been for you. Especially the aftermath."
"No. It--it's not that. I've moved on, shed my obligatory tears for those who perished. And no, that's not it."
"Then what is it??"
Gregory stares at the ground, "It's my past. Cassandra is inevitably going to find certain things out- she's not stupid- and I fear that the truth may cause her to lose trust in me."
"Ah. Your dealings in art...be it on a canvas or otherwise. Your Ghoul is not privy to many of your earlier escapades, is she?" Alistair knowingly taps the side of his nose and smirks.
"There is a myriad of reasons why I have kept her away from the excesses of Kindred Society. Her safety and welfare are, in its own way, a reflection of my own. But she deserves to know about it all- the good and the bad- before I send her into the belly of the beast."
Alistair shrugs a shoulder, "I suppose she does. Far be it from to prod too deeply, but she is adequately Bound to you via the Blood, yes? Loose lips, old boy, sink ships. And we'd all hate for anything bad to happen to poor Cassandra, hmm? She's been serving you quite well and is quite a lovely lass."
Gregory nods slowly, "She knows what's at stake here."
Alistair waggles a finger at his fellow Toreador and cheekily reminds him, "Unlife on nights like these is more like an extended warranty. It can easily be revoked- as can the very right of our kind to exist- for a wide assortment of reasons. We needn't go over all of those reasons, do we?"
"I know. No, we--we need not."
"What about the ugly?"
Gregory's brows knit together, "Hmm?"
Alistair sighs a little, "You mentioned her needing to know the good and the bad. What of the ugly? The unspeakable, unmentionable, and unsavory? Inversely, what of the truly beautiful things that are still locked away somewhere in this vast world of ours? The things that are so beautiful that they would make her want to cry and laugh and smile and cheer and weep and sing all at once?"
"I'll get to it. Piece by piece. I shan't be dumping everything on her head in a single night. That would just overwhelm her."
"Oh, no doubt about it, old boy."
Gregory raises his chin and changes the subject, "How is work?"
Alistair chuckles, "NHS admin doesn't quite have the same ring to it as esteemed surgeon. But, like you, I made my decision to withdraw myself from the practice of my profession of medicine. Y--you know why..." he trails off, his lower lip quivering in the slightest.
"It's noble of you. Lesser beings would've stayed on and fed that monster of theirs until it became a liability. Then they would've been forced out anyways."
"V--very true. Very true. I still don't know why, Gregory. Why was I so disturbingly fascinated with innards and insides when performing surgeries. I had to step down...for my own good. I'm only grateful that nobody was seriously harmed when I was in the operating theaters. Dead hands, after all, tended to be the steadiest of them all."
The former doctor goes on, "But my doctoring times are behind me, old boy. I--I had to step down, for everyone's good. I still see a few Ghouls, but in a private capacity. Mind if I pop by Cassandra's cottage and check her vitals before she heads out? If she's going to be carrying out the will of her Domitor, then she's going to need to be in tip-top shape. It's only the proper thing to do."
"Be my guest."
And with that, Alistair rises from his seat. He and Gregory walk to the front door, the 2 Aesthetes making small talk on the way.
"Keep in touch, eh? And what do I tell our friends at the Gloughchester Bridge Party, hmm? For what it's worth, those games of bridge that we had were rather fun."
Gregory gives him a weak smile, "Tell them that I wish them all the best of luck. They'll manage without me, I'm sure of it. If there's some kind of a fee to be paid--"
Alistair waves his hand, "Oh, no, no. Nothing like that. Your decision is final and deeply personal. Does that sound succinct enough?"
"Yes. Goodnight, Alistair."
Alistair puts his coat and hat back on. He waves farewell and heads off. Gregory shuts the door and stands in contemplative silence for a minute or 2.
With a pep in his step, Alistair whistles a jolly ol' tune and walks along the path to Cassandra's cozy little cottage.
The Gloughchester Bridge Party is an exclusively exclusive, Toreador-only, Plumaire-Type Coterie. Birds of a feather flock together, and social coteries like Plumaires ("feathered ones") exemplify this adage.
They're united by ties of social prominence or simple common enthusiasms, in this case, very intense games of bridge. They share similar tastes in music, fashion, and what have you.
(It's also a great place to socialize and network with those of the Rose).
Gregory was almost so close to having an "in" with this Coterie. Why did he withdraw?
Why, indeed?
Regardless, the existing members of the Gloughchester Six won't take such a perceived slight lightly. Some of these creatures are truly petty and are disturbingly capable of harboring grudges for decades on end. And their reach extends far beyond the confines of Gloughchester.
Cassandra's Domitor has made his decision, anyway. His steps are heavy and slow as he trudges upstairs, heading straight for the bedroom.
Alistair's knuckles rap on Cassandra's door. He calls out, "Hello, Cassandra, it's Alistair. I'm just popping by for a quick hello. Are you free to speak right now?"
"Just a moment!" she hastily calls out, some clattering and giggling noises following soon thereafter.
After more than a moment, Cassandra opens the door, a bright smile on her pretty face. Her cheeks look a tad flushed, her hair is messy, and her clothing is askew. Her chest rises and falls with every breath.
Alistair remarks, "Ah, Cassandra, you are quite the sight for sore eyes. Might I come in?"
She steps aside and ushers him in. Alistair takes a look around her cozy abode. He nods approvingly, saying, "This place is quite nice. I like what you've done with the interiors. You've really done a smashing job cleaning the place up and keeping it well-maintained."
A woman with short black hair silently pecks Cassandra on the cheek, hugs her awkwardly, and shuffles off. She squeezes past Alistair and puts a ring back on her finger. The Toreador refrains from commenting.
Cassandra's eyes follow the other woman as she exits her cottage. Her hazel eyes linger on the fading image of the woman's silhouette. Alistair still doesn't say anything.
He breaks the silence by asking, "I hope that I wasn't interrupting anything important?"
Cassandra shakes her head and smiles, "Oh, no, no. Not at all. So, what can I do for you? You spoke to Greg, yeah?"
Alistair settles into her warm couch and crosses his legs. Cassandra sits at the other end, her legs lazily resting across the length of the sofa.
The former surgeon answers, "Yes, I did. Did you know that he withdrew his application from the Gloughchester Bridge Party??"
"I did, yeah. He told me not to get too worked up over it. It's just a bunch of bridge games and chatting with posh types, innit?"
"That sounds a bit reductive, but I suppose that's the gist of it. I'm only commissioned by them to vet potential entrants into their little circle. Your Domitor was millimeters away; they were impressed with his...illustrious past record, to put it mildly. The withdrawal was highly unexpected. It's not like him, Cassandra."
Cassandra sucks some air in between her teeth, "I don't know what's gotten into him. He's been acting proper strange these past few weeks. Barely says a word, only does the bare minimum. He might've let it slip that he's not day-sleeping too well, either."
Alistair puts his hand on his mouth, "Oh, my. Now, THAT is cause for concern. Do you have any idea what- or who- could've possibly caused it?"
"He clams up when I try to ask him about it."
"Is he...mistreating you in any way?"
"Just being a bit vague, that's all. In the 2 decades I've been with him, I've never seen him like this before. Sure, he was always rather distant and--and withdrawn. But this is something else entirely. I reckon that something's not right with him. But I don't quite know why."
Alistair gives her a thin, polite smile, "Perhaps a long walk throughout the area would do him some good. He got himself- and you- the deal of the century with this entire property."
Cassandra admits, "It's a beautiful place, yeah. We're both really thankful that we're living here. I--I don't take such things for granted. Renting flats in London was a right pain in the arse. I've seen me fair share of moldy apartments. So, having this little cottage- even if it is in a remote, out-of-the-way place- is a bloody dream for someone like me."
"I can imagine. Enjoy it. Gregory has informed me about certain tasks that you are to carry out on his behalf. Well, knowing him, it's going to involve its fair share of physical demands, not merely mental ones. It's not going to be easy, Cassandra."
"Uh-huh. OK. And?"
"When was your last checkup? Do you have a doctor whom you regularly see. Yes, yes, I know, you're a Ghoul and all that. But that's no excuse to skimp on one's health. If you were my patient, I'd insist that you schedule a proper medical examination. I know many establishments that are...Kindred-friendly, so to speak."
"I'm not ill, but I--I understand what you're getting at."
"I could ask you a few questions, if that's alright with you?"
Cass shrugs, "Ask away."
The doctor in him asks the lass a few rudimentary, standard, and non-invasive questions. She gives truthful, honest answers and does her best to recall certain bits and bobs of information.
From there, the conversation drifts to other matters.
Cassandra brings up what she saw earlier in the evening, "I think I saw a bunch of coppers taking a bribe near the Jobs Center."
Alistair isn't too surprised by this, "Hm. Yes. Let's just say that coppers tend to get grumpy when they are denied their whack. And as a doctor, I've personally seen what the wrath of an officer of the law looks like. It's not a pretty sight, Cassandra. I've performed intensive, life-saving surgeries on more battered spouses of police officers than I care to recall."
The former doctor's voice hardens, "I'd be careful around the local police force in this Domain. And not just the 'bent' ones, mind you."
He taps the side of his nose in a knowing gesture, "There's more to them than meets the eye."
"Cor blimey. Will I have to cross paths with such tossers when I'm out and about doing Greg's stuff??"
Alistair leans forward, a glint in his eyes, "I believe that you will, yes. My advice is to always have a good, solid, plausible explanation at the ready. And it doesn't hurt to have your barrister on speed dial. You're being thrust into a world that you only have a theoretical understanding of. No offense, but the actuality is often quite different."
Cassandra chuckles uneasily, "Oh, I--I know. I know. I'm prepared, yeah. It's a big change and I've heard all the horror stories about things going pear-shaped out there. But I don't wanna let Greg down. He's counting on me to help him get his affairs in order. So, that's what I'm gonna do."
Alistair rises from his seat and exclaims, "That's the spirit! I shall be taking my leave now. Do let Gregory know that I was here. And try to get through to him. I'm worried about him. We all are."
Cassandra sighs softly, "I'm concerned about him, too. But he has to tell me what's got him so bothered. I'm not a mind-reader. I don't 'ave fancy Auspex and shite."
"That is true. Goodnight, my dear. And may the coming nights be...kind to you."
Cassandra walks him to the door and sees him out, "Likewise. Cheers!" She eagerly waves goodbye and closes the door.
She blows a sharp raspberry as the man's footsteps slowly fade into the distance. She's still standing at the front door, a small smile lingering on her face. Cass throws herself back on the couch and grabs her cordless landline. She dials a number and waits.
The phone rings once, then twice. The woman from earlier answers with a chirpy, "Hello!"
"Amy...hiya."
"Hello, yourself."
"You still driving? Should I--"
"Hands-free! That Bluetooth you got for me last Christmas was the bee's knees. Thanks again. I'm loving it!"
"Oh, good. Y--you free to talk? I just want to hear your voice. Sorry about that...sudden interruption."
"Eh, no worries. I didn't mean to get in the way. Is it work?"
Amy is in her 30s. She has short black hair, a freckled face, and a thin frame. She's a few inches taller than Cass. She speaks with a slight lisp.
Cassandra answers, "Something like that, yeah. I was hoping that we'd...be able to, y'know...spend the night together."
"Oooh, really now? A right cheeky minx, you are! What if I made it up to you sometime this week, hmm??"
Cassandra smiles softly, "I'd like that very much. What did you have in mind?"
Amy shifts gears and takes a smooth turn, "Well, kebabs. Takeaway, that sort of thing. Nothing too fancy, right? I know you too well to know that you don't like anything too over-the-top, eh?"
"Yeah, that sounds nice. Y--you don't think that you could turn back and--and spend the night here, right now, do you?"
Amy looks at the ring on her finger and grimaces slightly, "I don't want her to get any more suspicious than she already is. Please, don't put me in a tight spot. Who was that silver-haired man who popped by, anyway?"
"A--a friend of sorts."
Amy chuckles, "You have all sorts of interesting friends, don't you?"
Cassandra responds, "Well, you're a friend, too. I'd hate to lose a good one."
Amy rolls her eyes, "I think I'm far more than that."
"Yes, yes. But you--we 'ave to be careful!"
Amy clicks her tongue, her eyes flitting to the ring on her finger once more, "I know. But that's what makes all of this a tad exciting, innit?"
Cassandra sharply exhales, "I suppose so. I just don't want anyone finding out about...us, yeah? I--I'd hate to lose--"
"No one's going to find out, sweetheart. Before you sleep it off, think about me, yeah? Because I'll be thinking of you when I'm taking my bath tonight..." Amy seductively purrs.
The Ghoul giggles, "You're such a tease! I love it! Bloody 'ell, you've made me all wet just by talking! You're not making this easy, are you?"
Amy smirks, "Nope. But that's why you like me so much, isn't it? Goodnight, sweetie. I'll text you in the morning, yeah?"
"Y--yeah. I--I lo--I mean, yes, definitely."
Amy playfully singsongs, "You needn't say it! Goodnight and sleep tight! Cheers!"
Cassandra hangs up the phone, a big smile on her face. Amy taps on her Bluetooth earpiece and adjusts her rearview mirror. She presses her foot down on the pedal and speeds off.
It's obvious what's going on here, innit?
Anyways...
Sunrise is moments away. Greg and his Ghoul have already spoken on what is to be done over the next few nights. He vaguely tells her that he'll reveal more information, in a piece-by-piece sort of manner, over the coming nights.
She has no reason to doubt him. She decides to tackle things in alphabetical order (sort of). The 1st on the list is Brujah. The Banu Haqim are in Birmingham, and the Baali isn't scheduled to arrive anytime soon.
Besides, the Brujah that Gregory owes are right here in Gloughchester.
The Toreador ensures that the shutters are closed. He also checks the thick red curtains to make sure that they're adequately blocking out the sunlight.
The man is a creature of habit, and habits are hard to break. But this is 1 habit that has no room for compromise. After checking that everything is as it should be, Gregory trudges towards his large bed and throws the covers over himself.
His mind slowly goes blank. As the sun slowly peeks over the horizon, his thoughts grow foggy. He can no longer resist the allure of the day's long, long rest. Greg shuts his eyes and prepares to be pulled into the depths of the day-sleep.
His Ghoul, Cassandra, is already sound asleep. Now that she's been given her vacation, her schedule ought to be more aligned with that of her Domitor's.
Before she nodded off, she was busy putting the finishing touches on her own preparations. Her snoring is loud and deep, and she's sprawled across the length of her queen-sized bed, totally naked.
Her cellphone is by her pillow, and a few notifications light up the screen and cause it to vibrate a few times. Just some rather suggestive texts from Amy, nothing more.
Fear washes over Gregory. His body slowly goes all sluggish. He knows that he's not alone, even if his senses aren't telling him otherwise. He shuts his eyes and hopes that he'll be cast into the throes of the day-sleep, and the feeling will go away.
Somewhere in the middle of the day, his eyes snap open. Bloody sweat drips down his forehead, and his breathing is heavy. He's completely paralyzed on his bed; a look of shock is etched on his pale features.
That's when he sees...it. While perched at an odd angle, it stares down on him. There's a faint, distant humming noise. Thrumming and snarling soon follow.
Gregory gulps, his fingers barely twitching. He helplessly stares up at the figure, his body refusing to move an inch. His lips quaver in the slightest.
The creature of the Blood- the Aapilu- stares at Gregory with its 5 eyes.
5 eyes that Gregory himself is very familiar with. 1 pair belonged to his late wife; the middle eye is that of his late daughter; the last pair of eyes...are his own.
The Aapilu is the size of a full-grown humanoid. It has gray, ashen skin that almost shimmer like scales when viewed from certain angles. It's sweating Blood, and profusely. The Aapilu's face is mouthless, with the 5 eyes taking up most of its facial real estate.
Its 6 long, skinny fingers have claws in lieu of nails. Dark, spiky, and leaking some kind of black substance. The creature's 2 legs are as sturdy as tree trunks.
Its 5 eyes burrow into Gregory's very being. He lets out a weak, shaky gasp. The creature's eyes are unblinking, and the longer he stares into those eyes, the deeper they seem to sink into his very essence.
Words fail to come out of his mouth. He tries to speak, but no words are uttered.
The Aapilu makes a few dreadful clicking noises and moves closer. Its head tilts to the side and it leans forward. Its face is now mere millimeters away from Gregory's own.
It stares, and the Toreador is helpless but to stare back.
Aapilum (singular Aapilu) are a form of "spiritual" being that metaphysically resides within the Vitae of every single vampire in the world. Whyever is Gregory's Aapilu manifesting and interacting with him, then?
And why now?
There are numerous theories and interpretations as to what exactly an Aapilu is and what they're supposed to represent.
Nevertheless, this creature of the Blood has been tormenting Cassandra's Domitor for months on end.
Gregory has always been a bit of a withdrawn and somewhat jaded man (reasons not necessarily connected to the Aapilu, of course) but his attitude and outlook has gotten somewhat worse ever since.
The Aapilu whispers all kinds of things through Gregory's Blood. Is that why he's so keen on closing everything and moving on with a "clean slate"? But to where? Is that why he's sending Cassandra in his place?
Gregory stares into the eye in the middle of the creature's face. His late daughter. A few tears of blood stream down his cheeks.
The creature makes a strange gurgling noise and retreats, its body dissolving into a dark, black smoke. A foul and acrid odor permeates throughout the man's bedroom.
Gregory's eyes soon glaze over, and he gets yanked into the day-sleep, despite the creature's lingering presence.
There are many questions, but they shall be answered in due time.
Act 2: Brujah
"The Brujah Clan are many things. Passionate (but not like us Toreador), angry, and loud. Sometimes that rage tends to boil over and lead into rather nasty and unpleasant conflicts. Their temperaments are quite volatile, and it's a rare moment when a Brujah isn't arguing or debating with someone. Nevertheless, I find them rather tolerable. Some Brujah are very well-read and knowledgeable, and that is to their credit." -From the personal diaries of Gregory | Entry: On The Brujah.
The sun rises in this part of the world. Unlike her immortal master, Cassandra can't really spend the whole bloody day in bed. While still in the nude, she pushes open a window and sighs contentedly as a gentle breeze flows into her room.
She looks at the nature surrounding her cozy little cottage and smiles widely. Despite the gray gloominess, there are some warm colors thrown about. The nearby flowers are a pretty shade of pink.
The air is crisp and clean. There are birds singing and chirping in the background. Cassandra extends her hand and the rays of the sun touch her skin. She grins, her cheeks warming up.
This is nice, she thinks to herself. At least she's able to experience the waking world, unlike the one whose Blood she is reliant on for the lite version of immortality.
She turns away from the window and readies herself for a bath. She walks to the bathroom and brushes her teeth while badly singing some or the other song. She spits out the Colgate toothpaste into the sink and wipes her mouth.
She then plugs the tub and lets the water run. It's on the warmer side, just the way she likes it. The Ghoul settles into the water with a small titter. Cass spends an awful lot of time in there, letting the warm, bubbly water soak her entire body.
She then grabs her cellphone and shoots Amy a few goofy texts, throwing in a few random emojis to boot. Cassandra giggles softly and sets her phone aside, a small smile lingering on her lips.
Cassandra went absolutely crazy when emojis first became a thing. She practically spammed them everywhere and used them all the time.
Amy's response is a series of suggestive texts and a few question marks. She still doesn't get the thought process behind the Ghoul's steady stream of random emojis.
But it's all in good fun, yeah.
After washing her hair, scrubbing her body, and soaking herself, she gets dressed and goes about her day. She idly lazes around, does a wee bit of cleaning and rearranging, and then pops out to the nearby pound shop.
This time, Cassandra doesn't take the Lexus. She decides to go by foot. She has her phone in hand. She sees an older lady walking a really pampered-looking Pomeranian. The dog looks at Cass and barks loudly.
Cassandra uneasily winces and looks down at the pavement. She quickens her pace, eager to get as far away from the barking dog as possible- she's not much of a dog or cat person. But she does have a soft spot for capybaras, despite never having physically seen any in all her life.
She reckons, "Maybe I should go to the city's zoo and check if there are any, yeah?"
As she makes her way, she notices that her cellphone is vibrating. It's Amy.
Cass smirks and brings the phone up to her ear, "Couldn't keep away, eh?"
"You know me all too well, Cassie."
"Oh, do I, now? You're in a cheeky mood today, ain't you? Heading to work?"
Amy answers, "Mm-hmm. School admin is hardly exciting, but it keeps the lights and heating on."
Cassandra inquires, "Did they fix the cracks on the walls yet? You told me about it a few weeks ago, remember?"
Amy scoffs, "Bloody bastards put it off till the next election cycle. Tossers, tch. The cracks have actually gotten bigger, if you can believe it. 1 of the kids got scared that a wall was going to come crashing down on them! I share the same apprehensions, too."
Cassandra gasps, "Cor blimey! That's scary! How's everybody else handling this mess?"
"Petitions, Facebook posts, talking to bloggers who are barely read by anyone, the usual. We're not using our own funds to patch the bloody cracks because we're already running on a bloody deficit! This isn't right."
Cassandra asks, "Is there anything that I can do from me end? Like, reach out to our local MPs--"
"Fuck that. We've tried that already. I voted for the bugger. And she even dropped around for her usual photo-ops and shit. But when push comes to shove, that lot forget all about their own bloody constituents and go gallivanting off with the party bigwigs."
Cassandra sighs, "Well, I'll see what I can do, yeah? Have you tried Twitter? You'd be surprised how--"
Amy clicks her tongue in mild annoyance, "We've exhausted every available option, sweetheart. We even 'ave a WhatsApp group. Or HAD a WhatsApp group, more like. It devolved into a petty shouting match. Sort of like a catfight. I still have the screenshots saved; some of it is a right laugh!"
Cassandra crosses the road and nods, "Yeah. Some people can be massive twats, eh? Not all people, but some people. And those are unfortunately the ones who usually have the pull, power, and influence over the rest of us."
Amy chuckles, "Speaking from experience, are you? You're not even 30, babe. Why do you sound a lot older than you look? But you do look awfully sexy."
Cassandra laughs softly, "Oh, please! I--I--it's complicated, right?"
"We're complicated. But that's what makes us so exciting and fun, yeah?"
Cassandra stops and waves at the mailman, "I guess you're right. Anyways, I'm going to the pound shop, right? You...err...you want me to get you something?"
Amy replies, "Oh, no, no, no. Please. I'm fine. Really, I'm fine. Have yourself a lovely day. Mwah! Cheers!"
Cassandra makes a kissy sound back, "Mwah! Byee! Talk later, yeah? Send me a text, preferably something spicy. You know how...hot I like it."
"Will do, dear. Will do. Ciao!" Amy hangs up.
The Ghoul smiles and enters the pound shop, her Blackberry cellphone clutched tightly in her left hand. The establishment in question is fairly modern-looking. The moldy patches on the walls, however, are not easy to look away from.
Cassandra walks down the aisles with an easygoing smile on her face. She stops at the section that is filled with all sorts of cloths. She needs a few wiping cloths for keeping her cottage clean. The ones that she currently has were brought over from London and are quite worn-out.
She tosses a few packs of various colors and continues with her shopping. Cass drags her wheelie-trolley and walks to the next aisle. The employees seem to be a lot older than your average retail worker.
She swears that she once overheard an employee talk about how their pension was insufficient for their daily living expenses. Hence their need to seek this job and work extra hard. Cassandra still shudders whenever that conversation replays in her mind.
The Ghoul still remembers that man's harsh Cockney accent and the way his eyes drooped as he spoke. She doesn't see much of him around anymore, and she's been a bit shy to go around asking about him.
Cassandra grabs a few hand-wiping cloths while she's at it. These feel soft to the touch and are only £1.50!
She hoists her wheelie-trolley onto the conveyor belt and stands in line. She glances at the various newspapers displayed on the stand beside her. She snags a copy of the Guardian while cringing at the copies of the Sun and the Daily Mail. She also nabs another copy for her Domitor.
Cassandra pays with cash, says a sincere thank-you to the freckled-face lad working the till, and exits the establishment with her purchases in a plastic bag. She takes a deep breath of fresh air and walks on her merry way.
Sometime before actual lunchtime, her stomach growls a bit. She stops and checks out her phone. There are a few texts from Amy.
Cassandra bites her lower lip and reads them. She sits on a wooden bench by the nearby park. There are a few children running around, laughing loudly.
Cassandra's fingers mash into the keys of her Blackberry cellphone and she texts Amy back. She can't help but grin at every message.
She eventually goes around to asking: "What should I get 4 lunch?"
Amy texts back: "Scampi fries, I reckon? And no, I'm not free to meet up right now. Work. And allaying fears about the cracking walls of this school."
Cassandra pouts and replies: "Maybe later this week?"
Amy huffs a sigh: "Cass, love, you can get a bit clingy sometimes..."
Cassandra's brows knit together and her expression falls a little: "You say that like it's a bad thing, Amy. We're...err...you know..."
Amy rolls her eyes: "Yes. Now, I have a call coming in. We'll meet when we meet. That's that. I don't want anyone getting suspicious. The only way this works is if we be discreet about the whole thing. Getting caught is not in either of our best interests."
Cassandra sighs and types: "I'm sorry. I just...like you so much, yeah? I enjoy being with you and all. Me making you laugh is always brings a smile to me own face, innit?"
Amy smirks: "Oh, you're sweet, Cassie. I have GOT to go now. Bye. XOXO!"
Cassandra responds with a kiss mark emoji. After that, she grabs her shopping and heads off to find some scampi fries for today's lunch. She's rather peckish, and she hopes that scampi fries will be able to satisfy that hunger.
The rest of her afternoon goes by. A light rain begins to pelt the city once the sun begins its rather unceremonious departure from the sky. Cassandra spends a few hours binging some whatever's on the telly while texting back and forth with Amy.
Amy's replies are sporadic and somewhat dry, while Cassandra's texts are a lot more enthusiastic and frequent. Cassandra has no idea as to what the telly is even showing, but the background noise is nice.
She lays her head down for a quick nap. Within seconds, she's out. After about a few minutes, her eyes pop open and she lets out a grating yawn. Her hands instinctively reach for her cellphone.
Cassandra rubs her eyes and sits up. She opens up the message app and sends Amy a few more texts. No reply is forthcoming, however. Cass frowns and decides to give it some time.
For now, she has to get ready to carry out the will of her Domitor. She freshens up in the bathroom and changes her clothing. Since Gregory is still rather vague about a great many things, she decides to wear something rather bland.
A nice off-white blouse, wide-legged pants, and very light makeup consisting of lip gloss (from the pound shop), eyeliner, and a touch of blush. Her long brown hair is tied into a bun...of sorts.
She smacks her lips a few times, chuckles at her reflection, and grabs her handbag. She heads out into the rainy night with a large umbrella and makes her way over to Gregory's Haven.
The Toreador has already woken from his day-sleep. He'll stalk the shadows for his prey a bit later. For now, his inner Beast is as quiet as a church mouse. Gregory stands in front of a vanity mirror and examines himself.
He looks dull, tired, and older than he actually is. His skin is paler than usual, and there are some deep lines on his face. He shakes his head and groans as he walks away from the mirror. He glances at the bed and sees how bloodied the sheets and pillows are.
"I'll change it myself. I don't want Cassandra seeing this and bombarding me with questions. Questions for which I simply lack a cogent answer to."
Gregory lets his Ghoul in and wordlessly ushers her to the living room.
Cassandra greets him earnestly, "Hiya, Greg! Sleep well?"
He dryly replies, "Yes."
Cassandra regards him with a concerned look, "Not gonna ask how me day was? You...usually do that."
Gregory stifles a sigh, "Was it pleasant?"
Cassandra smiles widely and declares, "I had scampi fries! I also popped over to the nearby pound shop. That mold's getting worse, yeah? They ought to fix that up."
Gregory blinks, "Hm. Sit. There's much that needs to be discussed before I can officially send you off."
Cassandra sits down on the plush sofa and places her handbag next on the center table. A tight smile is plastered on her face, and she keeps glancing at Gregory out of the corner of her eyes.
He's in the kitchen, milling about and checking the state of the fridge. He mainly keeps it stocked in case a mortal guest shows up and is in need of some hospitality. The Toreador, however, doesn't keep bags of blood in there. He's not a bagger. He hunts for his meals.
Gregory walks into the living room with a bowl of crisps. He puts it on the center table and plainly gestures towards it, "If you'd like."
Cass grabs a single piece, thanks her Domitor for his consideration, and bites down. Gregory watches her chew with a rather unreadable expression on his face.
The crunching sounds fill the living room as Cassandra continues to chow down on the crisps.
Gregory begins, "I owe the Brujah a favor. I believe that now is the time to make good on that promise."
Cassandra finishes chewing, "Oh. What kind of favor?"
"It was open-ended. If they ever needed me, they were free to call upon me and I'd come running. Now, I'm authorizing you to do that running on my behalf. Do whatever they tell you without fail, Cassandra. It's in both of our best interests to remain on their good side."
Cassandra's shaky hand reaches out for another crisp, "What if they ask me to...do something messed up? Or something downright illegal? I don't wanna go to jail, Greg! Or get me head chopped off because I inadvertently breached the Masquerade or something!"
Gregory sighs, "I'll take...some responsibility for your actions. It's only fair, I suppose. That being said, don't make a catastrophic mess out of things. You're not a child, Cassandra."
"Condescending much? I know how old I am...and how old I really am. Blimey. Sorry. So, I represent you, eh? It won't be anything too outlandish, would it?"
"I haven't a clue what the Brujah have planned. I'm not going to lie to you. Be prepared for anything that they might throw your way."
"Who exactly do you owe this favor to, anyway? I've never heard about any of this before this evening! Why am I hearing about all of this now? Why are you keeping stuff from me, Greg?"
Gregory shrugs, "Because I didn't feel that it was relevant in the performing of your duties as my Ghoul. There are a good many reasons why I have kept you in the dark in regard to certain matters pertaining to Kindred Society and its broader happenings. But that ends tonight."
He emphasizes, "You won't just be representing me; you'll be representing me AND our Clan while dealing with the Brujah. Be on your best behavior, Cassandra. No quips or witty retorts. You have to be...a lot more serious than usual."
Cassandra fumbles with her crisp, "I--I can be serious. When I want to, yeah. I'm serious and plenty responsible! Got employee of the month at work, y'know?"
"Yes. In regard to your work, your vacation officially starts now. I've already taken the liberty of speaking with your superiors. We have to make the most out of these next few nights. It's quite possible that we won't be seeing each other for a good while."
"How far are you sending me, anyway?"
The Toreador Domitor flatly answers, "It depends. Ensure that the Lexus has enough petrol in it."
Cassandra licks her lips and asks, "Will you be sending me back to London?"
"No. Not with those blasted Garou running all over the place- the risk is far too great. Everyone that I know from London has either met their Final Death, moved out- like us, or have fled."
Cassandra shakily laughs, "We--we got out of London just in time, yeah?"
"Yes."
The Garou Nation (werewolves) were responsible for bringing about what is now dubbed the "Fall of London". They came in and viciously purged that entire Domain of its deeply entrenched bloodsucker population.
Greg and Cass moved out of the city long before the attacks began.
The Garou waged their war on multiple fronts (dimensions). They moved from the Umbra to the physical world and back again. They used any means necessary to get the job done. By the end of it, the ashes of many Kindred were a testament to the savage might and power of Gaia's mightiest warriors.
And now, London is firmly Garou country. But why?
Because the werewolves are preparing for a war against the water companies that have been polluting Gaia's oceans and seas (all those nasty sewerage discharges and spills in the UK, for instance). That's their primary concern.
The wolves are readying themselves, training vigorously, bringing new cubs into their fold, and getting stronger by the day. In another decade, they hope to bring the fight to the Bane Spirit of the Filthy Iguana (which has its talons deep in the water bosses and corporations).
Driving out the Kindred was a necessary component of the Garou's plans. They needed a staging ground from which to launch their attacks on the privatized water companies.
Bringing about the Fall of London was absolutely necessary to this end. The Garou couldn't possibly launch their campaign from the heart of Kindred territory.
The attacks were savage and caught the entirety of the Camarilla elite by surprise. By the time it was over, the ashes of many supposedly untouchable Cainites were all that was left of the legacies that they had spent centuries cultivating.
Even the Ghouls were not spared.
For instance, Gregory's own Sire was torn apart by a large pack of werewolves while attempting to flee from them. Even till now, many of London's survivors (Ghouls, Kindred, or otherwise) haven't fully recovered from the traumas and horrors visited upon them by the Garou Nation.
(As an aside, there are many Fomori compromising and tainting the UK's Environment Agency from within, so the warriors of Gaia have their work cut out for them.)
Anyways...
Gregory fixes his Ghoul with a significant look, "I intend to get even with the Brujah before the next sunrise. Whatever they ask of you- no matter how trivial it may seem- do it to the best of your abilities. Yes?"
Cassandra's expression gets a lot more serious. She nods, "Fine. Yeah. Where am I supposed to be, anyway?"
"The Horn 'N' Stag pub. It's quite a way off from where we are, so you best take the Lexus if you want to reach there quicker."
She asks, "What do I tell them if they ask me who I am and where you are?"
