Gregory answers, "Tell them what I told you: you are a Ghoul who is authorized- fully- to act on behalf of her Domitor. There will be no need for them to come around and seek me out. And I would prefer it if I weren't dragged into any further messes."
Cassandra slowly exhales, "Wow. OK. It's not gonna be too hard of a task, right?"
"I don't know what they'll throw at you. Just...try to keep a low profile. Don't call me unless it's an emergency. We don't want to draw any unneeded attention to ourselves. Especially with how...compromised phones may be."
"Tch, yeah. Don't remind me. We have to change ours soon, yeah? We're not taking any chances! We can't, right?"
"Hmm. Tell them that I sent you. The Brujah will know me. They all know who I am. I owe an Elder named Winston. If Winston isn't there, find a man named Harry- he'll point you in the right direction. Winston usually hangs around the pub; it's where we conducted most of our business dealings and negotiations."
"Oh. Nice! Sure, yeah. Anything else before I go off?"
Gregory grimly tells her, "Be very cautious, Cassandra. Be vigilant at all times. If you feel that something is amiss or that someone is watching you, then do your best to get out of the situation. Do not be afraid to defend yourself if need be."
Cassandra's expression falters, "Cor blimey. Defend meself against...?" She lets the question linger in the air.
Gregory shrugs, "If that's not an option, then run as fast as you can. Do whatever you can to slow down your pursuer...or pursuers. We've already established that you are not a warrior. And I myself have no issue with admitting that I'm not exactly cut out for combat either. But please be careful out there."
Cassandra chuckles uneasily, "You know how much I love me life, Greg! I love living in this world! You know how much I--I love being a Ghoul, forever me age and all that other stuff! I--I'll do my best, pro--promise."
He nods, "Yes. So please, please be very careful. Only reach out to me if absolutely necessary. I've told you all that you need to know...for now. If anyone raises a fuss with the arrangement, feel free to take on a more assertive tone. Be polite, but don't let them push you around. Then again, with Brujah, you're going to want to have a bit of thick skin."
Cassandra bites her lip, "Oh. Uh...sure, yeah, I can do that. I think."
Gregory notes, "You know quite a lot about the Brujah Clan. Use that to your advantage. As a matter of fact, utilize everything that you know to the best of your ability. The more information that you have in regard to any given subject, the easier it is for you to formulate a plan. And you possess a great deal of information."
Cassandra laughs softly and taps her head, "It's all in here, innit?"
"Hmm. I hope that those lessons were well worth your time and effort. You know things about Kindred Society that even I do not. It's undoubtedly a lot of information. But you've done well in retaining most of it, yes?"
Cassandra musters up a smile, "Mm-hmm. Are we sure that the Brujah aren't planning to pull some kind of nasty trick on us?"
"The Brujah are many things. But they aren't crass tricksters. Well, not this lot anyway. They may not be as well-liked as the Gangrel who mill about near the city's zoo, but they are not deceitful. They can, however, be given to their anger. You must be careful about how you approach them. And watch your words around some of them. You wouldn't want to set them off because of a poorly chosen word."
She winces, "I--I don't. Yeah, I know how...err...testy the Punks can get. So, Horn 'N' Stag pub, Winston. Got it. And if he's not there, ask for Harry, yeah?"
Gregory smiles tightly, "Yes. Precisely. Good luck out there, Cassandra. Be safe. And come back...intact."
She faintly chuckles, grabs a few crisps on her way out, and leaves the Haven with a lot of thoughts going through her head.
The drive to this public house is quite long- there's a small buildup of traffic along a few major roads, and Cassandra has to make a few turns and use side streets to bypass the worst of the traffic. And the pouring rain isn't exactly helping matters.
The Ghoul eventually reaches the pub. She parks nearby and keenly stares at the establishment. Its exterior exudes a sort of rustic, old country charm. Its windows are completely opaque, and there's a warm yellowish glow emanating from within.
The sign out front depicts a twisted pair of antlers and a large black horn. She can faintly hear the dull roar of the patrons from within. The Toreador Ghoul grabs her handbag, exits her car, and crosses the street. The rain pelts her umbrella. She glances at the pub's windows once more and takes a deep breath.
She forces it out and pushes the front door open. All the laughter ceases when she steps inside. So many dead eyes turn their gaze towards her. A chill goes up her spine, and she can't help but flinch.
Cassandra is frozen in her spot. The interior of this pub is quite spacious. There are several tables and chairs spread about, a bar with a good many bottles behind it, and a rather large crowd of working-class vampires. A strong, odorous, and pungent smell hits her nose.
It's not necessarily a bad smell, but it is rather overpowering all the same.
A bald man with a scar across his face harshly asks, "Who are you?"
"C--Cassandra. I'm here to see a Winston."
"Winston, eh? Ah, shit. Oi, 'Arry! Some bird's here asking about Winston!"
A rather short and thin fellow with a round face, fair complexion, and dark, greasy hair emerges from the backroom. A bloodied baseball bat is casually swung over his right shoulder. A large scar is visible on his forehead.
He speaks with a sort of nasally and grating tone, "Who the 'ell are you? What do you want?"
Cassandra looks over his shoulder and gasps when she spots a dead body slumped over in the backroom. She quickly averts her gaze and tries her best not to think about what might have happened to that poor lass whose head is a mushy mess.
She says, "I--uh...My name's Cassandra. Gregory sent me."
Harry points his bloodied bat at her, "You're Gregory's Ghoul, eh? Where the 'ell is that fucking muppet?"
Cassandra nervously gulps, "He--he sent me. I am authorized to--to act on his behalf."
Harry groans and shakes his head, "Wonderful. He sent his Ghoul to do his dirty work. How typical of him. What did he tell you?"
Cassandra shakily points to the dead lady slumped over on the floor, "Wh--what happened to her?"
Harry looks over his shoulder and shrugs, "The clone was acting up. Had to be put down."
"Clone?!"
The Brujah plainly nods. He rubs the bloodied sleeve of his bomber jacket on the doorpost, "Yeah. She was a fucking clone. Not a real person. Stop gawking at me like that. She's hardly the first one who 'ad to be put down this week."
Harry nods over to a random Brujah wearing a pair of dark-blue coveralls, "Oi! Muppet! Take that shit off when you're in here, yeah? Hang it up over there." He uses the bat to point to a row of coat racks on the left-hand side.
Cassandra is getting a bit lightheaded. She blinks rapidly and sits down on the closest chair. She stammers, "She--she's not a real person?"
"What are you, daft? She was a clone, not a real human being! Dunno what happened to the original and the CODACORP security team- of which I am a part of- isn't paid to care about the...product."
Another random Punk lifts her scuffed bag of blood high in the air, "Aye! Hear, hear! We drink to all the dead clones, though? Right, Harry?"
Harry flatly nods, "Hmm. So, Gregory sent his Ghoul in his place. Can't say that I'm surprised by that, honestly."
Cassandra grimaces when asking, "Where's--where's Winston?"
Harry's chuckle is mirthless, "Wouldn't you just love to know. Oi, any of you wanna tell this bird where that Elder currently is?"
There's a resounding "No!" from the gathered Rebels.
Harry sighs and lowers his gaze, "Right. Here's the situation, love--"
A young lass comes bursting through the front door. She's drenched in rainwater and heaving for breath. Her clothing is a scattered mishmash of styles and patterns. There's an expression of abject horror on her fair face.
1 side of her chin-length hair is bleached blonde whilst the other side is brown like chocolate. Her eyes are a dull shade of green. She looks to be in her early-30s, at most. And she's absolutely fuming.
Harry glowers at her, "Marble Cake...what do you want--oh, shit--"
(It's not too hard to see why she's called that!)
Marble Cake points an accusatory finger at her fellow Brujah, "Where is she? The latest clone? Did--did you kill her? She--she was showing signs of--of higher thought! You bloody bastard, you already--"
Harry sighs heavily, "Oh, great. This is what happens when you go and make friends with the fuckin' clones! I 'ad to do her in, alright? She was acting all erratic and you know how pissy the company gets when their product tries to pull a runner. No risks, not when our salaries depend on it."
Cassandra quietly whispers, "Clones. Cor blimey, what the 'ell am I--am I getting meself into here?"
Marble Cake hears that and glares at the Ghoul, "Who even are you?"
Harry answers, "Gregory's girl. He was too chicken to show up himself. So, he sent her in his stead- the next best choice, I reckon. You wanna tell her what happened to Winston? You're here, so you 2 might as well 'ave a chin wag, because I'm tired of explaining what went wrong with him."
Marble Cake sighs and tosses her jacket onto a table. She lazily gestures for the Toreador Ghoul to follow her. The pair head to a table in the corner and sit across from each other. Marble Cake takes the seat closest to the wall.
She stares up at some pictures of employees in their CODACORP coveralls. The same dark blue outfits that Harry and many others were wearing.
Cassandra awkwardly introduces herself by holding out her trembling hand. Marble Cake simply shakes it.
Cass twitches at how deathly cold the other woman's fingers are, "Uh...yeah, hiya. I'm Cassandra."
The Brujah motions to the way her hair is colored, "Most of the people around here call me 'Marble Cake'. I don't give out my real name so easily...Cassandra. It's a pretty name, I suppose."
"Th--thanks. You've got...err...nice hair, yeah."
Marble Cake faintly chuckles, "It's some nearly expired hair coloring that I nicked from the Asda's beauty aisle."
She leans closer and growls a bit, "And if you see me blagging stuff, then you didn't? Alright?"
Cassandra gulps and nods, "Yeah. No problem, y--yeah. Sure."
"Times are tough, love. I do what I have to do to cut corners. This city isn't getting any cheaper by the night. The prices keep rising and rising."
"Tell me about it! So, can I meet Winston? Gregory sent me. As his Ghoul, I have been fully authorized to act, speak, and even negotiate on his behalf. That's why I'm here. I need to see Winston."
A look of uneasiness washes over the Rebel's face, "Winston. Ah. About that..."
"What happened?"
A slight screeching noise echoes throughout the pub as Marble Cake winces slightly, "Yeah, that happened."
"What??!"
Marble Cake patiently explains, "He Frenzied. Bad. Real bad, actually. He turned into a Wight. Some of us nearly lost our limbs trying to contain him. This was a few nights ago. We've managed to contain him down in the sewers. In his state, he's already killed 2 sewage workers."
Cassandra's eyes widen to an impossible degree, "Bloody hell, he's a Wight?!"
Marble Cake grimly nods, "Yup. Harry, in his infinite wisdom, doesn't want to tell a damned soul about it. Usually, it's up to the Sheriff to come in and put down anyone who's succumbed to the Wassail, but 'Arry wants it all done...discreetly. If the Sheriff finds out that we have an Elder Wight on our hands, we'll all be in deep shit."
Cassandra is at a loss for words.
Harry returns, having washed all the blood stains from himself. He drags a chair and sits between the 2 women, "I hope that you're done catching her up on all the shite that's happened around 'ere?"
Marble Cake silently nods.
Harry turns his attention to Cass, "Winston's down in the sewers. He's gone. Fucking gone. I sent in a good lad of mine to try and turn him to ashes. No response. I'm not losing a 4th person to that fucking muppet."
He pulls out a pack of cigarettes and lights 1. He hands the pack to Cass, who declines it with a wave of her hand.
Harry forcefully exhales smoke and leans closer, "We've got a bit of a situation, yeah? Normally, we'd send the Childe in to take care of their Frenzying Sire, but Clive- that fucking muppet- is not amongst us. Ran off to America years ago, he did. Winston's other Childer, meanwhile, aren't anywhere close by. None of them are. Fucking muppets."
(He really likes calling people fucking muppets.)
Marble Cake snatches a cigarette from her fellow Punk and lights it up. The brief flicker of the flame causes her to shudder.
Harry continues, "So, the only thing that we've got left on our grubby little hands is a Frenzied Elder. The longer this goes on, the harder it gets to keep everything under wraps."
He jabs a finger at the Ghoul, "And that's where you come in."
Cassandra's jaw drops, "What? What do I have to do with any--any of this?!"
Harry mentions, "Your Gregory is partly the reason why Winston's a senseless Wight now. Your Domitor pressured an Elder of our Clan to acquire some or the other rare piece of scribbling for some or the other fucking muppet buddy of his."
He points a thumb at the backroom, "And Winston went out of his way to try and secure that piece. You know the funny thing? He actually got the scribbling. I don't see the appeal in it, personally. None of us do, but we can't deny the fact that Winston had a way with the arts. He had an eye for that sort of thing."
Harry bitterly goes on, "Tried to teach us about it, but we're too busy working our arses off to be worrying about any of that. The last time that I saw the scribbling was a couple of nights ago, before the Final Frenzy took hold of the poor sod. Then...the screaming started..." He trails off and vacantly stares at the front door.
Marble Cake takes over, "He's...strong. He--he was strong. We were lucky to even contain him. I've seen what a Wight can do. It's not pretty. And him being an Elder only makes things immensely harder for us. His Disciplines are stronger. His power and strength--"
Harry clicks his tongue, "Don't talk him up so much. If your Gregory hadn't pushed our Winston so bloody hard just for some scribbling, then we wouldn't be in this mess."
Cassandra avoids making eye contact with the 2 Punks, "Hey, listen, I'm not trying to stir up any trouble with you lot, yeah? Just--just call the Sheriff, get a Blood Hunt declared and--and finish him off, yeah?"
Harry scoffs, "A Blood Hunt? Blimey, you seem to know a lot about us, eh? Hmph. Greg is responsible; therefore, it falls upon Greg to come 'ere and fix this mess that his shady dealings 'ave caused!"
Cassandra gulps and lamely states, "He--he's busy."
Harry retorts, "I don't care--"
Marble Cake places her hand on top of Harry's hand, "'Arry, the Toreador has...authorized this one to act on his behalf." She fixes him with an inscrutable gaze.
Harry frowns at her, and Marble Cake lightly nods.
He looks at Cass with a certain glint in his dead eyes, "Well, isn't Gregory just full of surprises, eh? So, you're his additional pair of limbs, eyes, ears, all that shite, yeah?"
Cassandra swallows a lump forming in her throat, "Err...yes. He--he told me to find a Winston, so..."
Harry plainly states, "You'll get your expensive drawing AFTER you do us a favor, love. You don't look stupid, so you can already guess where this is headed, hmm? You want the drawing; we want Winston dusted before he can cause a bigger mess. It's a simple exchange. After that, we're even."
Oh, shit.
Cassandra's entire body shakes with fear, "W--wait, wait. What are you--are you saying that I've gotta go into the sewers and try to--to destroy A FRENZYING ELDER?! Are you mental?!"
Harry doesn't flinch, "Take consolation in the fact that he's probably been weakened a bit, yeah? He doesn't 'ave all his senses about him, so it should be easy to sneak around him. He needs to be destroyed and you're the lass for the job."
Cassandra erratically gestures to all the other Brujah in this pub and loudly asks, "Well, why the 'ell can't THEY do it? I'm a Ghoul! I'm not like you lot! This is utterly mad!"
Harry's voice is barely above a whisper, "Because it's not their responsibility, love. We already have jobs that suck the Unlife out of us. You try being a part of the CODACORP security team, running security for all those fancy cryo-pods and shite. Most of us get back home minutes before sunrise."
His tone suddenly turns bitter, "And the pay's absolute shite. But the projects are...somewhat exciting. The cryogenics is all a front for the real stuff- human cloning. This job...it--it keeps us stable; it provides us with an--an outlet."
Cassandra blandly asks, "An outlet for what?"
Marble Cake regards her with narrowed eyes, "You know the Brujah Clan Bane?"
The Ghoul nods, "I do, yeah. Violent Temper. Quick to anger and slow to come down from it."
"Right. For many of us, employment at CODACORP provides us with a sense of stability, camaraderie, and the occasional row...but in a safe space. And it helps that we're doing good work."
Cassandra's face scrunches up, "I don't know if I'd call human cloning 'good work'. There are a lot of ethical--"
Harry dismissively waves his hand, "Oh, sod that! We also provide specialized pods for the Orpheus Group. You know who they are, love? They're like...eh...ghost-whisperers, yeah. We manufacture those freezy pods for them so they can go and talk to dead people, calm them, put 'em at ease, and help 'em move on, yeah?"
"Oh. Oh, wow."
Marble Cake concludes, "So, as you can imagine, we're fairly busy most nights. This is simply our breaktime; work and even more work take up the remainder of our endless nights. You've barely got more time and energy to spare on other things. We couldn't destroy Winston even if we wanted to."
Harry growls and puts in, "Too many obligations...like disposing of the runaway product. The whole cloning thing is to solve the Hunger crisis that's worsening among our kind. Their blood is synthetic, sure, but you'll get used to its taste...eventually. You think we're making clones for kicks? It's bloody intense work!"
Cassandra prods, "And what exactly do you do, Harry?"
"I'm a security guard. I carry a gun which I barely get to use- not that I'd ever need it, anyway. Got me fists and legs to do all the talking. A lot of the people in here work the same shift as I do."
Marble Cake rubs her face and exhales sharply, "I myself am a...technical researcher. I--I don't look the part, haha! But that's what I am. We've recently shipped out a large order to an Orpheus subsidiary in Magnolia City, USA. Cryonics, cloning, I work on it all for pitiful pay."
Harry chuckles and pats his fellow Rebel on the back, "That's how we all feel, Marb...that's how we all feel. So, you ready to do a small, wee favor for us, Cassandra?"
Cassandra's face tics. She asks where the telephone is.
Harry vaguely gestures in some direction, "The dog and bone's near the back. Right next to the bathrooms. Hold your nose, hahahaha!"
Cassandra gets up and heads to the ol' dog and bone to give her Domitor a ring. She inserts a few coins into its rusted slot. It's a rotor phone with an old-fashioned feel to it. She dials Greg's number, and the line begins ringing.
"Speaking."
"It's me."
Gregory sighs heavily, "What is it now? I'm busy. I only told you to call me if it was something important."
Cassandra's voice cracks as she asks, "Can't you just--can't you please come here and do this? P--please?"
Gregory clicks his tongue, "No. I'm busy. Very busy. Whatever it is, you'll have to handle it on your own. And make sure you get the painting. Winston can be a bit stubborn on the price, but don't let him corner you."
The words get caught in Cassandra's throat, and she can only let out a hopeless whimper.
The Toreador asks, "What's the matter? Hello? Are you still there?"
Cassandra meekly answers, "I--I am, yeah."
"We shouldn't talk about this over the phone. You and I both know why. Whatever it is, just get it done, alright?"
"Y--yeah. Sure. I--I won't let you down, right?"
"That's the idea. Now, please, don't phone me until you've got the painting." He hangs up.
Cassandra slowly sets the grimy phone back on its holder. She takes a deep breath and exhales through her nose.
Harry and Marble Cake are having a very hush-hush talk with each other. The latter asks if it was wise to go and give Cassandra such vital company information.
The former derisively scoffs and states, "If those fucking muppets want me to zip me lips, then they'd do well to pay us more. Until then, I'm tellin' everyone and their dog about what it is that we do. It's no secret."
He turns his head to see Cassandra making her way over, "Oh, you're back. What'd that Toreador muppet say? Has he changed his mind?"
Cassandra has a defeated expression written across her face. She slowly shakes her head from side to side, "No. I...I can't leave without the painting."
Harry repeats the terms, "And I'm not letting you leave until that screeching mess down in the sewers has been turned to ashes. And don't even think of trying to blag that scribbling from right under our hairy noses! We're watching you. We're watching every bloody thing that you do, Ghoul."
Marble Cake nudges Harry, "Oi! Not so rash! She's smart enough to understand what's at stake over 'ere. No need to go and scare the remaining color out of her, 'Arry! Tch."
Harry rises from the chair and dusts himself off, "You wanna see the scribbling, yeah? I know how...enraptured you Toreadors can get when art's involved. C'mon, love, this way. And don't even think of trying to pull a runner- you won't make it past the front door before we're all on you!"
Cassandra numbly follows him and tries to keep her hands from shaking. Marble Cake puts her cold arm around the Ghoul's slumped shoulders.
The Brujah softly encourages, "Don't let him scare you. We're all rather tense around 'ere, lately. With Winston being the way he is and all. It came as a--a shocker to all of us."
Cassandra looks down at her feet, "Why'd he turn? How'd he turn?"
"I don't know the specifics, love. All I know is that it...just happened. And now we're still here, dealing with the aftermath."
"And you want me to...go down there and finish him off?"
"That's the idea."
Harry pulls a tatty old sheet from and reveals the painting. He grumbles, "Here, 'ave a gander at the thing. Worth millions of pounds, apparently. Fucking millions. What kind of muppet drops millions of quid on...what the 'ell is it?"
He squints and slightly bends forward, "Is that a woman's body?"
The painting in question is all dark colors. It appears to be a close up of a woman placing a hand on her chest. The colors are all muted- dark gray, and black. A lot of literal erratic scribbles have been painted over the entire canvas.
Nothing much is clear in this piece.
Cassandra asks, "Did the painting cause Winston to Frenzy? It certainly looks...unsettling. Eerie, even."
Harry scoffs, "You sound like a Malkavian. Mental muppets, that lot. Don't like 'em, never did. What the 'ell does Gregory even want with whatever this is supposed to be? Winston claimed that it was an 'otherworldly, priceless painting.' A lot of good that did him in the end. Pfft."
Marble Cake comments, "Due to how intensive our work can get, we don't really get time to...appreciate the arts or all the other finer things in Unlife. I missed a gallery opening because I was busy working on some or the other cryo-pod component. Such is the unremarkable Unlife of a CODACORP employee."
Cassandra is at a loss for further words.
Harry prods, "Let me guess: it's gonna collect dust in the house of some fucking millionaire muppet, yeah? Who is this drawing for, anyway? Or does your Domitor not tell you anything? Hmph."
He throws the tattered sheet back over the painting and leans on the wall, "Listen, I lost a good mate of mine. Sending him in was a mistake on my part, I'll admit. And covering up the deaths of 2 sewage workers is not something I take pride in. All the more reason to put Winston down. Now, tonight."
Cassandra mumbles, "I--I'm not privy to a lot of what Greg gets up to. I'm only vaguely aware of what he does- I know that it involves dealing in--in art and stuff. He doesn't really like talking much about it."
Marble Cake quips, "I can see why! Don't worry, Cassandra. We'll give you...adequate tools to complete the job on our behalf. And no, if you try and lure him out into the open when the sun's up, that'd complicate things further for us. That's the last thing we need."
Cassandra blows a raspberry, "I'm here and I'm n--not leaving. You lot won't let me. Fine, I--I'll do it. And after tonight, we'll be square, yeah?"
Harry gives her his word, "Yes. For what it's worth, I'll be glad to see the back of Gregory. He's partially responsible for this mess. And you're here to clean it up. After that, bugger off back to your posh, gilded lie of a life and leave us common muppets be."
Cassandra counters, "I'm not posh! Do I sound posh to you?"
Harry chuckles, "Hm. Whatever. Marble Cake, take her downstairs and get her equipped, yeah?"
"Sure. Come along, love. I have to warn you now: we don't exactly have the best equipment. And trying to requisition anything from our vast arsenal is more of a bureaucratic nightmare than anything else. Our employers are very...particular about their toys falling into the wrong hands. This is a big-big company, after all."
The Punk ruefully adds, "A big-big company that reports record quarterlies but routinely lays off a whole swath of employees with a few flicks of the wrist."
The pair of ladies go downstairs to a dimly lit corridor. A reinforced metal door is at the farthest end. The floor and walls are a drab, dark gray color. 2 individuals in CODACORP coveralls are guarding the door, holding their company-issued assault rifles.
The duo eye the 2 women approaching and immediately straighten their postures.
A bald man with a styled mustache nods at Marble Cake, "Who's this?"
Marble Cake shrugs, "Is Winston still banging on the door?"
The other person next to mustache man shakes their head, "He came by a few times, snarling and--and screeching at us. Blood was just flowing down his mouth. He stared at us for a few seconds, then he moved on, shambling about like a drunken sod. We heard a lot of crashing and banging noises, then it went quiet."
Bald mustache lad nods, "Mm-hmm. You found a solution to deal with this? Because neither of us are equipped to deal with an Elder who's lost his marbles."
Marble Cake explains, "This one's from the Toreador Clan. She's a Ghoul. Gregory's Ghoul, if you can believe it. We're sending her in."
Both individuals look at Cassandra, then back at Marble Cake, "What?!"
Marble Cake dryly orders the bald fella, "Give her your old supplies you leave lying around. I'll grab some petrol from upstairs. Do those flares of ours still work?"
He checks the watch on his wrist, "They should work...in theory. It's been decades since they've been used. This is all quite short notice, Marb. Are you sure that it's wise sending someone like her into the fray?"
She doesn't answer and starts heading back upstairs.
The man extends his icy hand, "Name's Charlie! C'mon, I'll show you me old toys that I've still kept with me...even after all these years. Oi, wait here and guard the door, alright?"
The other Brujah gives a thumbs up and returns to their previous stance.
Cassandra's hands are shaking. Her lips quiver as she tries to speak, "S--so, what is it exactly that you do?"
Charlie snickers, "Don't the coveralls give it away? I work security for CODACORP. As a matter of fact, a lot of us Agitators are employed by CODACORP. I'd be lying if I said that it was gainful employment, but it keeps the old roof over me head, so I won't dare complain too loudly. The pay's not great, but the hours are manageable...for our kind, at least."
Cassandra tries her luck, "Do you have a--a flamethrower? It would make things a lot easier."
"Funny you should ask. I left mine back in Somme. Not exactly pleasant memories. And the smell of burnt German flesh isn't exactly something you easily forget. Got jumped and Embraced while out on patrol. The rest is...history."
There's this sort of traumatized look in Charlies dead eyes. He still shows signs of shellshock. The imperialist slaughterhouse of WW1 was no laughing matter.
Charlie takes Cassandra to a cramped room full of lockers. He groans and scratches his neck, "I'd have to rummage through me old stuff, then. Gimme a sec, would you?"
Cass nods and leans against the wall, her eyes fixed on the floor.
Charlie pulls out a rusty chest, blows a whole layer of dust off the lid, and places it down with a heavy thud. He rummages through its contents and takes out a few items.
He pulls out a mechanically powered dynamo flashlight, its pullable string showing obvious signs of wear and tear.
Charlie comments, "Flashlights are bloody useful down there. This thing saved me neck numerous times."
"Is--is that all you 'ave?"
"Patience, patience. It's a lot of memories. Painful ones, mind you. Gimme--gimme a sec extra..."
Charlie holds up a heavy-looking revolver and inspects it from multiple angles.
"It packs quite a punch. I still have some ammo left. Always saved an extra bullet in case I--in case things got overwhelming and all the other ways were thoroughly exhausted."
Cassandra winces, "That gun won't even tickle him! C--can't you just gimme your gun?"
Charlie retorts, "Can you even shoot? And no, my weapon is my own. Company property and I'd be expected to pay a pretty penny if I lost company property. No can do, love. The revolver, however, is all yours...free of charge."
Cassandra mumbles, "This is mental."
He points the revolver at her and asks, "Do you know how to shoot?"
The Ghoul stammers, "Err--"
Charlie groans, "Oh, great. It's simple, just steady yourself, point, take aim, and shoot. The recoil on this thing is nasty, so you're going to want to make sure that you 'ave yourself a strong grip around the handle, yeah? And watch your footing, too."
Cassandra timidly accepts the revolver. Her fingers wrap around the gun's handle, and her thumb finds the hammer.
She gulps nervously, "It--it's loaded, yeah?"
"Yes." The Brujah tosses her a box of ammunition. The gun almost slips out of her hand when she tries to catch the ammo box.
Charlie shakes his head and sharply exhales, "Oi! Watch it! Don't go and shoot a part of yourself off, now."
Cassandra rightly asks, "What the 'ell am I expected to do with this? Your Winston's not some mortal who's lost it- he's an Elder who has already killed 3 people! At most, this gun would slow him down. Even in his Wight state, his wounds can still heal over time, right? You--you can't honestly expect me to be able to do this all by myself."
Charlie shrugs, "Listen, I'm just the guy guarding the door, yeah? Don't worry, Marble Cake's coming back with the real equipment."
"Oh, right. Fucking petrol. How am I supposed to get close enough to him to light him on fire without him tearing me to shreds first?! This is not my sort of thing!"
"Ah. So, you're a lover. Not a fighter, hmm?"
Cassandra shifts her weight from 1 foot to the other, "Y--Yeah. Something like that."
The Brujah simply tells her, "Then, I wish you all the best." He lifts his chin and walks right past her without saying anything further.
Cassandra is left standing there, clutching the revolver in 1 hand and the clunky dynamo flashlight in the other. A few bullets are spilled out from the ammo box near her feet.
She hears rapid footsteps coming down the stairs, and then the familiar voice of Marble Cake, "Oi! There you are! C'mon!"
Cassandra shoves the ammo and gun into her handbag. She stares at the flashlight and wonders where the "on" switch is. She sees Marble Cake's face appear around the corner, "C'mon!! C'mon!"
Cassandra hopelessly trudges towards the Brujah. She mumbles, "W--why petrol? Can't you give me something more practical?"
Marble Cake gives the Ghoul a toothy smile while holding up some signal flares, "You're more than free to sit around and make molotov cocktails, love. But we're on a bit of a schedule here, and time is a luxury we simply cannot afford. Besides, these are more than adequate for the job."
Charlie notes, "Fire destroys us. It horribly scares us, too. We call it the--"
Cassandra claps back, "I know what it's called! I'm not new to the nature of Kindred, OK? And--and how the 'ell does this thing even work?"
The person next to Charlie answers, "You have to pull on the pull chain. It's powered entirely by electricity generated by the muscle power of the user, so it does not need replacement of batteries, or recharging from an electrical source."
Charlie plainly nods his head up and down, a slight smirk dancing on his thin lips.
Cassandra groans loudly, "Errrrgh! This is bollocks!"
Marble Cake shrugs, "Too late to back out now, love. Only way out is...through that door. Winston's still running around in there, so be on your guard, yeah?"
Cassandra is provided with a schoolbag in which to put the flares and the petrol can.
She's expected to work wonders with a revolver, a loud-ass flashlight that requires manually pulling the string every single time, some lousy signal flares, and a can of petrol weighing her down in a schoolbag with faded childish pictures on it.
Lovely jubbly.
Cassandra mumbles under her breath, "This is fucking stupid. I--I'm not going to survive this..." She trails off and feels her heart rate steadily rising.
She's escorted by Marble Cake to the metal door, where the 2 individuals are still waiting. Marble Cake nods at them. Charlie puts his hand on the door's handle while the other Punk readies their weapon in case shit goes sideways.
The door swings open, and Cassandra is shoved in by Marble Cake. She lets out a gasp as the door groans shut on her face. The 2 Brujah guards fix her with a sympathetic look, then resume their previous duties.
Marble Cake looks at the trembling Ghoul and smiles feebly, "Good luck in there, love. And--and try not to die, yeah?"
Cassandra's face scrunches up in frustration. She looks around and tries to get her bearings. The lights in here are dim and look like they're on the verge of burning out.
She pulls out her revolver and readies her flashlight, too. She cranks on it once, the loud whirring noise of the machine's insides filling the otherwise eerily quiet area.
Gregory's Ghoul winces uncomfortably and gives the thing another tug. This time, it emanates a slightly better beam of light. It's bright enough for her to see the floor, and a few feet in front of her. But it slowly starts to fade out as the tool's mechanical whirring noises steadily grow quieter.
Cassandra grumbles, "Fuck!"
She's going to have to balance being able to see with not drawing any attention to herself. Who knows how heightened that Wight's senses are in his irreparably Frenzied state?
Cassandra blinks and steadies herself. She gathers her scattered composure and slowly walks down the dark and winding tunnel. Winston's banging and snarling echoes from the distant reaches of the sewer, causing the hairs on the back of her neck to stand on end.
She keeps the gun pointed forward and the flashlight pointing straight ahead as well. Cassandra takes a few more tentative steps and stops to listen for anything abnormal.
When she's certain that it's safe, she gives the flashlight a few good pulls so that it won't die out on her again.
Cassandra gulps and tries not to hyperventilate.
While walking and being sure to check her corners, she thinks to, "This is wrong. This is so fucking wrong and--and stupid and NOT what I had expected to--to be doing tonight! I don't know what Greg gets up to or how he--he got wrapped up in all this, but--but this is not what I signed up for!"
She stops and lets out a tiny gasp when a figure swooshes past her. Winston. She's only caught a glimpse of him, but he looked like an utter monster.
Cass licks her lips and points the gun at the wall ahead. Her arms are shaking and she's trying not to breathe too loudly. Sweat mercilessly drips down her forehead.
Cassandra can feel her heart racing like crazy.
The walls are rough and cold to the touch. The floor is a mess of broken bricks, shattered glass, and the occasional discarded wrapper, used condom, or empty plastic bottle.
The ceiling is a bit low, and she feels the occasional drops of water hitting her on the head. The aging pipes above her look ready to burst at a moment's notice. She tries not to think about how unpleasant that'd be.
Her foot accidentally kicks something, and it makes a rattling noise as it skids across the floor. She lets out a slight yelp and quickly points the revolver and flashlight at the item.
It's a squeaky rat toy that has some dried blood on it.
Cassandra shakes her head frantically and moves on. Winston lets out another guttural screech, the echo of which is being carried down the winding tunnel.
The sound seems to have come from the far left, and that's the way she'll be heading. Coincidentally (or not), she spots a nasty trail of blood, bits of viscera and gore, and the occasional dismembered body part.
She steps over a dead rat that's been crushed and savaged. Cassandra can't even tell if the fat rodent's head was torn off or was simply flattened to a pulp. The smell almost causes her to retch, but she makes a few gagging sounds instead.
Cassandra takes a few steps back, puts the flashlight and revolver back into the bag, and pulls out a signal flare. She was given 5 of these things to make the most out of.
She understands the basic logic of signal flare + doused vampire = Final Death.
It only becomes a matter of executing it while retaining a pulse (and all her body parts) by the time she's done.
She lights the flare up and hurls it as far away as she can. Its red light casts a wide, bright, and bloody glow in the vicinity, and the crackling sound draws a reaction in the form of a foul screech.
He appears in the corner of the flare's light and comes over to investigate it. Cassandra gasps and takes cover behind the wall. She presses her back against the cold concrete and tries not to hyperventilate. She even holds her breath, hoping that her rapidly thumping heart won't give her away.
A few tense seconds pass, then she hears some snarling. Winston bolts away from the flare and runs in the opposite direction, the sound of his erratic, clumsy footfalls growing quieter.
Cassandra lets out a silent, shaky breath. The flare continues to burn, its smoke trailing up into the air and filling the tunnel with a sickening, crimson hue. And the blood splattered all over the walls and floor is not helping the situation either.
She picks up the bag and starts walking deeper into the darkness, the flare's fading light acting as a sort of guide for her. She has the flashlight, too, but she has to be careful when yanking on that string. Its damned, bloody loud whirring is like a foghorn down 'ere!
Greg's Ghoul takes a sharp turn, and she finds herself standing in front of a mangled corpse. They're too much of an incomprehensible bloodied mess to determine anything about them.
Their entrails are hanging out and have been thoroughly chewed on. They've been thoroughly exsanguinated, too.
Cassandra's kinda squeamish when it comes to blood or guts. She holds back the urge to vomit and hurl all over the gaffe.
Her mouth tastes like rotten eggs, and her stomach is churning. She gets away from what's left of the decomposing corpse and keeps walking. A few savaged rodents help guide her way.
"Fucking 'ell!"
The rats have all been angrily twisted, bitten into at odd angles, and then left in a heap of bloody fur, bone, and exposed, glistening, slimy organs.
Since it's getting a tad darker, Cassandra decides to light another flare. Down to 3 flares now. She also takes the time to pull on the flashlight's string. The beam isn't as strong as she'd like, but it'll do.
The flare's eerie redness bathes the tunnel in a crimson glow. The light dances across the walls and floor as she carries the flare with her. The Ghoul has the gun pointed straight ahead.
Her hand won't stop trembling, but she's slowly starting to get a grip on things. She takes a deep breath and keeps going.
She grimaces when she steps on something gooey and sticky. She gags and elects not to look at whatever (or whoever) she stepped in. It squelches noisily beneath her shoe.
Cassandra gags again and hurries along, her eyes firmly fixed on the path ahead. An unnatural chill runs up the length of her spine. The Ghoul can feel something's terrifying presence here.
She's getting close.
Some bestial snarls and screeches cause Cassandra to jump out of her skin; she nearly loses her grip on the gun and the lit flare. She puts the flare down on the grimy, wet ground and readies herself.
Cassandra's entire body is shaking, her mind screaming at her to run away and get the 'ell out of here. She blinks and surveys her surroundings. Something big and nasty is surely lurking nearby.
Winston's spasming body is hunched over a pair of dying rats. His face is obscured by his matted black hair. He seems to be of average height, a bit on the thinner side of things.
His clothes are torn and bloodstained, revealing patches of skin that have been savagely clawed open and scarred over.
Any hints of a refined gentleman (and Elder) of the Brujah Clan are long gone. Winston's is tragically his Beast in every sense of the word. His eyes are even emptier than usual. They're like 2 black holes of violent madness.
His fangs are chipped and stained red, his claws broken and bloodied. The smell of blood and gore is heavy in the air.
The Wight slowly turns around and looks up at Cassandra. He rises from his squatting position and snarls at who may very well be his next meal. Cassandra points her gun at him and pulls the trigger. The shot goes horribly off-target and hits the wall behind him.
Winston lets out a high-pitched screech and charges at the Ghoul. She screams and points her gun at him. The next shot goes through the side of his torso. He wails like a banshee, but it doesn't stop him.
Cassandra thinks quickly. She holds up her dying flare and waves it at him in a rather erratic manner. Winston lets out an agonized howl and backs away.
Cassandra makes some hasty shooing noises while continuously waving the flare around, hoping it'll ward him off. Winston hisses at her before disappearing back into the darkness in a flash. The Ghoul waits for a few more seconds and then stops waving the flare around.
She's shaken, but she now knows that the harsh and bright lights of a signal flare scare him. But she's running out of time...and resources. Moreover, there's a chance that the Wight in question could still just try to overpower her.
Cass sets the flare down and gives the flashlight a few pulls. Every pull causes her to cringe slightly, but she manages to get a decent enough beam of light out of it. She pulls the revolver's hammer back and walks deeper into the tunnel. Winston's snarling gets closer and closer.
The petrol can in the schoolbag sloshes around with each step she takes. The Ghoul's heart is pounding so fast that her vision's starting to blur and she feels dizzy. Her mouth is dry and she's breathing far too rapidly.
Cass develops a sort of tunnel vision. Her breathing gets a lot more erratic as Winston's growling and desperate howling grows louder and louder.
The sound of footsteps rushing across the floor causes Cassandra to snap her head in the direction of the noise. It's Winston; he's running past her!
"Grr!" Cassandra growls and takes a breather. She decides to light another flare. Down to 2 flares now. The fire crackles loudly, its bright, eerie red light spreading all throughout the grimy tunnel.
She pulls out the petrol can from the schoolbag and shakes it around. She unscrews the cap, and the strong fumes make her cough. She holds back the cough lest Winston hears her. She then screws the cap back on the can.
She needs to douse him with this stuff and then light him up (somehow), or he'll just heal over time and get back up. The gun's simply not gonna cut it. At most, it'd serve to slow him down for but a few seconds. It could also further enrage him, though.
Cassandra doesn't want to know what a Brujah Elder's mad, Frenzying Beast would do to her. She already has a pretty good idea as it is.
For all intents and purposes, Winston's inner Beast has completely taken over. His Humanity is...gone. There's nothing left of the man. The Elder that once was is long gone.
Winston comes over and investigates the flare, his body swaying unnaturally. Cassandra takes this as her opportunity. While he's distracted, she gasps and quickly tosses the contents of the petrol can at him, the thick liquid splashing all over his clothes and onto the ground.
She doesn't throw it all on him, though. She opts to keep a bit in the can in case this hasty plan utterly fails. She needs to have something on hand just in case she needs to fight for her life.
Cassandra tries to light a new flare, but the blasted thing won't go off! The Wight roars and lunges at her, his hands outstretched and his claws ready to tear into her.
She yelps and leaps to the side, dropping the flare in the process. Winston stumbles but quickly gets back up. He's doused in petrol, heaving and snarling like a madman.
The Wight charges at her again, screaming bloody murder all the while. Cassandra pops off a few more shots till the pistol runs dry. She doesn't stop to reload, instead running away as fast as she possibly can.
The empty gun slips out of her shaking hands and lands on the grimy floor with a clatter. She only has 1 flare left. Out of anger, she tosses the dodgy flare at the rapidly approaching Winston. He growls and swipes at it, hissing as it narrowly misses his face.
Cassandra's legs take her as far away from the Wight as possible. She's running away from the dimming flare and back into the darkness. The adrenaline surges throughout her entire body, but she refuses to let the fear overtake her.
Cass gathers her composure and looks at the last signal flare in her dwindling inventory. She readies the petrol can and erratically throws the can's contents all over the Wight.
This time, thankfully, the flare lights up with a satisfying crackle. Cassandra tosses the flare at the bugger. Within mere seconds, the petrol-doused vampire goes up in flames. He screams wildly and runs around until he collapses onto the ground and fades away into dust and ashes.
There is a silent look of relief in his eyes as he goes out. He's finally free.
Cassandra sweats buckets. Not wishing to spend another moment in this damned place, she makes a hasty exit. She yanks on the tool's string and lets the beam light her way back to the door. She makes it there in record time.
She bangs on the door with her sweaty palm. Charlie and the other person guarding the door are genuinely surprised that she made it back. Before they unlock the door and allow her entry, they both ask if Winston has been destroyed.
Cassandra weakly nods, all the color from her face gone. She's pale as a sheet. The door is unlocked and she's told to head upstairs, where the others will indubitably be relieved to know that the nightmare is finally over.
Cassandra nods and mumbles under her breath. She dryly informs Charlie that his old revolver is somewhere in the tunnels. He tells her not to worry about it.
Back upstairs, Harry and Marble Cake are in a tense conversation.
Harry asks, "What if she doesn't make it? Are you ready to go by Gregory's Haven and inform him that his Ghoul's dead?"
The other Punk counters, "Why don't you do it?"
Before 'Arry can reply, Cassandra emerges out of the basement. Everyone else turns to look at her. All the talking comes to a halt.
With trembling lips, the Ghoul of Gregory declares, "It--it's over. I burned him...saw him go--go up in flames meself. He's...gone."
"WAY-HEY!" The entirety of the pub breaks out in a chorus of loud cheers and whoops.
Harry comes over and claps Cassandra on her back, "Oi, oi, oi! I knew that you 'ad it in you, love!"
Cassandra is a little taken aback, "Y--you're not sad about--"
Harry interjects, "We already mourned him, love. A long time ago. C'mon, you look like you could use a drink...or 5. C'mon, c'mon!" He ushers her to the bar, where he intends to get her something to drink.
Once she's seated on a stool, the other Punks surround her and thank her for putting an end to Winston's rampage.
She's shaken (and stirred). She weakly nods her head and musters up the tightest of smiles.
"So...I take it this isn't what you had expected to be doing tonight?" Marble Cake asks while presenting Cassandra with a glass of water.
The Ghoul gratefully takes it, "Y--yeah...not at all what I had in mind." She brings the glass of water up to her lips and takes a few sips. Some of it dribbles down her chin, but she doesn't care.
Marble Cake smiles warmly, "I'm sorry that we had to drag you into this, Cassandra."
Cassandra coughs a few times, "I--I'm just glad--no, not glad. I--I'm relieved that it's over, yeah." She smiles at Marble Cake, who returns the gesture.
Harry puts his hand around Cassandra's back and shakes her a few times, "So, what's your poison, eh?"
"I'm not much of a drinker. Not a huge fan, to be honest."
Marble Cake smirks and asks, "Not even a pint of bitter?"
Cassandra shakes her head, "No...no, not at all."
Harry insists, "OK. Fine. C'mon, luv. Anything you want. It's on the house!"
Cassandra gulps nervously, "Erm...white wine?"
Harry remarks, "Oh, a right posh drink, eh? White wine it is! Oi, pour the lady of the hour a glass of white wine. Fill it to the top!"
Someone in loose CODACORP coveralls does an elaborate bottle spin before pouring Cassandra some of the pub's best white wine.
All of the vampires nearby stare at her keenly. Cassandra feels a tad awkward with all those dead eyes boring into her.
She takes a timid sip of wine and lets out a nervous titter.
"WAY-HEY!" They break out into another cheer. Some clap the Ghoul on the back, congratulate her on a job well done, ruffle her hair, and make their way to back to their respective tables.
Cassandra takes another sip of the white wine.
Marble Cake regards the Toreador Ghoul with a fond smile on her face, "Never took you for a white wine sort."
Cassandra nervously chuckles, "Eh, what can I say...I...err...I like things that taste nice. I once tasted beer when I was a child. Absolutely fucking rancid it was, yeah."
Marble Cake laughs and sincerely thanks the other woman for what she's done for them. She then returns to the backroom. Harry sets the covered painting on the bar counter and takes a step back.
"Consider us even."
Cassandra holds up the glass and nods, "Yeah. S--sure." She takes another sip of the wine.
There's nothing more to be said, really. After she finishes her drink, she grabs the painting and makes her way out of the Horn 'N' Stag pub.
What. A. Night.
Gregory's Ghoul gets in the Lexus and takes a few deep breaths. In and out. In and out. A sense of relief washes over her. The nightmare is finally over. She sets the painting on the passenger seat and turns on the ignition.
On the way back, she bitterly remarks, "All over a--a fucking painting. Was it even worth it, Greg?"
She intends to have words with her Domitor. Traffic on the way back isn't as bad. The rain keeps on pouring and pouring and pouring. She puts the wipers on full speed. Thankfully, she got them replaced 5 months back.
It cost a bit of quid, but it's better than nothing. She gets back home without any major problems. She clutches the painting tightly and makes her way to the Toreador's Haven.
Gregory is idly sitting on 1 of the sofas. He's reading his copy of the Guardian. He looks up from his newspaper and goes to answer the door.
He lets her in with a neutral expression on his face. Cassandra's face is still a little pale. She has that rather intense look in her hazel eyes again.
She rudely shoves the painting into him, "'ere! It's done. Do you have any idea what I had to do to--"
"How is Winston? Did he say anything? Was the price--"
Cassandra explodes, "HE'S FUCKING ASHES! HE--HE FRENZIED AND TURNED INTO A WIGHT! YEAH, A PROPER WIGHT!"
Gregory doesn't flinch at the loudness of his Ghoul's voice. He just calmly sets the painting aside, "Oh."
"Is that all you 'ave to say?! Do you know what I had to do to get you that painting! I HAD TO DESTROY HIM! I HAD TO BURN HIM TO ASHES! I nearly lost me life down there tonight! And all you can say is 'oh'?!"
"What do you expect me to say, Cassandra? You did what you must. You, of all people, are no stranger to our world...this is how it works."
Cassandra's eyes well up, "I--I'm scared, Greg. Harry claimed that you bear some responsibility for Winston turning into a Wight--"
The Aesthete cuts her off, "That's preposterous. I simply asked the Brujah Elder if he was able to procure this artwork for me and on behalf of a client. That's all there was to it. Nothing more. Nothing less."
"What exactly happened?" Cassandra meekly prods.
Gregory sighs heavily, "I am under a lot of pressure. Like I said: there is a reason why I have kept you away from certain things for many years on end. The fact that I am now willing to share this with you at all is a sign of trust. It is also a sign of how dire things has become."
"Dire for who?"
Greg shrugs, "It's far more complicated than that, I'm afraid. Please, try and trust me. If or--or when it's all over, I'll explain everything. Until then, I'm going to need you to simply do as I ask."
Cassandra huffs, "Fine. But we're not done having this conversation, yeah? Am I going to be expected to destroy MORE Wights on your behalf?"
Gregory dryly answers, "I shouldn't hope so."
The Ghoul crosses her arms and leans against the wall, "What else am I supposed to do, hmm? Where--where should I head off to next?"
Gregory raises his chin, "Birmingham. I owe the Assamites a favor. I'd imagine that they'll want to call it in soon. Needless to say, I intend to have you do it for me. If you need extra money for petrol--"
Cassandra groans, "Why can't you just do it yourself?"
"I have my reasons. And even you, Cassandra, know that I don't appreciate being questioned in such a derisive manner."
Cass sighs harshly, "Fine! But--but this is just a bit wrong, yeah? I--I mean--"
She stammers and wildly gesticulates, "It's just wrong, innit? All of this. I--I mean, what you're doing is wrong, Greg! People shouldn't be getting hurt for art! 3 people died thanks to Winston!"
Gregory flatly counters, "You're going to stand there and lecture me about right and wrong? You, of all--"
Cassandra raises a finger and wags it at her Domitor, "No, no! Don't you dare bring that up! That's none of your business! We are talking about what YOU'VE indirectly done to these people!"
Gregory folds his arms and lowers his head. He takes a moment to consider the Ghoul's words.
He eventually says, "We'll talk more tomorrow evening. Pack your things- I don't know how long you'll be staying in Birmingham."
The Aesthete turns away from his Ghoul and makes his way back to the sitting room. Cassandra wants to say so much more. She wants to SCREAM at her Domitor, but the Bond of Blood kicks in.
She slumps her shoulders and makes her way back to her cottage. She needs to pack. Birmingham isn't that far off from Gloughchester; it's about 5 hours via car and half that time via train.
Cass would've preferred to take the train, but Gregory (for whatever reason) is insistent on her driving all the way over. After about an hour, Cassandra packs a suitcase and sets it aside.
Gregory removes the sheet from the painting and inspects it with a narrowed eye. He strokes his chin and takes on a very contemplative look.
He mumbles to himself, "The artist defaced it out of spite. Hm. Maybe that's what serves to increase its value. Hmph..." He trails off and puts the painting away in a safe, secure place.
Cassandra spends the rest of her night in bed. She dresses down to a loose sleeping vest and her knickers. She can't sleep just yet. The thought of what had occurred earlier in the night replays in her mind's eye over and over and over again.
She thinks about Harry and Marble Cake, too. Odd bunch, those 2. They seemed like decent enough people, though, just trying to eke out something resembling an existence in this dark, dark world.
Cassandra sighs. Her heart's beating fast, and she feels a wee bit dizzy. She grabs her phone and begins to dial a number.
Amy is sleeping next to her spouse when the phone rings. She grumbles and fumbles around in the dark for her mobile phone. She winces when she sees the name on the phone's screen.
"Hello?" Amy groggily asks while rubbing the sleep out of her eyes. She stands up and makes her way outside. The sounds of a sliding door opening and closing are heard.
Cassandra lets out a wistful sigh, "Hiya."
Amy hisses, "Are you mad? Calling me in the middle of the night?! What's gotten into you? What do you want now?" Her voice is low and slightly menacing. She has no patience for the Ghoul's antics.
Cassandra tears up a bit, "I--I just wanted to hear your voice. It's been a...tense night for me."
Amy's expression softens, "Oh. Work? Again?"
"Something like that, yeah. I can't go into specifics. Err...NDAs. But I just wanted to call you and hear your voice."
"I guess I'll have to find another way to cajole it out of you, then. You really have shit timing, you know that, right?"
"What can I say? I--I just want to be with you right now. I need to hear your voice. I want to...feel you. I want to hold you. I want to do a lot more than that, too."
Amy softly exhales, "Oh, Cassie. You really can get quite clingy, you know?"
She snickers, "But it's alright, I suppose. I'll have a few naughty dreams about you tonight, don't you worry."
Cassandra blushes, "That's...I suppose I'll 'ave a bit of naughty dreams, too, eh?"
Amy lets out a laugh, "You're adorable, Cassie. But please, try not to call me at odd hours. I...I don't want her getting any more suspicious than she already is."
Cassie's heart sinks, "I'm sorry. D--do you ever feel like what we're doing is...wrong?"
Amy's face scrunches up, "What?! What spurred this line of questioning on?"
"It's--it's nothing. You know what? Never mind that I asked. But...ah, shit...d--doesn't it feel wrong to you sometimes?"
Amy sighs, "The middle of the night is hardly the time to be having such a heavy conversation, don't you think? I'm sleepy, Cass. I have work tomorrow. And the crumbling walls don't exactly inspire feelings of confidence."
Cassandra winces a little, "I'm sorry, I'm sorry. I'm going to Birmingham tomorrow."
"Oh, lucky you. Work or pleasure, eh?"
"I--I don't know anymore."
"Well, good luck, love. And please, don't call- or text- me at unusual hours. Please. I--I don't want to arouse her--"
Amy stops and stares at the ring on her finger. She gulps and goes on, "I don't her f--finding out about...this. About us. Do you understand?"
Cassandra feels her eyes well up, "I--I do. Yeah."
Amy hears some wiggling on her bed. She stammers, "OK, OK. I have to go now. Mwah, goodnight. Sleep tight, don't let the bugs bite. Bye." She hastily hangs up before Cassandra can respond.
Cassandra sniffles and wipes a few tears away from her eyes. She presses her lips into a thin line and sets her phone aside She curls up in bed and tries to go to sleep.
The screeching of the Wight on flames replays in her head over and over again. It's going to be a long night. In time, however, she dozes off. Her snoring is loud and grating. She's drooling a bit, too. But at least she's getting some rest.
Act 3: Banu Haqim
"I have boiled it down to 3 things: Justice, Diablerie, and Shawarmas." -From the personal diaries of Gregory | Entry: On the Banu Haqim.
A new day dawns. The sun reigns supreme in the sky. Cassandra wakes up from her sleep. Gregory, meanwhile, is deep in the throes of his day-long slumber.
Cassandra stretches and yawns loudly. She still feels a bit tired and worn out, but she'll make do.
Up in his bedroom, Gregory is helplessly paralyzed while the 5-eyed Aapilu menacingly observes him up-close. It sniffs at him and makes that god-awful clicking sound again.
Gregory can only watch on. His entire Unliving body is frozen in fear. His lifeless blue eyes are locked onto the creature's many, many eyes. The one in the middle- the one belonging to his late daughter- catches his attention the most.
The mouthless being of the Vitae turns cocks its head at an unnatural angle. The Aapilu's slimy hand reaches out and caresses the Toreador's face.
Gregory's skin crawls. The Aapilu vanishes away after a few moments. Gregory's body goes slack and he falls into the cold pull of the day-sleep.
While her Domitor rests his immortal body, Cassandra eats some cereal and scrolls through various websites on her laptop. She looks up "Birmingham" in the search bar and finds out more about where she has to go this evening.
She checks the weather forecast and the traffic reports, too. The same old, same old. The default English setting of gray and gloomy. At least the traffic's not bound to be that bad.
After having a glass of tea, she gets dressed and heads out for a short walk across her Domitor's property. Oh, yes, most of the surrounding land now belongs to Mr. Gregory.
It's not just the fancy house (and the cottage).
She appreciatively takes in her surroundings. Tall trees, singing birds, buzzing bees, and fresh air. She deeply breathes it all in. The sun shines on her fair skin and makes her feel warm.
It's a good enough feeling. And not a day goes by when she isn't thankful for having a place that can (sort of) be called her own. She walks around the property for a little bit more until she decides to stroll by her Domitor's Haven.
She stares at the architecture from outside and lets out a small chuckle. When Gregory told her how much he bought this entire place for, she had to laugh out in sheer disbelief. It was simply too ridiculous not to!
So, how much was it all? The house, the cottage, and the Lexus, too?
£1.50
Mm-hmm. That's it. Not £150,000. Not £150,000,000. The Toreador who used to reside here offered everything to Greg for £1. The extra 50 pence was just because.
And because Greg also felt a tad guilty for getting it all for such an unbelievable (and quite frankly, unbeatable) price!
But why? Why did the prior owner of all of this property (and the surrounding land) decide to sell it to Gregory for such a small amount?
He was...bored, as certain members of the Clan of the Rose are oft wont to get. The former owner sought to fill the ever-expanding void in his long Unlife with a great many things.
He had claimed to have learnt of the path to Golconda from a random Salubri wandering the streets of Bristol. That person claimed that the path to Golconda was to be found high up in the Himalayas.
Asceticism was said to be the method that lit the path to Golconda. So, the dude who used to own this decided to part with his many worldly possessions- clothing, diamonds, watches, the whole shebang.
He even shaved his luscious blonde hair off.
Far be it from Gregory to yuck somebody else's yum. He paid for it all in cash...and coin, of course. The deal was finalized with a firm handshake and warm smile. And then blud left to find his "path to Golconda".
Or to have his ass frozen off, because fire isn't the only major threat to vampires. Uber-cold temperatures could very well lead to a messy Final Death.
And that's pretty much how Gregory and his Ghoul got 2 places, a car (secondhand, but pretty decent), and the surrounding land. For just £1.50 (pound shop prices!). The previous owner has not been heard of ever since he left to embark on his Golconda quest.
His Ghouls were given off to other Toreadors- Gregory had no intention of taking any more. Some contractors were hired to spruce the place up a wee bit- nothing too extravagant, just some general renovations and whatnot. And then, finally, the moving-in began in great earnest!
After reminiscing for a little while, Cassandra goes back to her cozy cottage. She was initially quite shocked by all of it, but the seriousness on her Domitor's face told her that it was all completely true. And so, it was.
For her part, she's absolutely chuffed that she doesn't 'ave to pay rent anymore. Searching for flats in a major metropolis like London was a right pain in the arse. And some landlords were proper creeps- asking for sex in lieu of outrageous amounts of dosh!
Utter perverts, that sick lot. If she didn't have Gregory agreeing to back her up, she'd be utterly powerless against some of the things that those creeps asked of her. And she had to make sure to take a good look around their places before handing over the money.
Mold, asbestos, all that shite.
Cassandra checks her bags and ensures that her suitcase is still packed. After that, she takes a stroll to the local pound shop- she wants to see if there are any deals on for today.
She grabs a few bottles of shower gel that were B1G1 (buy 1, get 1) free. Way-hey! 1 bottle for her and 1 bottle for Gregory, because even walking corpses need to smell good. She also gets 2 copies of the Guardian. They both like holding a physical copy in their hands.
They can both be a little old-fashioned in certain ways. But that's neither here nor there. Cassandra heads back home and waits for Gregory to rise.
And as surely as the sun sets, the Kindred in this corner of the world begin to wake up from their day-long slumbering. Gregory rises at the same time as usual. His day-sleep was disturbed. His eyes are bloodshot and his appearance is haggard. He has a severe expression on his face, too.
Regardless, he pulls himself out of bed with a strained groan and gets dressed. He goes downstairs and checks on the painting.
Cassandra can be seen waving through the glass doors that lead to his living room. Gregory sighs and dryly waves back at her. He gestures for her to come inside. The Ghoul obliges and enters the house.
"Hiya, Greg!" she eagerly greets, "Sleep well?"
Gregory plainly nods, "Hm. Are you ready for your Birmingham trip? All your bags are packed? Is--is there enough petrol in the Lexus?" His voice is low and gruff. He still sounds half-asleep, really.
Cassandra plops herself down on 1 of the sofas and nods, "Yeah, all set! Greg?"
"Hmm?"
"Do you...ever miss London? Do you miss anything about it? Anything at all?"
"No. You?"
"Cor blimey, no! But I do miss 1 thing, though."
"Oh?"
Cassandra nods and asks, "Remember the garden of that lady named err...Mary Marks, yeah? Very unusual woman, she was. Err...do you remember what she used to grow there? The flowers were rather pretty, weren't they?"
"No." Like always, his voice is flat and emotionless.
Cassandra's expression falls and she sighs, "O--oh, alright. So...erm...Banu Haqim in Birmingham, eh?"
The Toreador sits himself down from across his Ghoul. He intertwines his fingers and nods, "Yes. They are, however, not your run-of-the-mill Assamites, though. Many of them in that Domain are of the Blade of Haqim. I shan't waste your time with a history lesson on them, but I do hope you take the time to learn more about them."
Cassandra replies, "Ooh. Sounds exciting. I--I hope that I won't be thrown into a messy situation like...last night, yeah? I won't, right, Greg?"
Gregory mutters, "I'd hope not. Nevertheless, I owe these Assamites a favor. And rather than wait for them to call it in, I intend to have you do it for me. I have a feeling that they'll want your help in some or the other matter which is of great import to them. You must acquiesce, of course."
"Sure. Sure. Who should I expect to meet with?"
"The Mubarak brothers- Salim and Malik. Palestinian Assamites. And also, co-founders of the Blade. Genial sorts, the both of them. I trust that you'll be able to handle them without any...problems."
"Oh, yeah. Yeah. Sounds interesting. How come you've never, ever told me about any of this before, hmm? I'm learning a--a lot of things these days. Did you think I wasn't ready enough for it before?"
Gregory plainly tells her, "I had- and still do have- my reasons for withholding certain bits of information from you. But all of that is going to change soon enough, I assure you. This world of ours can get rather...messy."
Cassandra scoffs, "You're telling me! I know how messy it can get! So, err...how did these brothers and you meet?"
Gregory explains, "When it comes to matters of stealth and discretion, the Banu Haqim Clan are par excellence. Far be it from to stereotype, of course. Nevertheless, their expertise in such matters proved to be useful in my...dealings."
Cassandra's face scrunches up in confusion, "Err...what do you mean by that?"
Gregory sighs, "Simply put, they utilized the powers of their Obfuscate Discipline to...pardon the crudeness, nick certain priceless items and a few valuable artifacts that certain clients of mine have been demanding for quite a while now. I'm just the middleman in all of this, Cassandra, don't you worry. You yourself won't be held responsible for anything."
"Cor blimey...blagging stuff?!"
Gregory scratches the back of his head, "Hmph, yes. Consider it borrowing if it makes you feel any better--"
"And the Banu Haqim just went along with it?"
"Favors are the currency of our kind. But the Assamites except other forms of payment- Blood being the primary one. They did what they did, and in return, I spilled some of my Vitae into a container. What they do with it is their prerogative; those sorts of things never truly interested me, anyway."
Cassandra's eyebrows shoot up, "Wow. I--I won't be expected to make a blood donation, would I?"
"No. You better get moving- you have a long journey ahead of you. Here's the address where you're going to meet up with the Assamites. As always, be on your best behavior. Stay vigilant. You'll be representing me, so I want you to put your best foot forward. Got it?"
Cassandra gulps and nods, "I will. I--I will, yeah. And I'm fully authorized to speak, act, and--and negotiate on your behalf, right?"
Gregory nods, "Yes. That is correct. Do you still remember all of your learning, Cassandra?"
"Of course I do!"
"Good. Never forget it. In the coming nights, you're going to need to tap into your vast reserves of insight and knowledge. Now, off you go. There's a reason I'm not sending you via train. I know that it's a lot faster, but the trip is far safer when taken by car."
The Ghoul shrugs, "If you say so. Do I want to ask how you know this?"
Gregory stammers, "Just...trust me on this, OK? Do you require additional spending money? You can take my debit card if you'd like- you know the PIN--"
Cassandra holds her hand out and politely declines, "Oh, no. I'm fine. T--thanks. I--I have me own cash for this trip, don't you worry."
Gregory reminds her, "I've already booked you hotel room- I do hope that it is to your satisfaction. If the stay goes on for longer than expected, then worry not, for I shall cover the costs."
"A--are you sure? I mean, I 'ave dosh, y'know? I'll take care of it, Greg."
"I insist. You're representing me, acting and speaking on my behalf. It's the least which I can do as your Domitor, Cassandra."
Cassie smiles, "Well, thanks. I promise not to take advantage, alright?"
"Hmm. And for what it's worth, I am sorry about what you had to face last night. The suddenness of it all must've come as a great shock to you."
Cassandra nods and her expression becomes glum, "Yeah. It was proper scary, I tell you that. I--I thought that I was done for..." She trails off and her lips begin to quiver.
She sniffles and continues, "But I didn't give up. I, somehow, did what had to be done. But I feel a bit guilty, yeah? Is what I did murder, Greg?"
Greg shakes his head, "Winston was a Wight- too far gone to be saved. And to have him go on any longer would've been nothing short of cruel. You were merely defending yourself."
Cassandra points at her Domitor, "You tell that posh client of yours that people died for their painting!"
The Aesthete sighs heavily, "I'll...figure out a way to bring it up in our conversation. Now, is there anything else on your mind? The sooner you leave, the faster you'll get to Birmingham. As always, do not call or text me unless it is absolutely urgent."
Cassandra musters up a smile, "Don't 'ave to tell me twice. And we really should get our SIMs changed sometime soon, yeah?"
"Hm. Yes. Goodbye, Cassandra. Drive safely."
She gets the hint loud and clear. She takes a moment to go over a few more of the finer things with Gregory before heading out the door. She loads her luggage into the back seat of the Lexus and then sits down behind the steering wheel.
A light rain pours down upon her car as she makes her way to the main road.
Cassandra is dressed in a purple silk shirt with a few of the top buttons undone. A pair of dark capri pants hugs her legs. Her flats are plain and black and she's wearing some simple stud earrings. The outfit is very classy, and the colors go well enough with her fair skin.
The Toreador Ghoul also has a touch of makeup on. Nothing too extravagant- just a dash of blush, mascara, and pound shop lip gloss.
Birmingham, here she comes!
During the long drive, she inserts a CD of her favorite pop singer into the car's stereo system. She squeals in delight when singing starts.
A man announces, "Soho Starlet! Nicola Green!"
Cassandra lets out another delightful squeal.
Soho Starlet's voice fills the car. Cass bops her head to the smooth, textured beats. She happily sings along to her favorite bits of the song. Halfway through the drive, she shuts off the music and pulls her Blackberry phone out of the cup holder.
She dials Amy's number and waits for an answer.
"Hello, sweetie," Amy's voice comes on the other end.
Cassandra replies, "Hiya! Back from work?"
"In the bus right now. You in Birmingham yet?"
"Nah. Still on the road. I think I'll skip the M40 and take the M2 motorway to get there faster. I got some spare change for the toll booths."
"Well, good for you, then."
"So, how are...things?"
Amy wiggles on the seat of the bus and watches the rain pelting down outside the window, "Oh, y'know...the usual song-and-dance routine of an overworked and woefully underpaid school admin. Add to that the crumbling walls and worried pupils. Oh, it's all just a smashing, wonderful time."
Cassandra's expression softens, "I'm sorry to hear that. I--I wish I could be there to help you out with all of it..."
"I'll be needing some stress relief sometime soon..."
"Ooh, now you're speaking me language!"
Amy smirks and lowers her voice to a seductive purr, "Oh, I've been feeling...very stressed out lately. And I think that you can help me out with it."
Cassandra feels a twitch of excitement in her nether regions. She licks her lips and stammers, "Oh--oh, really?"
"Hmm. Any idea when you're getting back?"
"Oh, err...it may take a while, yeah. Well, why can't you come over to Birmingham and--and, you know..."
Amy clicks her tongue and shakes her head, "No. Absolutely not. And risk her finding out? No, Cass. I'm sorry, but I can't do that."
Cassandra sighs in disappointment, "I know, I know. But can you blame me for wanting to see you naked again, yeah? Just thinking about it is--is making me all hot and bothered! I--I need a release!"
Amy shushes her, "Quiet! I--I need it, too. Text me when you get to the city of a 1001 different trades, OK?"
Cassandra chuckles, "Will do! I...I'm going to miss you."
Amy takes a moment to respond, "I'm going to miss you, too. Come back to Gloughchester soon, alright? I don't want to wait for too long..."
"I--I'll try me best, Amy."
"Hmm. Bye."
"Yeah."
The call ends there. Cassandra takes a few deep breaths. She tightens her grip around the steering wheel. She blinks the tears away from her eyes and swallows hard. She wipes her face clean and then continues with her journey.
Traffic is quite dense in certain areas. And the rain gets worse. And then it gets better for a short while. And then worse again. Good thing she got them window wipers changed!
Eventually, Cassandra finally gets to the city of Birmingham.
The Domain of Birmingham. Since she isn't a Kindred, she's not required to present herself to the Prince of this fine city. She's just here to carry out the will of her Domitor, keep her head low, and then go back home to familiar Gloughchester.
After such a long drive, she's feeling rather peckish. There's a KFC that's opened at this time. She pulls her car into the parking lot. She pats herself down and checks her makeup before heading in.
As far as KFCs go, it's fairly average- sleek, minimalistic, and not too big or too small. A few people are inside already- office types, judging by their tight, formal attire.
Many baggy-eyed lads are chomping down on their burgers. And there are some young couples from the nearby unis who are enjoying their meal in peace and quiet.
Cassandra strokes her chin and stares at the overhead menu. A few of the food items look mighty good to her! Eventually, she opts to go for a zinger burger with fries, and a cold can of Pepsi.
Simple and to the point. For tomorrow's lunch, she'll get something different. Right now, she just wants to get done with her (late) dinner and meet up with the Assamites.
Cassandra's burger comes with some complimentary sauce that's orange in color. It has a creamy texture, but the taste is quite...spicy.
Cassandra, however, can very much handle spicy food. She shrugs and takes a big ol' bite out of her burger. The sauce in question goes rather well with the burger and fries!
She notices that some of the employees in this establishment are dragging their feet and yawning like mad. A few of them look half-asleep, as well. A look of sympathy washes over the Ghoul. She has an inkling of what it's like to have to work long shifts for minimum wage.
Not fun at all, yeah.
While eating her meal, the Ghoul spots the logo of Elbit Systems on someone's bulky jacket. She grimaces and shakes her head, very much aware of what that foul Israeli company is, what it does, and what it stands for.
She quickly wolfs down the remainder of her scran, wipes her mouth with a few tissue papers, and scurries away from the fast-food restaurant. Before meeting up with these Assamites, she swings by the hotel to check in.
It's quite a grand place located somewhere in the city's center. With a smile on her face, she greets the receptionist and shows him her ID. Once he's done with his work, he gives Cassandra her room key.
She's got a medium-sized 5th floor suite with a decent enough view of the Domain's skyline. And a lovely bed, too! After freshening up, Cassandra grabs her handbag and heads out into the dark and rainy night.
