Gregory's Ghoul drives her Lexus to a nearby underground car park. She locks up and walks to the building where she's supposed to be meeting the Assamites.
It looks like a community center of some kind. She spots a few kids laughing and running about- at this time of the night, how curious! She also spots a woman in her 40s wearing a headscarf. She politely waves and the woman smiles back at her.
Cassandra enters the main building and looks around for a bit. Brown carpeting, plain walls, and an old ceiling fan spinning round and round. The concrete buildings around her look the same, more or less.
She spots a large fella with a beard standing guard at the door next to the main reception area. The kids that she saw earlier come rushing inside, sweating and giggling. They squeeze past the man at the door and continue whatever it is that they're doing.
The man nods over to Cass and asks her, "Can I help you with something, miss?"
"I'm...err...looking for the Mubarak brothers. You wouldn't happen to know where they are, yeah?"
"1 moment, please." The man turns on his heel and goes deeper into the building.
Cassandra leans against the wall and patiently waits. She pulls out her Blackberry and shoots a text to Amy: "Reached. Sry 4 not texting sooner. Lovely looking city. Want me 2 get anyting for you?? Trinkets, knick-knacks, snacks?"
No response is received. A few minutes later, the man returns and says, "This way, please."
Cassandra shoves the phone into her handbag and follows after him. The man opens a door at the end of the hallway.
It's dimly lit by some orange-tinted light bulbs. There's a desk with a simple computer on it. There's a map of the city on the wall behind it. There are a lot of red pins in the map- probably important spots for the Assamites to keep an eye on.
A dark-skinned man who looks to be in his late-30s sits at the desk. He has a short beard, brown eyes, a diamond-shaped face, and a scarred nose. He has a very prominent widow's peak. The rest of his hair is gelled back. He's wearing a blue jacket with a slightly torn collar and some matching pants.
He politely greets her, "As-salamu alaykum (AR: Peace be upon you)."
Growing up, Cassandra had a few Arabic-speaking friends at the comprehensive school where she studied. So, she understands what he just said.
She stammers a little when returning the greeting, "Err...Wa alaykumu s-salam (AR: And upon you be peace). Did--did I get it right?"
"It is acceptable. What can I do for you on this fine night?" He speaks with a slight accent.
"Err...yeah, hiya. I'm looking for Malik or Salim Mubarak. Gregory--"
The Assamite introduces himself, "I am Salim Mubarak. How do you know Gregory?"
Cassandra sits up straight, "I'm his Ghoul, yeah. Cassandra's me name. I'm here on his behalf because he owes you lot some kind of favor."
Salim's expression doesn't change at all, "That is true. Is there a reason why he's not here with you? Were you sent forward to herald his arrival?"
"Nah. He's not coming. I've been fully authorized to act, speak, and negotiate on his behalf, yeah?"
Salim steeples his fingers together and exhales softly, "Oh. I see. He sent his Ghoul to do his work for him..."
He wishes to say more but elects not to. Instead, he asks her, "So, you are here on his behalf, then? Hmm?"
"Yeah. I'm ready to help you out with whatever it is that you need helping with."
Salim leans back in his swiveling office chair and explains, "This complicates matters. Oddly enough, it might also work in our benefit. I have been receiving word from certain...friends in Gloughchester. They speak of a Ghoul who brought Final Death upon an Elder Wight of the Learned Clan."
He leans forward and eyes her carefully, "You wouldn't happen to know anything about this, would you?"
Cassandra rubs the base of her neck, "I did what I had to do. Winston was his name. He'd already killed 3 innocent people in his state! I don't regret doing what I did...but I don't exactly feel good about it, either."
"Interesting. So, you are a capable Ghoul, then?"
"I suppose so."
Salim nods over to the big man and waves him off. The man in question takes the hint and goes away. The door behind them clicks shut.
Salim gets up from his chair and walks over to the map of the city. He puts his hand behind his back and stares at it intently.
He tells Cassandra, "If you are truly acting on behalf of your Domitor, then I shall explain the task at hand. Unlike what you did to that Rebel, this one will require...subtlety. Maybe you are the right person for this job. Gregory would've been far too much of a liability in this situation."
He paces a tight circle and explains, "There is an infiltrator among us. We know nothing of their identity beyond the fact that they have somehow managed to penetrate our defenses and blend in with us. Their motives are far from mysterious to us."
Cassandra folds 1 leg over the other, "Oh. Go on. I'm all ears."
"Before we proceed, I strongly feel that context must be provided. What do you know of the Blade of Haqim?"
Cass shakes her head and shrugs, "Nothing."
Salim adjusts a pin on the map and begins, "We are a faction of the Banu Haqim Clan, founded shortly after the 1948 Nakba. Do you know what the Nakba is, Cassandra?"
She correctly answers, "The ethnic cleansing of the Palestinian people...and the founding of Israel. Y'know, the usual crimes and atrocities against humanity."
Salim is visibly impressed with her knowledge and nods in approval, "That is correct. Perhaps you will prove to be the right one for this sensitive task. Anyways, I myself- along with my brother, Dr. George Fateh, Fatima Ibn Rashid, and many others- am a founder of the Blade of Haqim."
"What brought you to Birmingham? If--if you don't mind me asking. I hope that it's not too insensitive of a question, yeah?"
Salim softly smiles, "Far from it. The Zionists were- and still are- ruthless savages. We had no other choice but to flee, to run. Our home has been desecrated for decades now. Many of us were slaughtered by the emerging Jewish State. A great number of us managed to escape."
He tells her, "Me and my brother came to the UK after wandering around for so long. Fatima and Dr. George went to Magnolia City, USA. Dr. George stayed in America while Fatima soon went off to France- she's an Elder, so the Beckoning continuously takes its toll on her. Last I heard, she's in Turkey. But it's hard to send and receive letters. They take weeks to arrive, if they do arrive at all."
Cassandra's interest is piqued. She gestures for him to continue.
The Assamite resumes, some pain and heaviness lacing his voice, "When we escaped our homeland, we were not in much of a position to fight back. Even now, we fight an uphill battle against those who mean ill. The Assamites- as you may or may not know- are very dedicated to the pursuit of justice."
"Yeah, I know. I got a fair idea of your Clan's Banes and Compulsions. Learned all about them, I did."
Salim chuckles softly, "How very intriguing. A learned Ghoul. Fascinating, truly. Let us resume. After we came here, the decades flew by. We helplessly watched as the Zionists...annihilated everything that we held dear. And they continue to do so to this night."
Cassandra nods, "Yeah. All that fighting in Gaza. Horrid business. It's in the news, too."
(This is long before the genocide in Gaza. What she's referring to is Israel's barbaric policy of routinely "mowing the lawn". Every time that they feel like it, the Israelis just kill and maim and destroy and loot and pillage and rape. Their murderous assaults always receive some token condemnation from the UN or from other countries and Orgs, but never enough to actually stop the massacres.)
Salim lowers his head, a tear of blood rolling down his cheek, "And they receive the full backing of the usual suspects. The UK, USA, France, Spain, Germany, India, even Australia. Tch. A curse upon all of them and their households."
He composes himself and continues, "Nevertheless, we do what we can to fight against the Zionists. It is not an easy battle, Cassandra. A certain solidarity organization that we are associated with has been suffering heavy losses lately. And we have reason to believe that this infiltrator bears the responsibility for it."
"I see. What kind of an organization is it?"
Salim shrugs, "It mostly consists of students, activists, lawyers, and those who are interested in these important issues. Some of them are Kindred, some are Ghouls like yourself. But the vast majority are mortals. We don't really have a name for the organization."
"Do you meet up regularly or is it all secret-like?"
"Yes, we do meet. In fact, we're holding 1 of those meetings right now. Not here, mind you. I don't know if you've noticed, but there has been an increased police presence around these parts in the last few weeks. As such, we need to be very cautious at all times."
Cassandra concurs, "Yeah. Can't be too certain these days, eh? It's not just the bent coppers that you have to watch out for."
Salim chuckles, "Indeed. This infiltrator has caused damage to our organization. Many of our Ghouls have been turning up dead- usually shot in the back of the head in their own flats or violently beaten to death in back alleys. The police are quick to cover up the crimes."
"Cor blimey! And the mortal members?"
"They...are fine. For now. But we do not know for how long that'll be. Somebody with intimate knowledge of the inner workings of Kindred Society is targeting us. They want to move our Ghouls out of the way so they can get to us."
Cassandra strokes her chin, "I see. Yeah, makes sense. How many have you lost so far?"
Salim grimly answers, "At least 12. The last one was found in her house with her throat slit."
Cassandra shudders and looks away, her face scrunching up in horror, "Gruesome. Sorry to hear that."
Salim sniffs, "Hmm. At least we were able to prevent an autopsy from being performed. The results would've indicated that she was far older than she actually was. It was a good thing that our contacts were quick to act. It pays to know people in the morgues."
He faintly chuckles at that before his expression becomes serious once more, "Anyhow, you will be attending future meetings and observing everybody there. I strongly suspect that somebody from amongst the mortals is our infiltrator."
"OK, OK. So, what do I do, then? Do I 'ave to sign up for some membership? Is there a fee--"
The Assamite waves his hand, "Nothing of the sort, don't worry. I'll have somebody vouch for you, Cassandra. Worry not. There's a meeting scheduled for tomorrow morning. 11:00 AM sharp. Integrate yourself with them. Make connections. Get to know them. Learn what you can about their backgrounds."
Cassandra presses her lips into a thin line and nods slowly.
Salim instructs her, "Be discreet with your observations. The infiltrator must not become aware of your intentions. Otherwise, it could spell catastrophes for all of us."
"How am I supposed to identify the infiltrator if I don't even know what they look like?"
Salim looks ahead, "We will leave that to your discretion. Your Domitor owes us a big favor. But I am grateful that he sent you in his stead. No offense, but Gregory can be rather...difficult to understand at times. And he is far too...uptight. What is it that you Brits say?"
He snaps his fingers a few times and says, "Ah, yes. A stiff upper lip. Haha! Even till now, I find your...take on the English language to be most interesting. And a little amusing. So many expressions that me and Malik have yet to pick up on."
Cassandra smiles a little, "Yeah, it's quite something, alright. How--how have you found it here in the UK after all these decades? Do you...feel welcome and--and accepted by us Brits?"
The Assamite's gaze softens as he answers, "By the common people, yes. By your dreadful governments, no. And that goes for both Tories and Labor. Don't even get me started on the Greens and Lib-Dems. Heh."
Cassandra sincerely tells him, "I'm sorry about what I'm sure you've had to experience throughout all those years. The err...the racist abuse, the casual violence, and the discrimination. I really am."
Salim equably says, "You yourself are not at fault for such things. Governments the world over instigate people against each other while those of even greater power and wealth go about looting their own societies until there is nothing left. It is a foul trick as old as time itself. Divide et impera. Divide and rule."
"Yeah, absolutely. It's fucking mental, too! Err...do you mind if I ask an uncomfortable question?"
Salim sits down and gives her his full attention, "By all means."
Cassandra leans forward and asks him, "Have you ever been tempted by the call of Diablerie? I--I know what the Banu Haqim are about and all, but--"
Salim's smile is rather tight, "That is quite a personal question, Cassandra. It is something that the local Camarilla look down upon, yes. But we...we...do what we must to control ourselves in that regard."
"Uh...OK. Another question, if I may."
Salim motions for her to continue.
Cassandra continues, "What are you gonna do with the infiltrator once they've been caught? I--I mean, once I'm sure about who it is and all."
Without skipping a beat, the Assassin tells the Ghoul, "We will Judge them."
Cassandra's eyebrows go up and she quickly nods, "Gotcha. That's the end of my questions, then. Tomorrow morning, 11 AM sharp. Consider me present."
Before she leaves, the 2 of them go over a few things. Salim runs a few convincing cover stories by Cassandra- they eventually settle an office worker at some big-name company who has always been interested in the critical issues of the epoch- and Palestine is most certainly 1 of them!
He tells her that he'll have everything ready for tomorrow. His Ghouls will help get her setup, but they themselves are in hiding and (understandably) quite afraid due to all of the recent killings. So, their support will be fairly limited.
And Salim can only aid her at nights, but that also carries with it certain risks. In fact, the Assamites have to be very cautious around here for numerous reasons. The increased police presence isn't helping things, either.
While Cassandra makes her way to the door, Salim calls out to her, "Oh. And 1 more thing, Cassandra."
She turns around and politely waits.
Salim lifts his chin slightly and reminds her, "Always thank the bus driver. Us Banu Haqim are very particular about that here in Birmingham. Goodbye, Cassandra. And may peace and justice go with you."
"You too. Cheers! And thanks for...err...trusting me with all of this, yeah? I won't let you down. I promise! I--I genuinely care about these issues, y'know? It's not just a random assignment for me."
The Assamite presses his lips into a thin smile and nods, "It is relieving to hear that. I hope that you will succeed in your task, Cassandra. I wouldn't want to be put in the position of having to inform your Domitor that something has happened to you..."
Cassandra swallows nervously and mutters, "Yeah. I--I'll manage, don't worry."
The Ghoul opens the door and leaves the room, shutting it behind her.
Salim clasps his cold hands together and lets out a sigh, "Only time can reliably tell if she is the right person for this job...or if she is just somebody who will get herself killed..."
He stops himself, "No! I--I mustn't think in such ways. She is the best hope that we have of discovering the infiltrator...and delivering swift Judgment upon them. Hm. Yes."
After that, Cassandra goes back to the Lexus and returns to her hotel suite. She plops herself on the bed, her cheeks puffing out when she exhales loudly. She pulls out her Blackberry and sees that Amy still hasn't responded to her earlier text.
She texts her again: "Hiya! Doing OK? gonna b in birmigham 4 a while. cant stop thinking about u."
Amy's response is kinda harsh: "Go 2 sleep, FFS. Plz, not now. Just...put your bloody phone aside and stop being so clingy all the time, alright? I have work in the morning."
Cassandra is taken aback. She lets out a tiny gasp and puts a hand over her mouth. Her eyes begin to tear up. She sets her Blackberry on the nearby nightstand and stares at it with a broken expression.
Tiredness soon takes hold of the Ghoul's body. She falls asleep a few minutes later, her phone left on the nightstand. She cries into the pillow before slumber takes her.
The next morning, Cassandra wakes up feeling a tad refreshed. She takes a soak in the bathtub before going over the details of last night's meet up with Salim. The Assamite is expecting her to attend a meeting in the morning.
She checks her Blackberry phone. No text from Amy. The time is 8:10 AM. She's got a while before she has to head out. She turns on the TV and flips through some channels. She lingers on BBC for a good few minutes before switching to ITV.
From there, Cass flips to Channel 4 and idly watches whatever's playing at the moment. She's not really paying attention. She keeps checking her phone, hoping for a reply from Amy. Her hope dwindles each time that she picks up the phone, only to find no new notifications whatsoever.
None whatsoever.
She soon switches off the telly and decides to head down for the most important meal of the day; apparently, the hotel has a buffet setup.
The Toreador Ghoul grabs some toast and applies some dark-colored jam on top of it. She eats it and gets ready for the long, long day ahead. She's dressed fairly plainly- green blouse, jeans, and a few bangles on each wrist. Her brown hair is tied into a ponytail.
She heads outside and makes her way for the nearby bus stop. She's not really in the mood for driving around in the Lexus. Besides, she wants to see a bit of this city.
(Even though sightseeing is not why she's here.)
She takes a bus and rides it for about 20 to 25 minutes. On the way out, she remembers to thank the bus driver, "Thanks!"
The old man warmly smiles at her, "Cheers! Have a good one, miss." He tips his cap at her and drives off.
Cassandra walks around the city and takes it all in- the sights, the sounds, the atmosphere, the buildings, all of it. It's honestly not THAT different from Gloughchester. But it's still different enough to be noticeable.
Time flies by...
Cassandra soon meets up with a VERY frightened-looking Assamite Ghoul who proceeds to quickly give her the information that she needs. This young woman seems very eager to get out of here, very quickly. She's dressed in a thick hoodie, bulky glasses, and jeans. She keeps looking over her shoulder.
So, Cassandra will present herself as an office worker who has only recently started to take an interest in the burning issues of our dark times. She's free to use her real name or give out a fake one to the other group members.
Not all of the group's members meet up at once- reasons. So, Cass is slotted into an upcoming meeting with a few other people. All of them are warm-bodied mortals. Salim himself strongly suspects that the infiltrator is amongst this group of people.
After this is all explained to Cassandra, the Assamite Ghoul hastily gives her directions to where she's supposed to go and dashes off across the street, bumping into a few people and briskly walking off. Cassandra gathers her composure and goes on her way.
The place that this little meeting is supposed to take place at is in the breakroom of some music hall. Cassandra is at the front of this wide and imposing building by 10:30 AM.
It never hurts to be early in these types of situations. The Toreador Ghoul makes her entry and takes in her surroundings. The mezzanine area overlooks the grand, marble staircase and the spacious lobby. The rays of the sun beam through the tall glass windows, casting their light upon the floor and the staircase.
Cassandra pulls out her Blackberry and snaps a few pics of the interior before going down the stairs to the right side of the lobby. She finds the breakroom and knocks on the door before poking her head in.
"Hiya! I hope I'm not...err...too early?" Cassandra laughs nervously. She steps inside and closes the door behind her.
5 people are already seated in a circle of folding chairs. This is going to be interesting.
Cassandra introduces herself using her real name and grabs a nearby seat, "Cassandra. Pleased to make your acquaintance. I'm here because...y'know...I care about all the important issues, yeah."
A lanky man with olive skin, frizzy hair, and thick lips introduces himself, "Name's Reginald. I'm doing my PHD in Philosophy at the University of Birmingham." He's wearing a simple black t-shirt with an image of a person in a keffiyeh readying their slingshot.
The woman next to him is well into her 50s. Her hijab is colorful. She looks quite stern but also has a soft edge to her demeanor. She musters up a small smile, "I am Shirin. It is a pleasure to make your acquaintance."
The petite lady with a few piercings says her name is Ruby. Her dull gray eyes stare daggers into Cassandra's soul. She crosses her arms and nods, "Hey. Peace, yeah?"
"Adam," a ginger-haired man who looks to be in his indeterminate 20s flatly says. He's got a short beard, hazel eyes, and freckles. His attire is casual- a solid maroon t-shirt and dark blue jeans. He's doing his 1st year BA at a private university here in Birmingham.
The last man in the room introduces himself as Colin. He's a pensioner dressed in warm, bulky clothing. His fair skin is wrinkled, and his hair has gone completely white. His voice is gruff and somewhat gravelly. He used to be a binman before taking retirement due to a serious back injury.
He now volunteers at the music hall when his aging and frail body permits him to do so. He even used to play the cello but can no longer do so after a terrible accident at work.
1 of them could be the infiltrator getting all those Ghouls killed. Or the infiltrator in question could be in another different grouping altogether. Only time will tell...and there isn't much of that luxuriously lying around.
Cassandra tells them, "Right then! So, I--I'm here. We're all here. How does this usually work? I'm new to all of this. But I'm very interested in learning more!"
Ruby sits up straighter, "We're planning a protest sometime this week, right? Leave the placard designs to me, yeah? We're still going over some of the logistics and stuff."
Reginald puts in, "But the police still won't issue a permit, citing security reasons and the supposedly divisive nature of our protest. It's all bullshit, if you ask me."
Colin concurs with a simple nod of the head. Shirin silently watches them all from her seat.
Ruby looks at Cassandra and asks, "You look like you've got something to say, Cass. Don't worry, we're all friends here, yeah?" She flashes a small smile.
Cassandra nervously wrings her hands together and asks Adam, "You want to add anything? You haven't said much."
Adam softly chuckles, "Well, the protest will go on regardless. I'm organizing it, going around getting signatures, posting on social media, and so forth. As far as the police are concerned, we'll find a way around them. We always do."
Shirin chimes in, "I myself have been working with other groups and organizations here in Birmingham. I'm hoping to bring them together- if such a thing proves to be possible- for this upcoming protest. Peaceful protest, of course. Let us not forget that."
Ruby nods, "Yeah, yeah. Absolutely. Our main focus right now is seeing this protest happen. We do what we have to, push where we must, and never forget why we're doing this. It's not for us, it's not so we can feel all warm and fuzzy about ourselves. It's to bring attention to the plight of the Palestinian people- they're the focus, always have been, and always will be."
Colin nods and adds, "Yes. Precisely. Couldn't 'ave said it better meself. I've been attending and taking part in these kinds of protests for...decades on end. Maybe now's the time when we'll finally move the needle in a more favorable direction."
Cassandra keenly listens to their words. She takes this all in and nods.
Adam speaks up, "There's some hostility on social media- the usual suspects. Nothing new in that regard. But we're slowly gaining traction. If we can get a lot of people to show up, I think we'll be good."
Reginald prods, "How much is a lot, Adam?"
Adam answers, "1000? 10,000? They won't be able to turn their heads away from that."
Reginald looks at Cassandra and tells her, "Did you know that there's an Elbit Systems facility near this city's outskirts?"
Cass nods, "Yeah. I saw someone's Elbit jacket last night. What's that all about, then? Are we planning some kind of protest outside of that place? It makes sense to do that, right?"
Ruby shakes her head, "Too risky. Not that I disagree with the principle of the matter. But they could charge us with criminal trespassing and have us locked away for years. We take to the streets of this city. We make a big noise. We'll get heard, 1 way or another."
Cassandra carefully asks, "Are we the only ones meeting about this?"
Shirin responds, "There are others. We had a WhatsApp group, but it was recently shutdown without explanation as to why. We were even banned from Facebook and given no reasons for the ban. Colin was the group's admin."
Colin sighs heavily, "It just came out of nowhere. 1 day, it was all fine. The next time I log onto me computer, it's all gone. And the others in the group, too. Poof! Disappeared! Just like that!"
Adam chuckles and suggests, "Maybe we should try our luck and try making another group? Couldn't hurt to do that, could it?"
Reginald clicks his tongue, "Nah, too risky, mate. They've made it clear that they don't want us. Fuck 'em. We'll just organize the old-fashioned way. They didn't have fancy social media back in the 1960s and they still managed to shake up a storm. I'm sure that we can do the same."
He sounds confident. Almost too confident.
Cassandra has to measure her words carefully. 1 wrong or out-of-place word could cause the infiltrator to get spooked and dip. She has to be very subtle in her observations and approach. But she also wants to contribute something useful to the group.
She suggests, "I think we should keep pushing online. I wouldn't dismiss social media entirely, Reginald. It's a big thing and a lot of people use it.
Reginald pushes his glasses up a bit, "But how do we even go about that if our accounts keep getting banned?"
Cass shrugs, "I--I really don't know. But where there's a will, there's a way, right? If we can find another social media platform and work with it, maybe that'll help? Twitter, maybe?"
Ruby scoffs, "It's a microblogging website and the character limits are stifling! I mean...come on! How are we supposed to get anything done with that kind of restriction?!" She adjusts the beret on her head and leans back in her seat, looking frustrated.
Adam leans forward and says, "Let's not give up hope so soon. We'll find a way around this. I'll go over some things and get back to you once I'm certain."
From there, the meeting goes over a few other things- placard designs (Ruby is VERY insistent on designing any and all placards), routes for the protest, and a few other minor details.
It's nearly 1 PM by the time the group discussion draws to a close. They also take their time to go over the history of Palestine and the Zionist movement in the UK.
The group usually does lunch if meetings go on for long enough. Shirin knows this great restaurant down the street that serves excellent dishes from the world over, and at a fairly affordable price.
What better way to get to know a group of people than over scran? Cassandra readily accepts the invitation. They head over to a cozy-looking restaurant that isn't far off. The interior is pretty cool. It's got a very modern look and feel to it. The tables are nice and spread out, too.
A fountain sits in the center of the eating establishment, spurting forth fresh water. A few colorful fishes swim around in the fountain. Cass snaps a pic and decides to show it to Amy later.
Colin takes his time to arrive, mentioning that he had to swing by the pharmacy to pick up some medication. He doesn't seem to be in a good mood as he settles into his seat. He takes off his flat cap and rubs his face while groaning.
He sits at the head of the table. Shirin and Adam are adjacent to him. Reginald is seated next to Shirin while Ruby sits next to Adam.
Cassandra sits directly opposite Colin and feels a wee bit nervous. She smiles at everyone and gives them her attention. She prefers to let them do the talking, hoping that something useful will slip up when chins are wagged.
The menu is quite varied. As such, Cassandra is overwhelmed and has no idea what to order!
Ruby nudges Adam with her elbow and points at something on the menu. She follows it up with a snicker. Adam dryly laughs along with her.
Reginald takes off his glasses and wipes them with the nearby cloth napkin. He holds them up against the light, squinting. He puts them back on and sighs. Shirin shyly sips on her water and idly stares at the menu, already decided on what she'll get.
Colin's hand trembles ever so slightly when he goes over the menu. His breathing is also noticeably more labored than it was earlier.
The waiter soon comes along and takes everyone's orders.
Ruby goes first, "Yeah, I'll 'ave the Seatrout, but hold the asparagus and toss in some mushrooms if you can, yeah?"
Reginald goes next, "Gimme the Seasonal Soup." His eyes flit across the menu and he's about to ask for something else, but he stops himself, "I'll have that for now. Yes."
Shirin asks for the Blue Cheese Salad with a side of walnuts. She has the consideration to ask if Cassandra has a nut allergy before putting in her order.
The Ghoul shakes her head and smiles widely, "Nah, I'm great. Go ahead, Shirin."
Adam elects to go for a bowl of Chicken Fettuccini Alfredo Pasta.
Colin orders a piece of Portsmouth Fish Filet with a side of chips (fries). He mentions that his nan hails from that part of the UK.
All eyes fall on Cassandra. She wiggles in her seat and lets out an awkwardly nervous laugh, "O--oh! Haha! Um--um...I'll go for the Chicken Tikka Masala and some Jeera Rice."
She smiles and looks around, "Th--that'd be lovely, cheers!"
The waiter bows his head and hurries off to the kitchen to place the orders. The group begin to talk more about their personal lives. Cass is deeply careful in what she reveals about herself.
But she also wants to be friendly with these people- she doesn't want to seem like some random stranger who just came along and has no real interest in being a part of this group.
She plays the part of office drone perfectly, mentioning how humdrum and monotonous her work at some no-name office can get. But she also brings up how she's trying to make a difference, in whatever small way that she can.
Save for Colin (and maybe Shirin), the Ghoul's total cumulative age is far higher than the rest of the mortals at the table. It's not difficult for Cass to keep up with their conversations and banter.
Reginald does mention, with some bitterness lacing his tone, that he's lagging behind in his studies. And there's a chance that his professor may not be giving him the passing grade that he needs to continue with his studies. He's frustrated.
Shirin says that she's from Iran but came to the UK only quite recently. 2-ish years ago, as a matter of fact. She's still having some trouble adjusting to the culture and customs here but feels like she's slowly getting the hang of it.
She apparently used to work in a beauty salon somewhere in the city of Minab. She doesn't elaborate on what capacity she worked in. And she's fairly guarded about why she came to the UK in the first place.
Adam says he plans on taking a gap-year to Manchester, to sort of figure things out. Other than that, he tells the group how he and his girlfriend recently split up. It was amicable and they remain on good terms. He's been seeing other lasses since then.
Colin doesn't say much. He keeps checking his touch screen phone, squinting till the screen is nearly inches away from his nose. He grunts and sighs while texting someone.
Lastly, Ruby confesses that she is dying. The doctor's give her anywhere from 2 months to 2 years. She plainly refuses to elaborate on what she's dying from. All she says is that it's something to do with her body eating itself from the inside out.
That's why she's so insistent on doing some good in this world. As an artist, she intends to leave this world in a slightly better off state than when she entered it.
The conversation gets fairly lively. Food comes, steaming and piping hot. And the group digs in while they talk and do a little joking around. Cassandra tries her best to learn as much about the others as she possibly can without coming across as too nosy...or suspicious.
Colin's smartphone rings. He excuses himself and leaves to take the call.
Cass checks her Blackberry phone and sees that Amy still hasn't replied to her text message. She feels her heart sink in her chest.
When a large group of people flock into the restaurant, Reginald's face contorts into a grimace. Cassandra notices this and follows his stern gaze.
"What's up?" Cass asks him.
Reginald jerks his chin over to the group, "They are. Fucking Gen4 Security. Tossers. Gen4's a private security firm that, according to my research, is engaged in all manner of shady shit. It goes far beyond providing security for that lot."
Adam abruptly shifts in his seat and nods, "Yeah, I've heard about them." He takes another bite of his pasta and says nothing more.
Shirin lowers her head slightly, "Yes, I have heard of them as well. Their reputation precedes them. And it is not exactly a stellar or upright one."
Cassandra looks back at the group of people who just walked in. All gray suits and carefree demeanors. They're laughing and having a grand old time.
Colin slowly speaks up, "Heard from a Facebook mate of mine that they're also in the business of disappearing people."
Ruby shudders, "Yeah. I've seen some stuff online about 'em. Truth be told, I don't really know which of it is false and which of it is plausible. But all of it paints quite a disturbing picture if you know how to read between the lines, yeah."
Reginald pushes his glasses up again and sighs, "Let's just focus on what we're doing here. Sod them."
Adam briefly snickers, "Yeah. Haha! Sod them!"
The conversation over the meals goes hither and thither. But the topic of Gen4 Security does come up a few more times. If there's a protest, one can be certain that Gen4 will be there to provide security not for the protestors but for the types on the other side of the divide as it were.
Cassandra learns a few more interesting tidbits while volunteering some information about herself. She's cautious all the way through. Once lunch is over, everyone pays their part of the bill and splits.
There's another meeting scheduled for tomorrow. Same time- 11 AM sharp. Same place, too. The group members say their respective goodbyes, wish each other well, and disperse.
Cassandra takes her sweet time to walk back to the hotel. She looks over her shoulder every now and again to ensure that she isn't being followed or tracked.
Once she's in the safety of her hotel room, the Toreador Ghoul flops down on her bed and exhales slowly. She turns on the TV, keeping the volume at a low level. The afternoon drizzle outside slowly intensifies till it becomes a proper downpour.
When the sun sets, she'll report her findings to Salim. In the meantime, she checks her phone and decides to give Amy a ring. It rings once...
Twice...
Thrice...
Amy finally picks up after the 4th ring. Her voice is tired and drawn out, "Hmph. Hi."
"You've been avoiding me--"
Amy sighs heavily, "No, Cass. I have not been avoiding you. I've been busy trying to make ends meet in a school that is literally crumbling before my very eyes! Do you have any idea how frightened some of the pupils are. And I am utterly powerless to do a fucking thing about it!"
She sounds frustrated. Cassandra can almost feel her anger through the phone.
Cassandra winces and responds, "I'm sorry. Really, I am. If there's anything that I can do from me end--"
"No, Cass. You can't do anything to help. We've already been over--tch, never mind. How's...how's Birmingham?"
"It's a nice place, yeah."
Amy narrows her eyes and queries, "What exactly are you there for, hmm?"
Cassandra evasively answers, "Eh...y'know. Stuff and things. Kind of related to work, but kind of not. I signed NDAs and stuff, so please don't pry."
"Right. Fine. Look, I have to head home now. After your incessant texting and calling at odd hours, she's starting to get suspicious of me. A lot more suspicious. Please be careful next time."
Cassandra looks up at the ceiling and sighs, "I know, Amy. I know. I'm sorry, I just...really like you."
"I really like you too, Cass. But we have to be smart about this. I wish you all the best in whatever it is that you're doing in Birmingham, sweetie. I hope you come back to me in 1 piece. 1 sexy and intact piece."
"Yeah. I--I hope so, too. Bye, Amy."
"Bye, Cass. Mwah."
The call ends and Cassandra drops her phone on the bed. She shuts her eyes tight and rubs her face, feeling a swirl of emotions. It's kinda difficult for her. If she picks the wrong person, she'll indubitably be condemning them to a messy death.
Her thoughts are interrupted when someone knocks on the door. Room service? She doesn't recall ordering anything from room service.
She stands behind the door and loudly asks, "Yeah?"
The paranoid Ghoul from earlier harshly whispers, "Let me in! Quickly!"
Cassandra opens the door and the Assamite Ghoul hurriedly walks into the room, closing the door behind her.
The young woman impatiently paces back and forth, looking around with suspicion written on her face, "I think I was followed. W--what did you find? Anything useful?"
Cass answers, "I'm still looking. I need time, yeah? And I need a way to get into the other groupings, too."
"No. no. Not until you've confirmed that your current group is not compromised. We--we are putting a lot on the line by trusting you, Toreador. A lot. If you cross us...I can't even begin to imagine the consequences of such an action."
"I won't. I promise. I'm doing this because this genuinely means a lot to me. Yours is a cause that's fully justified, yeah? I want to do me part. I--I really do."
The Assamite Ghoul is put (somewhat) at ease by Cassandra's sincerity.
She nods and says, "Very well. I will return soon, and we shall see if your words ring true or not. Meet Salim at the same place you met him last night. 8:30 PM. He has other matters to see to, so don't be late. And be ready to give him a--a full account of everything you've observed so far. The slightest details can prove vital to us."
With those words, the Assamite Ghoul hastily exits the room. She pulls her hoodie over her head and walks away, trying to keep a low profile.
Cassandra sharply exhales and rubs her temples, feeling a wee bit tired. She mentally goes over what she's managed to gather so far. But it's all very limited. Not exactly enough to paint an accurate picture.
But she knows that she's getting close. For now, she strips down and lays her head on the pillow. Sleep soon takes her as she lies down, snoring loudly. Some drool runs down the corner of her mouth as she rests, deeply immersed in her naptime.
It isn't long before the sun makes its hasty descent. Cassandra awakens from her slumber and gets dressed- she wears the same thing that she had on earlier.
A 13th Ghoul has been reported dead. He was found in an alleyway, shot in the forehead and in the chest. His body was then set on fire.
Cassandra is escorted to the same place from last night. Salim has a grim look on his face. He sits with his fingers interlocked, staring intently at the Toreador Ghoul.
Cass takes her seat opposite the troubled Assamite, "I--I just heard. I'm so sorry."
Salim glares at her, "Help me catch this fiend. Report your findings from the morning, please." There's a harsh edge to his tone. He sounds more disturbed than he did earlier.
Cassandra shyly asks, "What about the other groupings?"
Salim stifles a sigh, "You don't believe that the infiltrator is amongst the group that you are currently in? Why? I have my own suspicions about each of them, but I would like to hear your take on it, too. I myself, due to external pressures, cannot be too certain on this matter."
Cass shrugs and leans forward, "From what I've seen so far, it could be anyone in the group, yeah. But I--I can't help feeling a bit...wrong about the whole thing, Salim. Spying on people and--and lying to them! It's not me style, never has been. And never will be!"
The Assamite faintly sighs, "I can sympathize with that. It is ugly, but it is necessary- that has been a defining feature of the Blade of Haqim for these last few decades. We are not without fault. But our cause is righteous, just, and pure."
"No disagreeing with you there, yeah. Err--"
Salim regards her expectantly, "Yes?"
Cassandra begins to recount everything to the best of her ability. Salim patiently listens and nods every now and again. He takes down some notes, too. He's left-handed. Cassandra didn't notice this until now. He also wears a silver watch around his right wrist.
On Reginald, Cass brings up his PHD, his lagging behind on the studies, and how his professor might not give him the grade that he needs to continue with his studies.
Salim dryly remarks, "A cause for frustration. Or it could be a convincing cover story."
When it comes to Shirin, she seems rather evasive about certain aspects of her life.
Salim takes this into consideration by saying, "Not suspect in and of itself. But I know how...deceitful our enemies can be. They could've flipped her. Blackmailed her into doing their bidding, even.
Cassandra points out, "Ruby's dying. I don't know of what, exactly- she won't say. What reason would a dying woman 'ave to do something like this? She's sincere about this!" She's got a point.
Salim nods, "I have seen her medical records, so she isn't lying. But that does not preclude her from being an agent of opportunity. Maybe she wants to...go out with a bang? Hmm?"
As for Adam, Cassandra mentions his recent breakup and online activity, "I've seen some of his posts online, yeah? He's got a certain bite to his words when he's talking about this sort of stuff. Maybe it could be an act?" She's not so sure.
Salim shrugs a shoulder, "It very well could be."
Cassie finishes by saying, "Colin's a pensioner. And I saw that he was struggling with his smartphone. Like, really, genuinely struggling with it. But...it could also be an act?"
Salim scratches his ear, "I've seen Colin since his time as a binman. But anything is possible, I suppose." He's very guarded with his words.
Cassandra lets out a slightly frustrated sigh, "I know. It could be anyone, Salim. That's what makes all of this so fucking difficult!"
Salim leans back in his chair and quietly exhales, "Hm. Yes."
The Ghoul changes the subject by querying, "How exactly do you know Gregory?"
"Did he not tell you of these things prior to sending you here?" The Assamite's tone is mildly derisive.
"I want to hear it from you. You know how...distant Greg can be sometimes. I get the feeling that he's sending me to do stuff and leaves me to figure out the rest. N--not that I'm complaining, mind you. But I'd like to know a bit more about...his acquaintances, yeah?"
The Banu Haqim jots down a few things before setting his pen down, "We...procured certain items for your Domitor. And none of it involved a trip to the local pound shop."
He chuckles and goes on, "Given our near mastery of the Obfuscate Discipline, we were the right picks for his many requests. A painting in this MPs house, a vase in that manor, etcetera. We moved like the wind- in and out. No casualties. Well, at most, a guard who had to be non-lethally pacified or subdued."
Cassandra smirks and nods along, "Makes sense. Did you ever feel pressured into doing any of the more...unsavory stuff?"
"As it is, we are already at a disadvantage- numerous reasons that I do not wish to delve into because it would take too long. As such, we take any opportunities that we are given. And your Domitor- despite his shortcomings- has been very helpful to us, so we repay him in kind."
Cassandra gulps, "Yeah, that's how it usually works in Kindred Society. Favors more so than anything else, really."
Salim mirthlessly chuckles, "And grudges. You'd be surprised for how long some of our kind hold onto those."
Cassandra gets back to the (pressing) matter at hand, "You know that there's an Elbit facility around here, right? Could there be some sort of connection? Also, what about the other groupings? I've only spoken to 1 so far."
"To shuffle someone new like you around would only serve to raise suspicion. These are the ones whom I suspect the most. Until they are cleared of all suspicion, I am hesitant to put you in any other grouping. And yes, I do know of the facility that is complicit in the annihilation of my people. They are known to us on many levels."
Cass prods, "'Ave you tried following them around yourself or having some of your mates follow them around, yeah?"
"It's not that easy, unfortunately. And the increased police presence doesn't aid matters. Besides, we can't stay Obfuscated forever. My brother- Malik- is currently seeing to some matters in Exeter. Our own Primogen is aiding a convoy of the Blood Trade in Bristol. So, I am left to...hold the fort, so to speak. To manage things in this Domain that pertain to our Clan."
Cassandra favors him with a sympathetic look, "Gregory would've been proper awkward for this, yeah? You--you're not cross that he's not 'ere, are you?"
The Assamite sighs and shakes his head, "No. As a matter of fact, I am grateful that he sent you in his stead. Otherwise, organizing these meetings in the evening time would be another headache that I simply do not have the patience or the temperament for."
He pauses and adds, "Any of us trailing them would incur suspicion. Birmingham is a fairly big city. There are also plenty of tight-knit communities here and there. 1 misspoken word and the infiltrator would surely pick up on it and scurry away like the filthy rat that they are. We must be exceedingly careful with how we proceed."
Cassandra hesitantly offers, "I could follow them around, yeah? I came in me Lexus- secondhand, nothing too flashy."
Salim nods approvingly, "If you so please. But be cautious all the same. You are still in Camarilla territory."
"What about getting the Prince and Sheriff involved? I mean, Ghouls are getting murdered left and right. Surely, they'd do something, right?"
The Assassin shakes his head, "This is something that is better handled internally. And our Primogen has...a strained relationship with the current Prince and Sheriff of this Domain. Nevertheless, when we do have something important, we might approach them for assistance."
Cassandra blows a raspberry and thinks out loud, "Well, I've given you everything I got. Where does that leave us now? Night's still young and I could take me car to do a bit of snooping around if you want."
Salim nods, "Very well. Do that. Unfortunately, I will not be able to ride with you. I have...funeral arrangements to see to. Please keep me posted on your progress." There's a certain heaviness in his voice when he mentions this.
Cassandra reaches over the table and gives him a gentle pat on the shoulder, "I'll do me best to help catch this fucker, yeah? I really want to help."
Salim gives her an appreciative nod and wishes her all the best in her endeavors. Cass stands up and leaves.
When her footsteps recede, the Assamite sighs deeply and interlocks his fingers once again, "I do hope that whomever you pick is the guilty party, Toreador. For the Blade shall Judge them, severely, harshly, and without mercy. And if they turn out to be innocent..."
He trails off and clicks his tongue. A few seconds pass before he resumes speaking, "It would be a great stain on all of our character, our Humanity."
The Banu Haqim closes his eyes and forces a few deep breaths through his lifeless lungs. He can only pray and hope for the right outcome.
Soon enough, Gregory's Ghoul is in the Lexus. The wind blows her brown hair about as she starts the car, reverses out of the parking spot, and drives down the road. A downpouring of rain makes visibility very low.
Cass drives by an NHS pharmacy. She sees Colin standing in line to get his medications. He's wrapped in layers of clothing and shivering slightly. An elderly woman with a cane stands behind him, looking equally as frustrated as he is.
Cassandra winces and tells herself, "He's just a pensioner. He can't possibly be who we're after, r--right? I mean, what reason could a pensioner 'ave to betray us? Besides, he said it himself- he's been serious about this for quite some time! Why'd he do a sudden U-turn so late in his life?"
She keeps on driving. When she goes past a particular residential street, she spots Adam exiting a house in a fairly upscale part of the neighborhood. The man looks like he's on a mission- a mission to get to the nearest pub, at least.
Adam throws his bomber jacket on, shoves his hands deep in his pockets, looks left and right, and strides forward. His head is lowered, and he's wearing a cap to keep his hair from getting wet.
When he walks by the Ghoul's car, she slides lower in her seat, careful not to alert the man. She licks her lips and carefully trails him till he stops by a pub. Some of his mates from uni are there. They greet each other with firm handshakes and pats on the back.
Cass decides to park nearby and wait for something interesting to happen. She heads in and takes up in a dark corner of this dimly lit public house. Their conversation looks a bit lively- lots of laughter and smiling.
So as not to arouse suspicion, Cassandra orders a tall glass of lemon juice, which she sips every now and again. It costs £5.
Adam eventually notices her from the corner of his eye. He orders another pint of lager and a packet of crisps before making his way over to her.
He fixes her with a puzzled look, "Hello."
Cassandra smiles nervously and greets him, "Hiya! I...err...I didn't notice you. How are you doing?"
"Fine. Mind if I join you?" Without waiting for approval, he sits opposite her.
Cass awkwardly says, "Oh, go right ahead."
Adam inquires, "This a usual spot of yours, Cassandra?"
"Ehh. A bit off me beaten path. Friend at the office recommended it to me."
Adam nods along, "Huh. Interesting." He doesn't take his eyes off of her.
Cassandra nervously asks, "You...how's your studies going? 1st year BA, eh? That's something! Is it what you've always wanted to do?"
Adam shrugs a shoulder, "Yeah, it's...fine, I guess. My old man wanted me to be a doctor. But I don't really have the stomach for that kinda thing, if I'm being frank with you."
"Hmm. Right..." Cassandra trails off and decides to leave, fearing that he'll somehow see through her ruse.
She makes her excuses and stands up, "Right, well, it's been a long day. And I--I got a cat back home that needs feeding. Rufus is his name, yeah. He gets right mental when he gets hungry, y'know?"
The Toreador Ghoul waves and walks away. Adam frowns at her suspicious behavior. She gets in the Lexus and drives off.
Cassandra happens upon Ruby, who's exiting the library with a heavy bag full of books slung over her shoulder. She adjusts her beret and crosses the street in a rather hasty manner. The Ghoul slows down and keeps a discreet enough distance from the dying artist.
Ruby turns a corner and enters a nearby alleyway. She sets her bag down on the floor and raps her knuckles on a door. It's painted dark green, with a bunch of faded characters engraved upon it. The door groans open and she walks into this rather dubious looking establishment.
Cassandra parks a few paces away and decides to be a bit brave. She takes a risk and knocks on the same door. A fairly plain-looking man with dull blue eyes answers the door, "Yeah? You need something?"
The Ghoul nervously stands on the balls of her feet, "Err, yeah. I'm here for the...thing, y'know--"
To her surprise, he nods her in and gently closes the door behind her. The interior is fairly average- concrete walls, white-tiled floors, a few chairs stacked up, and a bunch of flyers pinned to a bulletin board.
Cassandra asks, "So...the thing."
The man slowly walks on, "Mm-hmm. Men's naked drawing group. For purposes of honing and refining one's craft, nothing more. I don't see any supplies with you, miss...?"
"Uhm...Amy? D--do you know a Ruby?"
"Yes. She's a regular in our group. Poor lass has...months, at best. Might I ask why you're inquiring about her?"
"I'm..." Cassandra nervously gulps and uneasily smiles, "A mate of hers. F--from Facebook, yeah! Facebook. Actually, I think I saw an--an advert online sometime back."
"Hmm. Yes. We're starting in 5--"
Cassandra quickly turns on her heel and hurriedly walks away, "Yup! Uhm, I'm sorry for wasting your time! Don't tell Ruby I--I was here, OK? You go ahead and--and--and start without me, yeah? I just remembered that I 'ave to go to the shops and grab some--some pies for me nan, y'know! She's proper fond of 'em, she is! B--bye!"
She awkwardly waves and hurries out of there, shutting the door behind her. She gets in her car and drives off.
Makes sense for an artist like Ruby to want to perfect her skills as an artist before her infirmity takes her away from this world.
It takes an hour before Cassandra is able to track Shirin down. The latter is finally seen exiting some kind of bureaucratic government building with a pained expression clearly written on her face. She looks rather tired and frustrated.
Shirin adjusts her hijab and waits by the bus stop. She wipes some tears from her eyes and sniffles.
Cassandra parks nearby and rolls her window down, "Oi, Shirin!"
The other lady nearly jumps out of her skin when she hears her name being called. She looks around before cautiously approaching Cassandra's car.
"Oh...hello, Cassandra."
The Ghoul pats the passenger seat and invites the woman inside, "Come on. You look like you could use a lift, yeah?"
"Are you sure?"
Cass shrugs, "I got a full tank of petrol."
Shirin hesitates before getting in the car. She closes the door and puts the seatbelt on, "Thank you. I just had another meeting with the people at the Home Office. Some issues...with my visa. They are making a fuss because of some or the other technicality that is far outside of my control."
Cassandra begins driving, "Sorry to hear that. Right bleedin' bastards they can be sometimes, that lot. You need a hand sorting things out? Maybe a barrister?"
Shirin stares out the window vacantly, "That is kind of you, but I will be fine."
Shirin asks to be dropped off at a laundromat. She says, "The washing machine in my home is currently broken, so I am resorting to this. I called the repairman, but he is yet to dispatch someone."
Cassandra parks and offers her assistance, "I'll give you a hand if you need it, yeah?"
"Do you have some change for a fiver?" She holds up a polymer £5 note with Sir Winston Churchill's ugly mug on it.
"Oh, yeah, sure!" Cassandra digs in her handbag for some coins before handing them over to Shirin. The 2 ladies enter the laundromat- it's dimly lit, a tad grimy, and there's only 1 old man here reading a newspaper. He's got a pair of headphones on.
Coin-operated washers and dryers and folding tables for laundry are set out in rows. Cassandra helps load Shirin's laundry into a dryer. Shirin puts in the coins needed. The 2 eventually strike up a conversation about fairly mundane things.
Shirin shyly inquires, "Cassandra, if you do not mind me asking, what exactly drew you to our pro-Palestine group? It is just that I have come across my fair share of insincere people over the years- opportunists, liars, provocateurs, and such."
"Oh, I--I'm sincere about this. Really, I am. I care about this issue deeply. I see what the Israelis are doing in--in Gaza and even the West Bank. Calling it apartheid is understating matters! It's far worse! I never really paid attention to all this before, I'll admit that. But what's been going on these past few months has made me properly take notice of all of this, yeah."
Shirin is somewhat satisfied by that answer, if her "Hmm" is anything to go by. She sits back and watches the clothes go round and round. A tear rolls down her cheek and she sniffles again. She wipes it with her handkerchief and shakes her head.
"Sorry, sorry. I--I should not be like this..."
"Something's bothering you? What is it? You can tell me, yeah? I'm a good listener."
Shirin lets out a shaky breath and lowers her voice, "I--I might have overstayed my visa. The authorities don't necessarily know that yet, but I am afraid that they shall soon find out. And if they do, then the life which I've built here, the friends which I've made...it will all be over."
Cassandra presses her lips into a thin line and places a hand on the other woman's knee, "I'm sorry. I wish there was something I could do to help you out, yeah? C--can I do something to help you out?"
Shirin forces a smile and shakes her head, "It's OK."
"People 'ere have done far worse than overstaying their visas...and they're walking the streets freely. I know that it's not bloody fair, but it's true."
Shirin has an expectant look on her tired face, "Please don't report me to anybody. Especially the police? Promise me that, Cassandra?"
Cassandra silently nods. Shirin gives her a grateful smile and pats her hand in appreciation. She says that she can grab the bus back to her house.
"It isn't that far off, actually. You should be getting home now. It's getting late. And I don't like being out late at night either, for...many reasons. Sometimes, I--I see the shadows moving on their own."
Cassandra stands up, bids the other woman farewell, and leaves.
Cassandra drives through the city for another hour. No sign of Reginald in the usual spots. Where would a PHD student usually hang out around here?
The Ghoul eventually sees him strolling down the wet sidewalk with his hands shoved deep in his pockets. He's wearing a brightly colored yellow heavy-duty raincoat. His galoshes are also rather vibrant.
Cassandra turns the headlights of her Lexus down. She squints and observes him from afar. He stops by a worksite and flashes something or the other to the security guard on duty. The guard nods and lets him through.
Cassandra waits a few minutes. Reginald still hasn't come out. She gets out of her car and walks towards the gate. She approaches the guard and asks her, "Oi, who did you just let in?"
The security guard is tall and thin. She's got a slight scar near her lower lip. She asks in a low voice, "Who's asking?"
Cassandra conjures up a lie and states, "I'm...with the Met. And that man you just let in? He's on a watchlist...or 2. What's his name? Wh--what's he doing 'ere?"
The security guard blandly answers, "Reginald. He works here part-time, officer...?"
Cassandra lifts her chin and prods, "Not relevant. I'm here about Reggie, not me. How long has he been working here? Do you know him well?"
The security guard raises an eyebrow, "Yeah, he's been working here for a couple of months now. Went for some drinks after, but he was too tired to come with us to the pub. Should I be worried about him being a terrorist or something?"
"What exactly does he do here?"
The guard beckons to the half-done structure behind her, "What do you think? He works with the construction crew."
Cassandra nods and points at the other lady, "We never 'ad this conversation, you never saw me. We never spoke. And not a word of this to Reggie, yeah? But--but don't act all weird around him, too. That may tip him off."
The security guard shrugs, "Sure, whatever you say, officer. Will that be all?"
Cassandra gives the lady a quick and curt nod before returning to her car.
Reginald taking this job would explain his lagging behind in his studies. Money's must be pretty tight for him to be resorting to such desperate measures.
When Cassandra gets back to her hotel, she locks the door behind her and throws herself on the bed with a loud groan.
She pulls her Blackberry out and shoots Amy a text: "Me feet are killig me!"
Amy texts back with a simple laughing emoji.
The 2 of them spend a few minutes texting before a heavy sleepiness overtakes the Toreador Ghoul. She yawns and stretches before putting her phone aside and shutting her eyes. Within mere minutes, her loud snoring dominates the hotel room.
Much later...
The infiltrator makes a phone call.
A man on the other end of the line picks up. His accent is stiff and formal, "You have news for me?"
"Not necessarily. Just checking in, sir. We've got a new addition to our part of the group. She's a complete and utter moron, by all accounts."
"Really now? Is she going to be a threat to your cover?"
The infiltrator shakes their head from side to side and confidently replies, "No, sir. She's just as clueless as the others."
The man on the other end of the line orders, "Drop by the Elbit facility tomorrow. After you're done with that silly little group chat of yours."
"Understood, Captain."
"Will there be anything else?"
"No, Captain. Are we getting any closer to the blank body threat in this city?"
The man straightens his posture and firmly answers, "We are peeling the layers off, 1 by 1. It's only a matter of time now. When they inevitably make a grave error of judgment, we shall swoop in and end them all. But don't get overconfident. It wouldn't be the first time that these creatures have slipped out of our grasp. Crafty buggers."
The infiltrator notes, "That's why you called me in. A specialist in subversion and undercover work."
"Yes. You understand your purpose perfectly. Get some rest now. We'll speak more once there's something of value that you have uncovered."
"Understood. Goodnight, Captain."
"Likewise."
Uh-oh.
Well, at least these bastards (whoever they are) don't suspect Cassandra of anything. One hopes that she uncovers the infiltrator's identity...and soon!
The sun begins to rise. A ray of light comes through the window. Cassandra rubs the sleep (and sleep crust) from her eyes. She groans and rolls over. She wiggles around on the bed and opts to catch a few more zees.
Cassandra groggily wakes up at 9:27 AM. She elaborately stretches herself before slowly sitting up. She yawns and looks at her phone before shoving the charging cable into it. She gets ready for her morning routine.
The Toreador Ghoul strips out of her sleeping vest and knickers. She discards them into a corner of the room before walking over to the bathroom. She sways her hips from side to side while singing a few lines from her favorite singer.
She repeats those cheesy, recurring lines from Nicola Green's 2007 album. It's an upbeat and catchy little song about yearning and desire. Cassandra really vibes with it.
The Ghoul's singing becomes louder as she steps into the shower cubicle. The warm water cascades down her naked, curvaceous body. She runs her fingers through her long brown hair, enjoying the feeling of the water caressing her skin.
After thoroughly washing herself and brushing her teeth, she wraps the towel around her body and steps out. She wipes the condensation from the mirror with her hand and looks at herself.
For this day's outfit, she goes for a Burberry's checkered cotton dress that comes all the way down to her ankles. Stud earrings and assorted bangles complete her ensemble.
Cassandra applies only a bit of makeup- some dark eyeliner and concealer along with some lip gloss. She fluffs up her hair before leaving her hotel room.
She fiddles with the colorful hairclips in her hands as she walks down the hallway. She eventually snaps a few of them onto her long hair.
The Ghoul heads down to the hotel's restaurant and takes her time to select from among the many buffet offerings. She helps herself to a small bowl of mixed fruit, a cuppa some Yorkshire tea, and some mini scones.
She devours her meal, feeling a bit icky about the whole thing. Something about spying on people and giving them half-truths just don't sit right with her.
But she does tell herself that it's for a good cause. At least it'll stop another innocent Ghoul from being brutally murdered.
After breakfast, she decides to go for a quick stroll through the city. 11 AM is a long way off, so she has plenty of time to spare in the meanwhile.
Cassandra ends up heading down to the local library. She plops herself down on a beanbag and grabs whatever's within reach. She ends up reading through some bland thriller.
It's now 10:49 AM. She better get moving if she wants to get there in time for the meeting. Cass sincerely hopes that the infiltrator will inadvertently slip up and reveal their true intentions today.
She's soon in the usual breakroom. The usual faces are already present.
Reginald's eyes are baggy and his cheeks are rather gaunt. He looks as if he hasn't had a decent night's sleep. He tiredly greets Cassandra and gives his arm a slight pinch to help keep himself awake.
Shirin's wearing a black hijab with a floral design today. Her smile doesn't quite reach her eyes as she asks how everybody else is doing.
Ruby glares at Cassandra quizzically before going back to scribbling something or the other on her notepad.
Colin dusts his checkered flat cap and adjusts the scarf around his neck.
Adam's eyes dart to the analogue clock mounted on the wall every few seconds.
Colin gently brings his hands together in a soft clap, "Right, then! Shall we begin? Does anyone have anything to say- anything at all- before we proceed? We are eager to get this protest underway, but we want to ensure that everybody is fully on board with this."
Reginald stifles a yawn and blinks rapidly, "I'm fully on-board, mate. Yeah, we should be able to make it work."
Shirin avoids making eye contact when admitting, "I am still speaking with the others. No luck yet, but I hope to change that soon."
Ruby faintly mumbles, "I'm working on the designs for our placards. Just leave that to me, yeah? We want them to be eye-catching, a tad bold, and fully able to convey our demands to those who'll even bother to see. I...I can manage that."
Adam has got his arms crossed over his chest, "And I've got some of my mates interested in joining the actual protests. I can vouch for them, they're good lads. Solidarity, y'know? But I don't know how many of them are willing to risk getting arrested for this...should it go sideways for whatever reason."
Colin gives everybody an approving nod and musters up a smile, "Right, right, excellent! We're making progress. Slowly, but surely. Let's hope that this protest actually happens soon. You mightn't know this, Cassandra, but there's a lot that goes into organizing something like this. There are a number of formalities that need to be sorted out."
Shirin timidly raises her hand, "If I may? Have--have we yet received the appropriate permits from the authorities?"
Adam frowns and shrugs his shoulders, "I think someone in another grouping has an MP's number, so that should help expedite the process. But I wouldn't hold my breath..." He trails off and looks at the clock again.
Colin continues, "There's still a lot to do, but we should be able to get it done. Now, let's go over the routes once more. We're going to want to avoid the Synagogue on Blucher Street--"
Ruby interjects with a scoff, "Why? If we make a bunch of upper-class Jews uncomfortable, so fucking what? That's what we want to do, innit?"
Colin winces a little, "We're going to want to avoid going past there lest the tabloids paint us in a bad light, Ruby. As it is, we're not exactly their favorite types of people. It would do us no favors to be seen marching past such a place."
Reginald sighs heavily, "Fuck the tabloids. After that nasty phone hacking business, you'd have thought that their readership would've crumbled by now. But I still see plenty of people lining up to buy Rupert Murdoch's rags. I don't see the appeal in any of it, personally."
Shirin points out, "The tabloids in this country are like a--a virus, infecting the hearts, souls, and minds of the people. It's disturbing. And quite tasteless. Especially those Page 3 girls, or whatever they call themselves. Vile."
Cassandra leans back in her seat and casually says, "I'm sure we'll make it work. Adam, are you doing alright? Your leg's shaking and you keep looking at the clock. You 'ave a class to be at or something?"
Adam stiffens up and shakes his head, "N--no. It's more of a review session, really. Exams aren't anytime soon, so I--I get to breathe a little, yeah?"
The group discuss other matters before eventually adjourning the meeting. They all start leaving. Shirin asks if everyone wants to do lunch today, just like yesterday.
Reginald rubs the base of his neck, "I got a doctoral dissertation that needs editing AND I got a mate who needs help with hers. Sorry. Maybe another time, hmm?"
Ruby tugs on her bone horn earring and declines, "Got a doctor's appointment to keep up. Not that it matters anymore, but I guess it's better safe than sorry."
Shirin then turns to Adam, Colin, and Cassandra, "Would you like to come?"
Colin puts on his flat cap and politely declines, "Can't, sorry. I got a pain all over me body. Shivers, too. Took me 5 whole minutes to get out of bed and to the bathroom. Old man problems, haha! I'm feeling a bit poorly, too."
Shirin nods and shifts her gaze towards Adam.
Adam taps his digital watch and plainly states, "I have to complete my reviews, Shirin. Sorry. Maybe Cassandra would like to join you?"
All eyes are on Cassandra now.
The Ghoul gently lets Shirin down by stating a (plausible) excuse, "Me nan's been cross with me lately. I reckon that I'll go and try to smooth things over with her. You know how nans can be, right?"
Colin chuckles, "Oh, yes! Indeed! I wish you the best of luck, Cassandra."
Shirin gives the other members of the group a sad little smile, "Well, I guess it is just me today. I--I understand. Life, more often than not, tends to get in the way. But perhaps...another time?"
Adam nods and quickly makes himself scarce.
Colin smiles widely at Shirin and hobbles away.
Ruby winks at Shirin before leaving.
Reginald tells the older lady, "Absolutely. Once I get my shit sorted, I'd love to do lunch with you and everybody else. Cheers!"
Cassandra asks, "Need me to drop you anywhere?"
Shirin ponders the question for a few seconds before politely declining, "It's fine, thank you. I think I'll walk. I was thinking of buying a scooter. But I am afraid of...well, you know what we discussed last night, right?"
"Hmm. Yeah. You should get in touch with, reach out to someone who's capable of helping. See if they can do something about it, yeah? They'll understand your circumstances. I'm sure of it."
"Maybe you're right, Cassandra. Thank you. I will do that once I get home. Have a--a pleasant day."
Cass wishes her well and walks out of the breakroom. When she steps outside of the music hall, Adam accosts her. She nearly jumps out of her skin and lets out a slight yelp.
Adam extends his hands out in a placating gesture, "Whoa, whoa, it's just me. Relax. Take it easy."
Cassandra glares at him and remarks, "You shouldn't sneak up on people like that, yeah?"
"Eh. Old habit of mine. Hard to break. Anyway, I wanted to know what your socials are. You know, Facebook, Twitter, Reddit, that sort of thing. It'd help us all keep in touch with each other, especially if something important comes up."
Cassandra thinks up an excuse and smoothly says, "Oh. I--I deactivated most of those, sorry. After me...partner moved to Sweden, I couldn't bring meself to go on social media anymore. Too many memories, yeah? Some of which were painful."
Adam rubs the back of his head and gives her a sympathetic smile, "I'm sorry to hear that. As someone currently going through a breakup, I can empathize with your pain. But you've got to move on eventually. Right?"
Cassandra side-eyes him and tersely replies, "I guess so."
Adam slowly chuckles and pats the side of her arm before going off. He shoves a pair of earphones deep into his ears as he walks down the steps.
Cassandra's brows knit together in deep suspicion. Why'd he just come up and ask for her socials? Was he waiting for her out here? Why? She feels that something is very wrong, but she can't quite put a finger on it just yet.
She decides to feed her suspicious curiosity by following Adam. He hails a taxicab and gets into it. Cassandra keeps her hands firmly on the steering wheel of her Lexus and does her best to keep up with the speedy taxi.
They make their way out the outskirts of this city. The cars start to get a bit too scarce for Cassandra's liking. Her suspicion only deepens.
The taxi soon rolls to an abrupt stop in front of an empty lot. Adam pays the cabbie and steps out. He surveys his surroundings with narrowed eyes before walking down the road.
Cassandra slows her car down and maintains a safe distance from him. She squints and cranes her neck when she notices that he's walking towards a worn path. Scores and scores of large trees surround her and the road.
Adam suddenly stops and looks over his shoulder. Cassandra gets out of her car and ducks behind a tree. Her heart's racing and sweat is starting to form on her forehead. A light drizzle starts.
She peeks her head out and sees Adam picking up the pace. His head is lowered and his fists are clenched. He disappears deeper into the woods.
What kind of a review session is this?!
Cass carefully trails after him, taking care to hide behind trees every so often. The muddy ground beneath her feet is making it a tad difficult to stealthily follow someone. And the footprints sort of blend in with the wet and thick mud.
Adam eventually comes to a stop. In front of him stands a fairly gargantuan facility of some kind. It's nestled deep in the woods.
The entire structure is dull looking from the outside- colorless walls with bits of moss growing on them. An electrified chain-link fence surrounds the perimeter of the whatever this place is.
Cassandra gasps softly when she spots a few security cameras (very hi-tech looking) installed on a large pole near a small guard's booth. A guard sits within, smoking a cigarette while reading through a magazine.
Adam approaches the guard and exchanges some kind of friendly, informal, and casual handshake with him. The guard lets him in. Adam walks past the entrance and into the facility.
Cassandra sneaks closer. Her jaw goes slack when she sees a sign that reads: "Elbit Systems" above the main entrance. Adam's figure fades away into the distance.
Holy Nod...
She gulps the lump of fear in her throat and slowly backs away. Her body shudders from the cold rain. She turns around and hastily returns to her car. Cassandra nearly trips and falls over a few times.
The Toreador Ghoul hops in, starts the car up, and peels out of there. The tires squeal and the brakes screech. She drives away as fast as her car will allow.
Her lower lips quiver as realization helps to banish the last bits of disbelief still lingering within her.
Adam is the infiltrator! Adam has been reporting back to someone all this fucking time!
Cassandra angrily slams the steering wheel and grunts.
Well, at least she now knows who it is. Before leaving, she managed to snap a few pics of the facility. Not that her Blackberry has the greatest of cameras, but it'll have to do for now.
Her hands are relentlessly shaking. Her heart thumps in her chest. She wants to stop and pull over, but she doesn't feel safe just yet.
The evening cannot come soon enough...
Cassandra skipped lunch and has been staying awake with nothing but coffee and Jelly Babies. When the sun finally sets, she gets up from the bed and stretches out. Her spine pops a little.
She groans and watches at the sun finally retreats from view. Her lips are dry and her face is a little paler than usual.
Cassandra grabs her handbag and heads out to meet with Salim and relay the disturbing news to him. She only hopes that she's right and that Adam is indeed the infiltrator.
Then again, why would someone part of a pro-Palestine group just happily waltz into a fucking Elbit facility? What'd a person like that be doing near such a foul and wretched place, anyway?
(Apart from protesting it, of course. Or attempting to shut it down.)
And the way he was acting with the guard at the front- all chummy and friendly...it just doesn't add up. None of it does! The whole thing is making her head spin.
Cassandra wildly gesticulates and talks a bit too fast for Salim's liking when telling him about what she saw earlier today.
The Assamite calmly urges her, "Please, please. Calm down. You look...awful. Did you even take any rest in the afternoon?"
Cassandra's eyes are wide, "Couldn't! I--I was scared out of me mind! So, I spent the rest of the day locked up in me hotel room watching David Attenborough documentaries, drinking coffee, and eating Jelly Babies! Couldn't eat anything else, really. Didn't 'ave the appetite for it."
Salim's eyebrows go up a little, "Ah. David Attenborough. A pleasant man. His documentaries, I have found, can be quite captivating. And informing. Anyways, let us return to the pressing matter at hand."
Cassandra sniffs, "Yeah, yeah. So, it's Adam. Here are some pics of the Elbit facility. It's not the best, sorry. I saw him walk into the place like it was no biggie! I would've stayed, but the muddy ground felt like it was shifting underneath me feet. I got lucky that I wasn't spotted, yeah?"
Salim squints at Cassandra's phone and confirms, "Yes, that is indeed the Elbit facility." The Assamite sounds a little distraught.
Cassandra tucks some of her hair behind her ear and asks, "What now? You don't really look that angry."
Salim sighs, "I am not surprised. But what infuriates me the most, Cassandra, is his connection to Elbit. It makes this even more sinister than I had originally thought."
He squares his jaw and proceeds, "This is damning evidence in and of itself. You have done well, Ghoul. You may leave, if you'd like."
Cassandra uncomfortable shifts around on her chair, "Well, yeah. About that...what are you going to do?"
Salim has an unreadable expression on his face when asking, "Are you familiar with the inscription atop the main entrance of the Old Bailey?"
Cass quotes it from memory, "'Defend the Children of the Poor & Punish the Wrongdoer.'"
"Yes. Especially that last part. The courts will not do their jobs in punishing such treacherous people like Adam- or whatever his name really is. The Blade, however, is not held back by the hypocrisy of such institutions who sit idly by while the peoples of the Middle East are slaughtered day and night. This is the only way to bring justice."
"Oh," Cassandra gulps and nods a little.
Salim leans closer and emphasizes, "We don't know what other crimes Adam is complicit in. Name a court of law that will prosecute him for those. None of us can. Our solution, I will concede, is far from ideal and makes people uncomfortable. But it is magnitudes better than allowing such evil to prevail. Adam must pay."
