He growls slightly, "He must be Judged. The Blade will Judge him."
Cassandra presses her lips into a thin line and nods along, "S--sure. Err--"
"Something troubles you. Please, do not hesitate to tell me." Salim's dead eyes are very soft and gentle now.
Cassandra notes, "He didn't 'ave the look of someone walking into an Elbit facility with the intent to blow it sky high. His walk through the woods felt practiced and full of purpose, like he'd done it a million times before. And the way he was acting with the guard at the front- all friendly like and such."
The Assamite agrees, "Hmm. Definitely damning. You have done your part, Toreador. We will take it from here."
"Erm, I'd like to see it through to the end. How are going to find out who Adam REALLY is?"
"The Post Office."
Cassandra's face scrunches up in confusion, "Post Office? What do you mean?"
"I will explain when we see each other again. 22 Somerset Road. Don't be late." He rises up and strides out of the room.
Cassandra quickly follows after him, her feet feeling a tad wobbly. She looks down and sees that there's still bits of mud clinging onto the bottom of her boots. She scrapes her feet and walks on. Her heart's beating very fast again.
The Post Office in question is a fairly narrow structure. At this hour of the night, it looks particularly menacing. The lights within are dimmed and the other nearby buildings are either shuttered or boarded up. Only a few cars are parked along the side of the road.
Cassandra parks her Lexus and joins Salim. She wonders out loud, "What is this place? Why 'ere?"
Salim raps his knuckles on the plexiglass window near the door, "It's an actual post office, among other things."
A tall man in his indeterminate 40s with deathly pale skin, piercing amber eyes, and very short brown hair opens the door. He gives Cassandra a smirk and turns to Salim.
"Come on in," he says, his accent vaguely resembling that of a Scouser. His voice is surprisingly soft and quiet. The man's wearing a light-brown long-sleeved shirt which is tucked into his high-waisted black trousers.
Before Cassandra can get a word in, the tall guy turns to her and smiles in a slightly unnerving fashion, "Horace said you'd be coming. Cassandra, right? C'mon in."
Cass is dumbfounded, "Horace? Horace Grimley? You--"
He lets out a slight laugh, "Oh, sorry. Where are my manners. Name's Nigel. Malkavian by Blood. Of the 9th Generation. Approximately...110-years-old, give or take a decade or 2." He extends his cold hand for a shake.
She shakes it and feels a tad unnerved by his preternatural grip, "You...spoke to Horace? He's a good friend of mine."
Nigel nods, "Yes, the Fledgling and I speak regularly through the Malkavian Madness Network. He said that you already know what that is. Anyways, Salim, mate. How are things faring, eh?"
Salim plainly states, "We need your help. And it is the kind of help that you specialize in providing, my friend. This matter is most urgent."
The Malkavian smiles knowingly at Salim, "So I've heard."
He then asks, "Either of you got a light for me bifter?" He holds a cigarette in between his index and middle fingers.
Both Salim and Cassandra shake their heads.
Nigel shrugs and pushes his glasses up his nose, "Ah, no worries, lad and lass. Let's take this to the back, eh? The bizzies are about the place, and I don't want to risk drawing any more attention to us than we already 'ave."
They make their way into a cramped room with a long table and several chairs around it. A computer is nestled on the far end of the table. A few large cabinets line the walls.
Nigel sits in front of the computer and cracks his knuckles, "So, what's the happenings, eh?"
Salim explains that he requires some help in uncovering Adam's true identity. Nigel keenly takes it all on, having this unnatural shine in his dead eyes. He keeps muttering about how this is going to be "fun."
Cassandra skeptically asks, "Can you actually find out who this bugger really, TRULY is?"
The Malk snickers, "'Course I can! But I'll need time. Lots of it. But not lots-lots. Hacked into some top-secret, clandestine government databases after I figured out the passwords in me day-sleep. Once I find something on this 'Adam' fellow, I'll let you 2 know. So, you both can wait around here in the meantime, eh?"
A few hours later...
Nigel is shocked by what he's managed to uncover, "Well, isn't he a right scally? Salim, you seeing the same thing that I'm seeing, mate?"
Salim's expression hardens as his eyes go over the information displayed on the computer screen, "Yes, I see it all. So, that's his real name, then? Tch."
Cassandra reads it out loud, "Avishai Lieberman. Israeli intelligence officer. Cor blimey!"
Nigel scrolls down a bit and points at the screen, "His dad is with the Shin Bet and his sister is a military lawyer of some kind. Wow, you've got yourselves a proper infiltrator in your midst, eh?"
The picture on Avishai's file exactly matches that of Adam's.
Salim balls his hand into a fist, "Yes. And he will pay for his crimes against our people. Thank you, Seer. You have done us a great service, and we do not easily forget those who aid us."
Nigel's amber eyes twinkle, "Oh, it's me pleasure. And lemme guess: Adam/Avishai won't be going to the ozzy after you lot are done with him?"
The Assassin simply pats the other man's shoulder and departs from the cramped room. He beckons Cassandra to come with him. She waves an awkward goodbye and follows after Salim.
As they exit the post office, she remarks, "Cor blimey! I never would've guessed that he was Israeli intelligence! Shit!"
Salim has a slightly pained look on his face, "Indeed. And that only makes all of this even more insidious than it already is. We will deal with this swiftly and quietly. Do you know what the worst part of all of this is?"
"No."
The Assamite lowers his head pensively, "I helped him set up a few fundraisers for people back in Palestine. He told me that he wanted to do some good, help the people under occupation, send them some money for food, medicine, shelter, daily expenses, and so on."
"That's messed up. What do you think were his real intentions?"
Salim grimly states, "To annihilate us. I--I watched on as he accumulated the personal information of people back home. He told me that this was a way of doing good for them. And in my naivety, I believed him! Tcah! I am such a fool! How could I not have seen the treachery right in front of us?!"
Cassandra places her hand on his tensed shoulder and says, "I'm sorry to hear that- it sounds awful, yeah. But I still need some explaining on what EXACTLY he was doing."
Salim forces a deep breath and says, "It was benign and altruistic of him- and we all certainly cheered him on. He'd put well-intentioned people in touch with those in the Occupied Territories who were in desperate need of basic necessities. It's dire and the boots of the Jews on our necks don't make things easier."
"So, he's...got a lot of personal info that'd make it easier for Israel to...disappear people? Or worse?"
Salim gives her a grave nod, "Yes. You've said it clearly, Toreador. And I helped him. I helped him get this information, verify it, and I even organized a few fundraising drives. I even introduced him to people who'd be willing to help him out. All this time, I was signing death warrants of my own people..." He trails off and sighs heavily.
Cassandra pats his arm, "It's alright. We got him pegged now. He doesn't know that we--you're onto him."
Nigel stares at the 2 of them through the grimy window. Stacks and stacks of boxes and uncollected mail are piled high near his feet. A faint smile creeps onto his face.
Salim politely waves back, "He's a strange one, that Nigel. But he has been helpful to us...and to our cause. I will have to tell the relevant people about all of this."
He fixes Cass with a significant look and remarks, "Now, you are free to leave. Consider us even, hmm? And give your Domitor my best regards. You have done your part. Now, stand back and let us do ours. I would not recommend being around for what happens next."
Cassandra nods a bit too vigorously and says, "Yeah! But I--I understand why you're doing it. I get it."
Salim notes, "Word is travelling fast of a Toreador Ghoul who destroyed a Brujah Wight. You are developing quite a reputation among our kind."
She nervously laughs and says, "Is that so? I--I only did it because there was no other choice. Otherwise, I'd be dead."
"You needn't justify your actions to me. Your survival is justification enough. We all have to do what we must in order to survive, yes?"
"Yeah."
They walk down the footpath before parting ways with a firm handshake and warm smile. Salim's smile doesn't quite reach his eyes, though. He quickly turns around and disappears into the dark night.
Cass hops into her Lexus, starts it up, and peels off. She speeds down the road. Her phone rings and she answers it.
Amy's voice comes through, "Hey, babe! Still in Birmingham?"
Cassandra pulls over to the side and replies, "Yeah, yeah. Just 'bout to leave, actually. I'm checking out and coming back to Gloughchester."
"Oooh. I can't wait! What kind of souvenirs you getting me, by the way?"
"You didn't exactly ask for much and...this wasn't really a trip for pleasure, to be honest."
"Hmm. Just get your pretty self back here, yeah?" There's a sultriness in Amy's tone that's quite hard to miss.
Cassandra cheekily grins, "Oh, I will. Bye!"
"Mwah! Bye!"
Cassandra begins driving back to the hotel. She packs her things away and prepares to check herself out. After she exits her room, she takes the elevator down and goes to the front desk. She places her palms on the counter and smiles at the woman sitting behind it.
"Hiya, I'm checking out now. Cassandra."
The woman nods and types a few things into her computer. Even now, tons of people walk to and fro. Cassandra glances at the clock nearby and notes that it's almost 11 PM. It's gonna be a long drive back home.
She waits for the well-dressed woman to finish up with whatever she's doing and finalize her check-out. The woman then asks, "And how was your stay, Ms. Cassandra?"
Cass shrugs a shoulder, "It was fine, yeah."
"Just fine?"
"Ehh. It was more of a work thing more than a trip for pleasure," Cassandra says while making a few vague hand gestures.
Her eyes drift over to a stack of flyers next to the computer. She notices something that's particularly eye-catching: it's some kind of recruiting ad for Elbit Systems. A young, racially ambiguous man is smiling brightly as he stands behind a vast megastructure not too dissimilar to the one Avishai walked into just a few hours ago.
Cass's face scrunches up as she gingerly picks up the flyer, "How did I not notice this before? What's this?"
The other woman shrugs, "Came in a day back. Between you and me, love, unemployment in this city has skyrocketed. This Elbit lot, however, seem to be hiring. Maybe I can tell me brother to get a job there. He's been having a right tough time of it ever since he graduated uni. And me couch is sinking in because he's sleeping in it all day. Tch." She rolls her eyes and groans.
Cassandra winces and shakily says, "Don't. They--they're a shady lot."
"Oh?"
Cassandra vigorously nods, uneasily smiles, turns on her heel, and quickly strides towards the entrance of the hotel. After her stuff is loaded in the dickey (trunk) of her car, she pulls off and makes the long drive home.
She stops by to grab a shawarma. She sees another stack of those damned Elbit flyers sitting next to the register. Cassandra looks away and orders her food. She pays in cash and finds a table for herself.
Even at this hour of the dark night, the place is full. Smoky and meaty scents waft about the area. Everyone is speaking in a different language or dialect. There's a thick slab of meat on a hot spinner thing being rotated by 1 of the workers behind the counter.
While eating, Cassandra can't help but think of Avishai's inevitable fate. It has been sealed. Her eyes linger on the way that meat is being heated up and spun around and around. She feels her stomach churn a little and she forces herself to stop thinking of such things.
She chews and swallows the last bit of food before leaving the restaurant. She wipes her hands and discards the tissue papers in the nearly overflowing trash bin nearby. A few people stand around and smoke, chatting amiably amongst themselves.
Cass greets them with a tight smile and hops back into her Lexus. She blows a raspberry, her cheeks puffing out. She pulls off and drives down the highway. She elects to drive a little slower than she normally would.
While nearing the city's outer limits, she squints and sees a billboard being erected. A bright white light shines down upon the workers who are currently busy screwing the sign in place.
A look of disgust overtakes Cassandra's face when she reads the words: "Elbit Systems- Face the future with empowered confidence." And right underneath it is an image of a diverse group of smiling people in suits and coveralls looking at a futuristic-looking megastructure.
She drives off as quickly as she can.
The unemployment is hardly an issue confined to the Domain of Birmingham. It's pretty widespread throughout the UK as a whole. And all the austerity, cuts, and policies implemented by the ruling parties aren't helping matters at all.
That, in turn, permits predatory firms like Elbit to swoop in and stick its talons into desperate communities. Neoliberalism in practice, y'all.
Back in Gregory's Haven...
The phone of Cassandra's Domitor rings. Alistair is present with him. Greg holds up his cold finger and answers the phone with that ever-so-flat tone of his.
"Hmm? I see. OK, very well. Yes, we'll talk more when you return. Mm-hmm. You, too. Goodbye, Cassandra. Yes, I'm fine, thank you. That's alright. Hm. Bye."
Alistair has 1 leg folded over the other while seated on an ornate armchair. He smiles a little, "Still sending her out and about, eh, old boy?"
The other Toreador merely nods his head.
Alistair picks up from where he left off, "Like I was reminding you: your halcyon nights are behind you, old boy. However, in your relatively short Unlife of trafficking art, you have managed to build something for yourself. Something of a...name, perhaps? A reputation?"
He takes a sip of the blood from a wine glass before going on, "A legacy, if you will?"
Gregory sighs a little, "I doubt it goes that far, Alistair. I--I only went into this line of work because I couldn't keep doing the...other thing- which I actually developed a liking for, mind you- forever. My Sire pointedly reminded me of this inconvenient little fact. And he..." He stops and glances away for a moment before fixing Alistair with a significant look.
The former doctor narrows his eyes out of concern, "Something the matter?"
Gregory waves his hand dismissively, "No, no. I thought I--I saw something in the corner of my eye, but it must've been nothing. Sorry, I--where was I? Oh, right. My Sire told me that I had a natural talent for trading in art and that I could make a somewhat decent Unliving out of...doing this sort of thing."
"Was he right?"
Gregory sighs heavily, "Even after all these decades, I'm not so sure."
Alistair leans forward and inquires, "Do you miss your old job? Do you still have a fondness for that sort of thing? Do you even remember how to do it?"
Gregory flatly answers, "Some things, you never forget. Like riding a bicycle, they say."
The other Toreador chuckles softly and says, "I know you don't exactly like talking about it. For what it's worth, I do miss the operating theaters, but stepping down was in everybody's best interests. I had become far too Obsessed, and I certainly didn't want--want--"
He chokes up a little and clears his throat, "My apologies. I didn't want it to towards an unhealthy direction. It takes a Toreador to understand the struggles of another Toreador, doesn't it, old boy?"
"I suppose it does. Alistair, if you don't mind indulging me for a moment..."
Alistair adjusts his silver cufflinks and gestures with his free hand, "Go on, old boy. Anything. Anything at all. Name it."
"Do you...see things- unusual things- in your day-sleep?"
"Like dreams?"
"No."
"Hallucinations?"
"Y--yes. In a manner of speaking."
"Hmm. Well, old boy. I'd say that everyone of us Kindred sees strange things in their day-sleep. Why, are YOU seeing something peculiar?" Alistair leans forward a bit, his expression growing a tad concerned.
A look of discomfort washes over Gregory's features as he fiddles with his thumbs, "Well, I--it's hard to describe."
Alistair looks at his silver watch and notes, "Well, I don't really have anywhere important to be tonight. There's a friendly game of bridge scheduled- courtesy of the Gloughchester Bridge Party- but my presence isn't strictly mandatory. They're a very understanding lot."
"Hmm. Are they...cross with my decision to suddenly drop out of their Coterie by withdrawing my application?"
Alistair smiles wryly, "It was quite unorthodox- abrupt, even- but I put in a good word on your behalf, old boy. You have nothing to worry about. Your decisions for withdrawing- sudden as they may be- are yours and yours alone. You are free to do as you will. As we all are, eh?"
"Hm. Thank you, Alistair. You've been a decent enough chap throughout it all."
Alistair chuckles softly, "And thank YOU for much of the same, really. So, what exactly are you seeing during the day? Or I suppose it's more accurate to ask...WHO exactly are you seeing? It's not just some fleeting image, is it?"
Gregory shakily responds, "It's hard to describe. But--but when I see it, my entire body helplessly freezes. I feel like I can't move, can't speak, can't think. I--I can't even blink or close my eyes."
"Good heavens! Mind if I ask what 'it' is?"
Gregory's eyes suddenly flit over to a darkened corner of his Haven. He sees a faint silhouette standing there, looking right at him. The 5 eyes on the figure have a menacing glow. He gulps, his lips quivering. He can feel his dead heart palpitating.
Alistair follows Greg's gaze and looks over at that corner. He sees nothing, no one, nobody at all.
He wonders out loud, "What's over there? Who--who are you staring at?"
Gregory blinks rapidly before facing his friend, "Wh--wha-what?"
"You zoned out on me, old boy. It happens a lot to us Aesthetes, eh? Be that as it may, what exactly are you seeing?"
Gregory's mouth hangs open a bit, and he stammers, "I--I can't exactly--maybe this was a mistake. Perhaps it--it shall fade away with the passage of time. I shan't keep you here any longer. Thank you for listening to my concerns."
Alistair's eyebrows shoot up, but he doesn't prod any further. He nods in acknowledgement, "Think nothing of it, Gregory." He stands up, pats his mate on the shoulder, and takes his leave. He exits through the main entrance of the Haven, leaving Gregory all alone.
And a tad afraid.
Hours later...
Gregory returns from a walk around the property. While walking through the woods, he swore that he heard twigs snapping in the distance. He kept walking, though, thinking that it must be some forest critters scampering about or what have you.
Cassandra is nearing the city limits for Gloughchester. Back to familiar ground for her. At last. She passes by a group of people who are walking on the sidewalk. They're dressed in bulky parkas, boots, and beanies. A few of them wear sunglasses despite it being nighttime.
Vampires? Eclectic types? Eccentric types?
All of the above, maybe?
Nevertheless, Cassandra pulls over and dials Amy's number.
Amy politely excuses herself and finds a better spot to take this (very) important call. There are numerous guests in her house, and her spouse is busy running the show and entertaining the guests.
Amy bites her lower lip and keeps her voice low, "Cass..."
"Hiya! I'm back! What's all that commotion in the background?"
Amy sighs and casts a few glances over her shoulder, "We're entertaining guests. Like always, it's mostly her posh friends from the bank she works at. Who knew that Barclays investment bankers could be such bellends, eh?"
Cassandra sympathetically winces, "Wow, you sound proper riled up. What's gotten into you?"
Amy sighs and looks over her shoulder once more. She fiddles with the ring on her finger and squeezes past a few more of her guests before saying, "You know how I am when she invites all of her fancy mates to our house, right? And that's not the only thing. We had a bit of a row. Again."
Cass ventures a guess, "Money problems. Again, yeah?"
Amy's chuckle is mirthless, "Mm-hmm. It got so...heated that she blurted out how little I make in comparison to her and how I ought to pull up my socks, find a better job, and all that. She--she didn't necessarily mean it, but...she didn't exactly take it back, either."
"Cor blimey. Listen, you want to...maybe...err...come over and spend the--"
"No, Cassandra. She's already on-edge and I have a feeling that she suspects something is...amiss. I'll come over when I'm ready. I--I--I could use a bit of a...release."
Cassandra bites her lower lip and smirks, "So could I. How's work? Walls at the school still crumbling?"
Amy grunts feebly, "Yeah. I'm getting quite annoyed with how nobody is interested in fixing things, actually. And I'm far from the only one, y'know?"
"Yeah."
Amy glances over her shoulder once more and says, "It pains me to constantly hear the pupils worrying about whether they're going to get flattened by a falling ceiling or something. And then there's the usual crowd who just don't give a rat's arse. Or maybe they really DON'T care."
"I care, Amy."
Amy shakes her head and lets out a small chuckle, "I know you do, babe. Do you...recall when we first met at that pub?"
"How could I forget? We bumped into each other on the way to the ladies' room and--and hit it off. 1 thing led to another and...it came as a shocker when you told me you were married! Haha! But after that, we..."
Amy smiles and sighs, "You don't have to remind me. We kept shagging. Everywhere and anywhere we could. It was so naughty and thrilling. It still is, is--isn't it?"
Cassandra takes a good few moments to respond, "She always puts you down because you don't make as much as her, Amy. I won't ever judge you in the way she does. I like you, yeah? I--I really do."
Amy swallows the lump forming in her throat, "I know, Cassandra. Listen, I--I have to go now. We really ought to see other...naked. And soon, yeah? Any more out-of-town trips for you?"
Cassandra hesitates before replying, "M--maybe. But I'll try to make some free time in between. Just us, yeah? You and me. Me and you, eh?"
Amy's voice is low and husky, "I would like that very much. I--I have to go now. I can feel her eyes burrowing into the back of my head. Talk to you later, babe. Bye."
Cassandra makes a kissy sound, "Mwah! Bye!"
Amy hangs up and slips her phone back into the pocket of her trousers. She pats herself down, gathers her composure, raises her chin, and strides back inside the house.
Amy's spouse titters and sends someone off before focusing her full attention on her partner. She has an arched eyebrow, and her expression is mildly disapproving.
Amy glares at her and musters up a tiny smile while making small talk with a few of her wife's friends.
Amy's spouse is some kind of portfolio manager for Barclays. Lots of £ compared to Amy's pitiful salary as a school admin- this has always been a cause of friction between the 2 of them.
And it's no secret that Amy's feels a tad insecure about how little she makes in comparison to her partner. It's frequently brought up in the loud arguments of the couple. Amy is constantly reminded of this by her wife.
Anyhoo...
Cassandra is back in the comfy and familiar coziness of her cottage. She hastily unpacks, freshens up, and plops herself down on her comfortable bed for a quick breather.
Gregory raps his cold knuckles against the door of his Ghoul. Cassandra rolls over and grumbles a little.
"It's open!"
He steps in and calls out, "Are you...feeling alright, Cassandra?"
Cassandra rubs her face, sits up, and watches as he takes a chair near the entrance of the bedroom and places it beside the bed. He sits down and regards her with a rather unreadable expression.
She sighs, "Yeah, why? Are you faring well?"
"Adequately. So, how was it? Did you assist the Assamites in their...business?"
Cassandra glances away and mumbles, "I did. Yeah. This one's a--a right thriller, yeah. Rooted out an infiltrator and all that. It'll take me a good long while to properly process the whole thing, though. But I reckon that he probably had it coming."
Gregory joins his hands together and notes to himself, "So, I am even with the Banu Haqim Clan. Good."
Cass glares at him, "Who's next, anyway? Daughters of Cacophony, right? Oh, wait, no...Children of Osiris, yeah? Sorry, I'm a bit tired."
Gregory corrects her, "No. The Baali. He has just arrived a few hours back, actually. This time, he owes me a favor. I--I suppose it's fortunate that I have a Ghoul who is willing to assist me in these sorts of tasks."
Cassandra heaves a long sigh and pinches the bridge of her nose, "Mm-hmm. So, tell me more about this Baali, then?"
"Not until you tell me about your Birmingham trip. And please, Cassandra, spare no detail."
Cass trills her lips and begins recounting everything from the tippy top. Greg keenly listens; he occasionally zones out and stares at the wall.
The next day...
A light rain drizzle pelts the Elbit Systems facility in Birmingham. Right in front of it, something akin to a kneeling figure can be seen. The Gen4 Security personnel were called in to have a closer look at it- they're the ones providing primary security for the place.
There are 2 dark vans with tinted windows parked near the front gate of the facility. The operatives approach with their bullpup weapons shouldered. The kneeling figure appears to have their arms stretched out to either side of their body.
Something appearing to be rebar sticks out of their chest and palms. The operatives' boots sink into the mud as they carefully approach the body. They have their finger on the triggers.
"What the fuck is this?" 1 of them harshly asks.
Avishai's body is deathly pale. He has been impaled with rebar and completely exsanguinated. His head is snapped back, and his mouth hangs open. His face is frozen into a permanent rictus of shocking pain and horror.
The other operative gasps and warns, "Lock the area down! Immediately! Code Red! Code Red! Get a fucking clean-up crew out here! NOW!"
"Potential hostiles in vicinity! Ready your weapons! Fan out and--and find who did this! Go! Go!"
The operatives assume defensive positions around the body of that fascist rat bastard. The rebar has been driven into the hard, solid ground. An operator takes a cautious step closer and asks how such a feat of strength is even possible.
Their question is soon answered when a tall man approaches the body. He has fair skin, is well into his 60s, and has short and somewhat curly gray hair. He speaks with a firm and authoritative tone when commanding the Gen4 personnel to leave this area at once.
"We will be taking control of this scene at once. Your services are no longer required. Say nothing of what you saw here today. Nothing."
He paces around the dead body and ducks down to inspect the wounds. He hums, nods, and glances over his shoulder, "This is the work of blank bodies. Let me guess: the surveillance footage is...missing?"
The head operative gulps, "There--there's nothing on it, s--sir. It all looks fine, then...out of nowhere, his body just suddenly appeared on the grounds like this! We're doing everything we can to--"
The tall man holds up his finger, revealing a few burns and scars on his hand, "I know your procedures and protocols all too well. But your efforts are wasted here. They are long gone, these...savage creatures. This was a message that they intended to send. They don't want us here."
The operative wonders, "And who exactly is 'we', sir?"
"That, my boy, is classified. Far beyond what a lowly Gen4 operative like yourself would be privy to."
The operative nods and turns away.
The man paces a tight circle around the body, stroking his shaved chin and mumbling to himself. He shakes his head and mutters, "We certainly have our work cut out for us, don't we?"
He heads deeper into the Elbit facility and takes a very important phone call from someone high up in his particular Org. He straightens his posture and licks his thin lips when the voice on the other line asks him about the Birmingham situation.
A woman's stern and somewhat metallic voice demands, "Report, Captain Meadows?"
"Situation is under control, ma'am. Blank bodies left us a little present like a cat leaving a dead and bloodied rat at the doorstep."
"You've lost your only way into the inner circle of the vampiric threat. Was it wise bringing somebody from Israeli intelligence into this? He was, in all likelihood, compromised. It's no wonder that he was dealt with in such a...heinous manner."
Captain Mark Meadows nods solemnly, "Yes, ma'am. That would appear to be the case. What are your instructions, ma'am?"
"Operation Brightburn must proceed at all costs- it is primarily your mission, after all, Captain Meadows. Don't fail us. Branch 16 is counting on you. We are counting on you. Do not fail us. Neutralize the blank body threat by any means necessary. If you have need for more resources, you need only ask."
"Yes, ma'am. As a matter of fact, I think that our presence here in Birmingham has been compromised. I'm not quite ready to risk an all-out war with these creatures."
"What do you intend to do, then? Hmm?" The impatience in her voice grows.
He replies, "I am requesting permission to set up shop in the city of Gloughchester, ma'am. The blank bodies over there have been getting rather sloppy in hiding themselves. And my...source is never wrong about such things."
"What of Birmingham? Is it a lost cause, then?"
"No, ma'am. Not--not necessarily. We will continue to monitor the blank bodies in Birmingham, but I fear that we shall get nowhere if we keep pushing in that direction. Without a man on the inside, we are essentially flying blind."
The lady can be heard shuffling a few papers on her desk and typing on her mechanical keyboard.
She hums, "Permission granted. As a matter of fact...we already have a building that shall serve our purposes well. A very fitting location, indeed. I will send you the address and necessary passcodes as soon as I can."
Even though no one can see him in this dimly lit room, Captain Mark bows his head a little, "That would be most helpful, ma'am. Can we continue to count on your support in other matters, as well? My...contact in Gloughchester still remains aloof about how he is able to acquire such vast information on the blank body threat."
She dryly intones, "Your secretive contact is of no concern to us. The results that he has delivered, on the other hand, are what really matter. Keep your pet informant happy and fed, Captain Meadows. Given the nature of our work, we can't afford to be picky."
Captain Mark firmly nods, "Yes, ma'am. We--we certainly cannot. And what of the...others who we routinely work with? Far be it from me to overstep clearly defined boundaries, ma'am, but--but I can't help but wonder..."
"Your skepticism regarding their involvement is well-founded, Captain Meadows. And it is hardly a secret amongst Branch 16's top brass. Nevertheless, they are an asset that we must make the most of in our operations against the blank bodies."
Captain Mark mumbles, "The lesser of 2 evils. I still do not trust them, ma'am. Nobody sh--should have such powers over--over reality itself!" He almost screams out that last part.
Her voice is cold and unfeeling as she flatly states, "The blank bodies are even worse, Captain Meadows. Focus on them. Just be glad that we're getting...outside help. Even if the ones offering a helping hand aren't exactly what we'd define as 'conventional'."
Captain Mark sighs and says, "Very well, ma'am. I shall keep you fully updated on the developing situation. Is--is the Gloughchester facility...full of them, ma'am?"
"Find out for yourself, Captain Meadows. Good day and godspeed."
Captain Mark lifts his chin and firmly declares, "God save the Queen!"
"God save the Queen," the woman on the other end plainly repeats before hanging up.
Captain Mark Meadows puts his phone away and takes a few moments to gather his thoughts. His eyes are heavy with weariness, yet there is also an unwavering sense of conviction and purpose that radiates from him.
He straightens his posture and walks off to attend to the matters that only a man of his stature and reputation can properly deal with.
The forces of the Second Inquisition are coming to Gloughchester.
(It wouldn't be the first time that the Blade of Haqim has Judged someone like this. Plenty of psycho imperialist soldiers returning from committing war crimes in the MENA region were also subjected to a similar fate. Until the mortal institutions get their shit together and act decisively, alternative forms of justice will continue to be pursued).
It is simply the way of things. And condemning the desperate deeds of people like Salim is a fool's errand.
Can things be better? Yes! Absolutely. But a conscious effort has to be made in order to move the needle and bring about said betterness. And that takes a great deal of time.
Act 4: Baali
"The Baali aren't a Clan, per se. But they're numerous enough to count as such (among other reasons). Anyways, their Bloodline is comprised of alleged...devil worshippers (or demon worshippers). I barely attend church these nights (can't even recall the last time I went). But none of the few Baali I've personally come across have erupted into flames. Perhaps there's another reason for their...infamy amongst our kind." -From the personal diaries of Gregory | Entry: On The Baali.
The sun is hidden behind clouds this morning, giving it a rather dim appearance. A few of its rays, however, manage to find their way through the clouds and gently caress the ground below.
Cassandra's alarm clock goes off, but she smacks it off the bedside table and lets out a long, long groan. It clatters on the floor and keeps on making an obnoxious ringing noise. She rolls over and covers her head with a pillow.
After Gregory spent most of last night giving her a long explanation on who this particular Baali was and why he owes the Toreador, she felt a headache coming on. And Greg really was vague about a great many details. He's always doing that- being vague and such.
The Ghoul, for her part, was simply too tired to press her Domitor for answers or clarifications. He simply said that more will be made known when the time's right. And so, she simply shrugged, gave him a thumbs-up, and trudged to her bed.
She was out within minutes. Her loud and grating snoring now fills the air as the alarm clock continues its incessant ringing. Cass wishes to sleep in for a bit, but she also has a nagging urge to wake up and do something.
She's a Ghoul, yes, and is beholden to the will of her Domitor.
Nevertheless, she snorts, wiggles on her bed, and rolls onto her stomach. The pillow does its best to muffle her loud snoring. The drool keeps on flowing, and her mouth hangs open. Cassandra's eyes are closed, and her brown hair is a bit disheveled.
She dreams of a great many things, which is the norm for her. It's the same old routine that has become somewhat comforting and familiar. Some are recollections from her past whilst others are just plain fanciful fluff.
Cassandra dreams of taking a selfie with her favorite singer- Soho Starlet, Nicola Green. Both are dressed in stylishly trendy clothes, and the latter has her arms wrapped around the former's shoulder. Cass is all grins and she squeals after taking the selfie.
In a separate dream, Cassandra is back in her comprehensive school days. She vividly recalls how her working-class voice and language of home were relentlessly belittled by the middle-class educators.
They did everything to get her to speak more "proper" but, despite their best efforts, her accent remains defiantly Northern. She has long since learned how to not let such snooty, elitist, posh wankers get under her skin.
Cass's dreams go hither and yon. She feels herself in a body that is both familiar and isn't quite hers at the same time. It is not her flesh and bones, yet she can control this other being's movements.
The sun is blazing, its piercing rays coming through the blinds. She stands in front of a small mirror and inspects her face. It's her face, alright. Hazel eyes, brunette hair, whatever else. She notices a tiny beauty spot near the corner of her eye. She rubs her finger over it and blinks a few times.
From whatever (and wherever) that was, she is yanked into a different dream. The landscape is completely dark, and a great number of strange, colorful, and glowing shapes are moving around.
By the time the Toreador Ghoul wakes up, it's nearly midday. She groggily opens her eyes, rolls over, and yawns loudly.
She stretches herself out, feeling some popping and cracking, and sits up in bed. She blinks a few times, rubs the crusty bits out of her eyes, and groans, "Cor blimey...what's--oh, that's the time. Hrrrrfhgh. Ah, well..." She trails off and readies herself for a bath.
While the water in the tub is filling up, she brushes her teeth and checks her phone. Just a few cheeky texts from Amy, but that's just about it. Cass replies with a stream of heart emojis and puts her Blackberry aside.
Since she's already naked, she steps into the bathtub and settles down in the warm, sudsy water. A sigh of contentment escapes her lips as she gently leans her head back.
Cass sings out a few lines (off-key) from some of her favorite Nicola Green songs. She's a bit rusty and can't recall the exact words at the moment, but that's no matter.
"LaLaLaLaLa, love is in the air...sweeping me through my hair. OooOoOh, I'll always love ya, babe...oh, baby..."
Once done with the bath, she steps out of the tub and wraps herself in a towel. She glances at the mirror and runs a hand through her hair, "Ah, yeah...that was proper refreshing!"
Tonight is when the real work begins. So, Cassandra throws on a simple Adidas tracksuit that she got back in the year 2000 for only a few quid; she's done her best to keep it pristine. White stripes run down the sides of the blue tracksuit.
She slips into a pair of pink trainers, ties the laces, and puffs her cheeks. After grabbing her cell phone, she leaves her cottage and makes her way outside. A few orange leaves have already fallen, and they crunch under her soles.
Cassandra inhales a lungful of the crisp autumn air and lets it all out. She heads towards a small bench a few paces away from the cottage and plops down.
The Ghoul fiddles around with her phone while deciding whether to take breakfast, wait till brunchtime, or just skip the meal entirely. She goes for a short walk, pops by the local pound shop, but doesn't end up buying anything.
The rest of her afternoon is spent lazing around in her cottage, reading a book. This is quite a heavy read: "The God Delusion" by Richard Dawkins.
Cassandra identifies as an atheist, but she is NOT an obnoxious "Reddit Atheist" who trashes religion (and people of faith) just for some superior gotcha points and hollow ego-stroking. To her credit, she doesn't even own a fedora!
She has a modicum of respect for the man and even looks up to him a bit. Keep in mind that this was long before Richard expressed all sortsa nasty, yikes views like harboring eugenicist views, supporting a genocide, being a massive transphobic twat, or most pathetically...going all goo-goo-gaga over his AI girlfriend.
Anyways, Cassandra isn't necessarily the type of lass to blame all of the world's ills on religion (organized or otherwise). She recognizes that the current socio-economic system is rotting on its feet and dragging not just the UK but the entirety of the world down into the abyss.
She can't help but grimace whenever some of her fellow atheists (online or offline) point their fingers at religion, especially when it comes to matters of the Middle East. They completely ignore imperialism, colonialism, capitalism, and a myriad of other factors that are directly responsible for the current (miserable) state of the region.
Cass has gotten into a few arguments with them- mainly on Facebook- and it always devolves into an angry back-and-forth before 1 of them ends up blocking the other.
Cassandra sets the book aside and yawns. She picks up some fiction, turns to a random page, and starts reading. Ever since she was a wean, she's always been a curious and eager reader- everything from her father's leftie newspapers to books not quite meant for her age to random Internet articles.
When Wikipedia first became a thing, she spent a whole week proper going through and absorbing every last word that the then-fledgling site had to offer.
In time, Cassandra elects to 'ave a quick nap. Sunrise isn't that far off. She tosses and turns a bit on her bed, trying her damnedest to get comfy. A light rain patters on her window, and she soon drifts off into a peaceful snooze.
Cars go to and fro. The streets of this Domain are slick with rainwater and puddles. It's a gray, drizzly day- typical British default weather. And the gloominess that hangs over the city does a good job at reflecting the overall mood.
A slight mist pervades the area, obscuring some of the buildings. This city is about to undergo...a shaking up in the next few days (and nights).
For now, it remains decidedly unstirred.
The sun begins to make its retreat from the horizon, and the shadows lengthen. The Kindred in this part of the globe begin to arise. Their senses sharpen, and the Beast within them growls for a liquid red treat.
Gregory's eyes pop open, but his body remains frozen. His breath hitches, and his eyes dart back and forth. With great effort, he turns his head and sees...it.
The Aapilu just stands there, menacingly cloaked in the darkness. The creature of the Blood twirls its 6 fingers and glares right at Gregory. Its gray and slimy skin appears to be slick and moist.
Gregory can't move a single muscle. He helplessly watches as the Aapilu runs its fingers across the wall. It stays where it is and merely stares at him with its 5 eyes.
He desperately tries to free himself from this paralysis, but the Aapilu's very presence has rendered him entirely immobile. Gregory grimaces and tries to make a sound, but no sound escapes his dry lips.
The Aapilu cocks its head to the side and makes a clicking sound. Gregory can do nothing as he watches the creature stand in place and taunt him. The Beast within him is restless, but its roar is suppressed by the Aapilu's powerful influence.
Something resembling a tail abruptly flicks and smacks the wall behind it. Gregory tries his damnedest to look away, but the Aapilu's gaze is locked with his. His Blood runs cold and his body trembles.
Gregory squints and tries to get a better look at what the creature is doing, but it's quite hard for him to tell. Even blinking his eyes is proving to be Herculean task.
Another clicking noise comes from the Aapilu. Gregory's dead heart thumps wildly as his fear grows. The Aapilu doesn't move an inch. It crouches down and growls softly. Its 6 fingers twitch and clench into fists.
Gregory gulps and tries once more to move, but his efforts are met with no success. And just as suddenly as it had appeared, the Aapilu vanishes into thin air. Poof!
The body of Cassandra's Domitor becomes limp. Gregory lets out a painful wheeze and pulls himself out of bed. His face is a bit pale, and his forehead is covered in a layer of cold and somewhat bloody sweat.
He forcefully exhales and hobbles over to the bathroom. He switches the light on and winces when the bright and fluorescent light hits his face. Gregory turns the sink faucet on and splashes cold water on his face. He stares at his dull and baggy eyes in the mirror.
With his face still dripping wet, he dabs a fresh towel over it. The Toreador sighs, and the first thing that pops to his deeply troubled mind is how Cass is gonna deal with the Baali tonight.
This guy owes Greg a huge favor- it has something to do with the Aapilu that had just visited him. This particular Baali claims to have an understanding of these ancient beings and even offered to help "banish" the being who is currently tormenting Gregory.
The Toreador's expression is grim. He only hopes that the Demon Worshipper is true to his word and follows through on his side of the deal. Otherwise, it's going to be a massive problem.
Gregory walks back to his bed, plops down on its edge, and forces a deep, deep breath. He clasps his cold hands together, closes his eyes, and feebly mumbles.
Gregory is a sort of backslidden Anglican. He's been a C of E (Church of England) lad ever since birth. That being said, he does attend church whenever he feels like it- usually for Christmas or Easter services.
He sits in the back, stands when told, sings from the hymnbook, kneels on the cushions when told, folds his arms when the sermon begins, and generally goes through the motions. He even takes communion but eventually ends up spewing it out as scarlet vomit.
(Because if it ain't actual blood, the Beast declines it in the sharpest way possible.)
There are times when Gregory will have a little prayer or a hymn stuck in his head, but it doesn't happen that often. It's more of a Toreador thing than anything else.
Anyways, he doesn't discuss religion that much and kind of understands why his Ghoul isn't a believer.
He opens his eyes and rubs his palms over his face. His shower is fairly brief and mundane. The hot water flows down his naked body and soothes his taut muscles.
His stomach growls. His eternal Hunger is making its demands known. And, judging by the sounds outside, there are a few mortals out and about.
Like always, Gregory is sending his Ghoul on his behalf. So, he slips into a plain pair of trousers, puts a nice polo shirt on, combs his hair a little, applies some body lotion, and steps out of his bedroom.
He heads downstairs and takes a seat at the couch. He looks around his sleek and modern Haven, appreciating the interiors and the view outside.
It's shaping up to be a pretty decent evening, all things considered. The issue of the Aapilu still lingers on his mind, but Greg is hopeful that the Baali will be able to aid him in getting rid of this supernatural pest.
After about 10 minutes or so, Cassandra waves at her Domitor. She is dressed in a leather jacket with a t-shirt underneath (official Nicola Green merch that costs a pretty penny), denim jeans, and a pair of Doc Martens.
A red headband holds her brown hair in place. The Ghoul flashes a big grin and waves, "Hiya, Greg!"
The Domitor ushers her in and dryly replies, "Hello, Cassandra. How are you...feeling? All prepared for tonight's tasks?"
Cass nods and says, "Yeah! See, I even wore me official Nicola Green t-shirt!" Her grin widens when she tugs on it.
The words are a bit faded, but it's a glittery style that simply reads: "SOHO STARLET".
Gregory has no meaningful reaction in either direction. He simply walks back to the couch and sits down. Cassandra follows him, and she breaks the silence, "So, um...this Baali. If you owe him, then why am I being sent? Shouldn't I be tagging along with you?"
Gregory answers, "I'm not the biggest fan of rock music. I have no issues with those who take a liking to such music, but it is simply not to my taste. It--perhaps you'll like it?"
Cass shrugs, "I'm a Nicola Green fan. Always 'ave been and always will be. But, yeah, I suppose I fancy a bit of rock 'n' roll from time to time. What's his name, anyhow?"
"Sven. But he's painfully American."
"Oooh. Some friends from across the pond, eh?"
"Hm. Yes. He's planning on putting on some sort of a concert here in Gloughchester for the next few nights. I'm told that it has been advertised all over social media? Have you seen anything in the regards to it? Perhaps heard it from a friend?"
The Ghoul shakes her head, "Can't say I 'ave, no. You know, Greg, you should open a Facebook account, at least. You don't 'ave to put your picture or use your real name, yeah?"
"It's not for me, Cassandra. Anyways, Sven will be playing here in for the next few nights. He owes me and I expect him to keep up his end of the bargain. I'm sending you on my behalf to ensure that he doesn't forget. Rockstars like him are rather...forgetful, and they tend to change their minds."
Cassandra grimaces a bit, "You're not expecting me to do anything improper, yeah?"
Gregory sighs, "Of course not. Just give him a friendly reminder. Offer to help him get his concert set up. Make yourself useful, if you must. Promote him on your social media...pages or however you call them. Just make sure that he keeps his word to me. This--this is important to me. I need his help."
The Toreador doesn't wish to elaborate any further.
Cassandra, on the other hand, prods, "What exactly do you need him for?"
Gregory's lower lip quavers, "It's nothing for you to get bothered with, Cassandra. It's best if you just go and take care of business. What I require assistance with shall remain squarely between myself and this Baali rockstar. You needn't get involved."
The Artisan gives her a serious and stern look, and that's enough to silence the Ghoul.
Cassandra gulps and nods, "Uhm...fine. Yeah, fine. I--I understand. I got it. So, where's he gonna be tonight, then?"
Gregory produces a slip of paper, "Here's the venue he'll be performing in. There will also be some sort of a VIP party after his performance. I've managed to speak to his personal assistant and manager so that you'll have easy access. Just show the bouncers your name and that'll get you in."
Cassandra's eyes light up, "Oh. Wow. A proper backstage pass and all, eh? You--you think that you could do something like this for Nicola Green, hmm?"
Gregory's chuckle is faint and brief, "I shall see what can be done."
"Ah, right, then. I'll head off now, yeah? Anything else to add?"
Gregory thinks for a moment, "Hm. Be careful around him, Cassandra. Always be cautious. And please ensure that he remains true to his promise. This Demon Worshipper owes me, and he shan't leave the country until we are even. Got it?" There is a hint of desperation in the man's voice.
Cassandra picks up on it and does a 2-finger salute, "Got it, Greg! I'll remind this Sven fella what's what. And if he makes a bit of a fuss, then what?"
The Toreador sighs heavily, "It goes against what we agreed, but give me a call if he expresses recalcitrance. Keep it brief and I shall be there to put him back in line. But please don't resort to that unless absolutely necessary."
"You don't 'ave to tell me twice, haha! Want me to get you some kind of merch from this concert? A trinket, a knick-knack, some sort of memento? A signed--"
Gregory curtly responds, "No. Just ensure that Sven remembers his obligation to me. Nothing more, nothing less."
Cassandra smiles, "Yeah, OK. Got it. Err...bye, Greg!"
"Yes. Goodbye, Cassandra."
As she cheerily walks off, the Domitor mumbles to himself, "And come back safely. I need you just as--just as much as you need me."
Cassandra does her makeup in the Lexus. She applies some black eyeliner, a little blush, and a dash of purple eyeshadow. She's really leaning into a very "rock 'n' roll" aesthetic for tonight's concert.
She sprays some perfume onto her neck and wrists. She fluffs her hair up, starts the car, and begins her journey.
As she drives to the location, she listens to some Chase & Status on her phone. She sings along, tapping her fingers against the steering wheel and bobbing her head in sync with the fire beat.
At this wee hour of the evening, the city of Gloughchester is abuzz with activity. People go out and about, the streets are filled with traffic, and there are many cars parked along the roads. Bright neon lights flash from various establishments, and there is an energy to this metropolis that makes it truly 1-of-a-kind.
The drizzling intensifies. Cassandra has the wipers turned on. They swish and swoosh, clearing out the rainwater and allowing the Ghoul to see clearly. She hums to the beat of the music and taps her fingers.
Sven's concert shall be held at a large indoor area with full aircon, superior acoustics, and stuff. Cass has never been there, but she's heard quite a few tales about the establishment in question.
It's supposedly under the ownership of a Ventrue Ancilla, but she has never been able to independently verify such claims.
Cassandra's Lexus comes to a screeching halt in front of a huge line. Many fans have already arrived and are eagerly waiting for their favorite rockstar to grace them with his presence.
Despite the rainfall, the line seems to extend for a few meters or so. The fans have umbrellas and are huddled under tarps. Some have been here since morning, as evinced by the tents that they occasionally poke their heads out of.
Cassandra's heart beats wildly with excitement. She finds a parking spot, readies her umbrella, and gets out of her car. She locks it, puts her keychain into her pocket, and circles around the vehicle.
Her white umbrella is opened, and she makes her way to the back of the building.
Sven's personal assistant (and manager) tells the armed (!) guards to let her pass. The assistant is a svelte woman in her 30s. She wears a pinstripe skirt, has her graying hair tied in a bun, and is constantly on her tablet. A bulky touchscreen phone pokes out of her large purse.
She gives Cassandra a once-over before telling her to hurry up and get inside. She speaks with a decidedly plain American accent, probably generic Midwestern.
"You're late," she curtly states.
Cassandra nervously smiles and responds, "Sorry about that, yeah. I'm Cassandra, by the way. Greg's...err...y'know? Where's Sven at?"
"Carla. Sven's getting ready and he takes his sweet time. So, I was told to give you the VIP treatment, right?"
"Mm-hmm."
Carla sighs and pinches the bridge of her nose, "I'll get the bouncers to ensure that you have the necessary clearances...but just behave. People tend to go all wild and lose any sense of propriety around him."
Cassandra playfully bumps the other lady, "Ah, don't worry. I'm a Nicola Green fangirl. I've never heard of this Sven before."
Carla raises an eyebrow, "Hmm. Just behave yourself, OK? Not everybody gets the opportunity that you do. Where is Gregory, anyway?"
"Busy. He sent me in his stead."
"Hm. Interesting. I was really hoping to get his opinion on a piece of art that I've had my eye on. Never mind that, please, this way."
She looks flustered while leading the Ghoul through a brightly lit corridor. The floors are shiny, the walls are pristine, and the lighting is warm and welcoming. Personnel charged with organizing the concert hastily run back and forth.
There are posters and banners plastered all over the place. None of them have Sven's picture on it, but they do have timings, dates, and ticket prices.
(And they do be some pricey prices!)
Cassandra gawks at how much they cost, "Blimey! That much-- you lot are charging an arm and a leg, yeah?"
Carla's phone buzzes and she checks it. She ignores the Ghoul and hisses into the phone, "No, dumbass. Yes! That's what I fucking said last night! NO! Hmph, fine. Call me when you DO manage to get your head out of your ass and find the right one. Yeah, fuck you, too."
Cassandra walks on and looks concerned, "Everything alright? You--you need any help? Greg told me to offer up me helping hand in case there was anything."
"We're fine. I'm--I'm fine. We're almost there, now. Please, try not to screech when you see him. People tend to do that, and I get nasty headaches whenever that happens. I'm serious. Just stay quiet and--and act normal, OK? NORMAL."
The Toreador Ghoul shrugs, "Fine. I'll behave meself."
Carla trills her lips and picks up the brisk pace. Cassandra keeps up with her, and the pair soon arrives in a well-decorated room. Carla takes a deep breath and pushes the door open.
The room is carpeted, has seductive mood lighting, and a nice, long couch. On the wall are framed albums that have been personally autographed by the Baali himself. Oddly enough, his signature is a simple "K" in lieu of an actual name.
The only window in the room is covered with bead curtains. Makeup artists surround the man of the hour himself. Someone dabs his cold skin with a brush. Another makeup artist applies a good amount of concealer to cover up that deathly paleness characteristic of a Kindred.
Carla clears her throat, "Sven. I have someone here to meet you. The fact that she hasn't gone nuts is a good sign. This is Cassandra. Gregory's...assistant, I guess?"
She focuses on Cass and firmly asks, "Can I trust you enough to be left alone in his company? Are you gonna behave? No screaming? No flashing your boobs at him? No throwing your bra at him? No asking him to have your babies?"
Cassandra cringes and gives the other lass a look of incredulity, "I'm not about to go and do something like...THAT! Cor blimey! Yeah, you--you can trust me. I'm not going to cause any problems."
Carla's stare is penetrating, "Promise? I don't want to stay up for 12 hours dealing with another lawsuit."
Cassandra's face scrunches a little, "Lawsuit? Like--"
Carla holds up a finger and interjects, "Just don't do anything that we'll all regret, OK? I'll be out and about if you need me. Have fun, now. Hey, Sven--"
Sven speaks, his voice smooth and oddly charming, "Carla, I heard everything, honey. Get outta here. Make sure that those sound people do another mic check. We don't want a repeat of Mississippi, now, do we?"
Carla sighs, shakes her head, and grumbles, "Alright. We--we certainly don't."
Before striding away, she fixes Cass with another pointed stare and mouths, "Behave."
Cassandra folds her arms and leans against the wall. Her eyes are focused on the Baali, who is surrounded by a gaggle of makeup artists.
Sven can be heard asking for everyone's number, "Write it down on your way out. I got a bit of an afterparty...and you're all invited. Because I do love my groupies. And I love 'em very, very much."
He gives them a big wink and a smarmy smile. Some of the MUAs blush and giggle amongst themselves. Once done, they quickly pack up their equipment and leave the room.
Cassandra is left alone with the Unholy Kindred. Sven gets out of the styling chair with a practiced and fluid motion- it's like he's done this so many times already. He inspects himself in the mirror, points finger guns at his reflection, winks, and grins a little.
He looks over his shoulder and then turns around to face Greg's Ghoul.
Sven is a certified heartthrob in his early-30s. His face is clean-shaven, angular in all the right ways, chiseled, and handsome. His lips are plump and very kissable. He's dashing, well-built, and has a certain boyish charm to him. His chin-length hair is black and swept back.
His dimples make his smile look quite appealing.
The Baali spreads his arms to either side of his body and says, "Hello, hello, hello. Name's Sven. So, you're Gregory's Ghoul, right?"
"Yeah."
Sven side-eyes her, "This is usually the time when the ladies- and the gents- would start swooning over me." He is wearing a very tight shirt with the topmost buttons undone. His sleeves are rolled up, revealing muscular arms.
His slacks look quite new, and his boots are pristinely polished. A pair of expensive-looking aviator sunglasses loosely hang from his shirt.
Cassandra shifts her weight from foot to foot and changes the subject, "I'm here on behalf of Gregory. He wants to make sure that you don't forget your end of the bargain. You owe him. So, he sent me to ensure that you hold up your end of the deal, yeah?"
Sven shrugs a shoulder and smirks, "I'm a man of my word. Cassandra, right?"
"Mm-hmm."
Sven exhales and takes a few steps closer to her, "Well, you've got a VIP pass. Want me to uh...autograph that for you? Give it a decade or 2 and that shit will skyrocket. You can sell it and get yourself all the uh...uh...tea and crumpets in the world, hahahaha!"
"I'm fine, thanks. I'm just here to--"
The Baali tuts, "Yeah, yeah, OK. You said it once and I got it. Anyways, wanna join me at the afterparty? Everyone's invited. Why? Cuz I show everyone- guys and girls- a good time." A seductive tone comes into his voice. He's oozing charm and charisma from every single pore of his dead skin. 
Cassandra looks away, "Ah. Eh...no. Y--you're bi?"
"Sure am. Old...colleague of mine used to say that he loved apples and oranges and hated choosing between them. Just love 'em all, y'know what I mean? You dig?"
Cassandra politely declines his offer, "I'll 'ave to say no. But thanks for the--the invite, yeah? And the VIP thing, too. Do you need any help around here?"
"Do you have a degree in event management?"
Cass shakes her head, "No. Why?"
Sven jerks his chin to the closed door that Carla walked out of, "Carla does, but she's got her plate full. Since you're here and so eager to lend a helping hand, go on and find her- tell her I sent you. I'm sure that she has plenty of shit for you to do, Cassie. Can I call you that?"
Cassie shrugs, "Makes no difference to me."
Sven tries his luck again by rubbing his palms and coming a lot closer to her, "So, about that afterparty...I've been showing my groupies so many things over the...decades. Yeah, I'm pretty fuckin' old, but I still got it. And you don't look like you're too far behind me."
Cassandra smiles tightly and politely deflects his advances, "Like I said, no thanks. Err...you're not me type."
Sven's mouth turns downward, "Aww. Well, I won't push you any further. But...my offer still stands, Cassie. And if you ever feel lonely, just walk in on us going at it like fuckin' hyenas. We'll always be glad to include another babe in the mix of bodies rubbing up against bodies." He winks and flashes her a big smile.
Cassandra's eyebrows go up, "Wow. That's...something. Do you mind if I ask you a few questions?"
The Demon Worshipper points finger guns at her, "Shoot. Pow-pow! Gotcha! Haha! Too slow on the draw. What do you wanna know, hmm? I'm a completely open book!" He sits down on the couch and leans back, crossing his legs and letting an arm casually drape on top of the headrest. His other hand rests on his knee.
Cassandra sits next to him and begins, "How do you know Gregory?"
Sven chuckles, "Ah, we go way back. He had an eye for art and shit like that, and I needed a way to dodge the taxman...or taxwoman. I snagged a few snazzy pieces from him, and we've kept in touch ever since. He's the best art dealer I ever did fuckin' meet, haha!"
"You--you're dodging your taxes?"
"So, what if I am, huh? Already paid my fair share when I could still breathe and get a suntan. Hey, did you know that I there was a time when a former assistant of mine too busy handling other stuff. He was so busy and became the reason why a show of mine wasn't held on a riverboat casino."
Sven wildly gesticulates and goes on, "A freaking riverboat casino! Do you have any idea how awesome that is?"
Cassandra cringes a little, "It's still not right to dodge the taxes, though. I mean, they go for--for lots of good things, yeah? Schools, roads, hospitals, and all sorts of public works, innit? I know that things are a bit dodgy in America, but...y'know. It's still a bit wrong."
Sven clicks his tongue and dismissively waves his hand, "Yeah, whatever. Listen, lady, I didn't come to the land of...high tea, chavs, and offensive comedy so that I could pay some more fucking taxes. I came here to perform, and I need to keep as much of my cash as possible, alright?"
Cassandra sighs and pursues another avenue of conversation, "OK. Fine. So...why rock music? What makes that particular genre so...important to you? Is it special to you in some way?"
"What is this, an--an interview? Y'know, Rolling Stone is gonna do a profile on me sometime soon. I even got fuckin' Vanity Fair on my ass. And I'm in, like, half-a-dozen BuzzFeed articles." He is very confident in himself and his abilities.
"It's just curiosity, yeah. The only Americans I know are--are the ones I see on telly or social media; I don't know any in-person, y'know? So, I just wanted to learn more about you, where you come from, why you do what you do, and so on."
Sven rubs his palms together, "Well, rock 'n' roll is where I see the dollar signs at. It's that simple, really."
Cassandra further inquires, "The ticket prices are a bit...much. With the soaring prices in the UK, aren't you a bit worried about losing fans? Aren't they concerned about that?"
Sven callously shrugs, "Their loss, not mine. I'm successful in spite of them, NOT because of them. My shit has broad appeal, so me losing out on a few poor kids with crooked teeth, mental problems, and a shitty haircut is a non-issue."
Wow. That is downright shitty!
Cassandra winces slightly, "Is--is it a charity? For a good cause, yeah? Is that why the ticket prices are higher than usual? Me favorite singer- Nicola Green, Soho Starlet- always does these charity performances where she raises a lot of money for good causes like cancer research, STEM education, youth scholarships, LGBT rights, and all sorts of other things."
Sven rolls his eyes, "I'm sure that Nicola Green is very charitable. But I roll differently. Success has and will always be my ultimate goal. And if I were to grade myself, I'd say A-fucking-plus-plus with a little golden star for good measure. Unlike...others, I had to start down from the fucking gutter, Cassie. The gutter!"
The Baali gets up from the couch and begins pacing around the room, "You have no idea what these past decades have been like for me. You have no fucking clue, and I don't expect you to understand what it's like, either, OK? My former...bandmate reveled in his success...and all the pussies and dicks that came along with it."
He clenches his fists and resumes his rant, "But me? I was thrown aside like--like I was nothing. But I'm something now, baby!! I'm a hell lot more than something!" A fiery intensity burns in his dead eyes. He is clearly consumed by ambition and greed.
His eyes widen even further, and he takes a few deep breaths, "Don't get in my way, OK? You...you seem like a nice enough gal. Hell, look at you, you're practically dressed like a freaking rockstar!"
"Hmm. I--I reckon that I do look pretty rad tonight, yeah?"
Sven grins, "Rad to the max, baby! So, I'll let you slide...for now. Don't worry, once I'm done dazzling the crowd with my legendary awesomeness, I'll help your boss out with that little problem of his. I never go back on my word." That last part has some insincerity to it, and Cassandra can sense it.
Cassandra corrects him, "He's not me boss, he's me Domitor. There's a big difference, Sven."
Sven holds out his hands in a placating gesture, "Oh, yeah. Of--of course. Sorry. My bad."
"It's fine, it's fine. Is Carla your Ghoul?"
Sven's face scrunches in disgust, "Carla? That nag? No fucking way! I usually burn through assistants/managers faster than I burn through boyfriends and girlfriends. If I'm lucky, I date them, but...Carla's probably gonna see the pink slip before this year is over, haha!"
Cassandra shakes her head in disapproval, "Why do you treat them that way?"
"Because I can. Because it's a part and parcel of my whole persona. Do you know how many lovesick teenagers have posters of my immortal face on their bedroom walls, Cass? Can I call you Cass? Yeah, that's what I thought." He puffs out his chest in pride.
Cassandra lowers her gaze and silently wonders to herself, "What the 'ell did I get meself into now? Cor blimey, I'd take fighting another Wight over...whatever this is shaping up to be, yeah? Oh well, I did say that I was willing to help out Gregory with whatever he needs done. So, lucky me."
Sven snaps his fingers and gets the Ghoul's attention, "Yo! Missy miss! You with me or are you zoning out. Shit, we usually start popping pills only AFTER the concert is done. Well, except for me. I take mine straight from the veins, heh-heh."
The Toreador Ghoul heaves a heavy sigh and keeps listening to Sven's self-important bragging.
He proceeds, "You know, I once fed from a guy who was so high on ecstasy. Wowee! I saw shit that'd make a fucking Malkavian think twice, haha! And...and then I bit into a groupie who snorted up a few lines of cocaine which I myself had laid down. Boy, those were the nights, huh?"
Cassandra nervously shifts on her seat, "Uh-huh. You really like wild partying, eh?"
Sven continues to run his mouth, "I've been asked to guest-star in a few episodes of some popular reality and TV shows! I can't say which ones, though." He mimics zipping his mouth, locking it, and tossing the key away.
Cassandra rubs the base of her neck and queries, "How did you arrive here, anyway?"
The Baali boasts, "Private plane. Bought it from a Setite for a very reasonable price- 5.2 mil, paid all cash, no biggie."
Cassandra's gasps and thinks to herself, "Cor blimey! He's...posh. Ah, great. Now, I really need to find a way out of here...or hold me breath and put up with it until the bloody concert is over."
Sven does a horrible imitation of a British accent when asking, "A penny for your thoughts?"
Cassandra musters up a smile and replies, "It's nothing, really. What are your plans for after the concert? Will you be able to see Greg and help him out with--"
"Ah, ah, ah, ah, ah. I'll see your Domitor when I'm ready, whenever I feel like it. I might owe the guy, but that doesn't mean he dictates my itinerary. On these trips, I have a very meticulous schedule...and it mostly involves partying all night, hahaha!"
Cass shakes her head and rises to her feet, "Fine, then. If that's what you want, then--then fine. But you 'ave to help Greg, yeah?" She fixes him with a slightly stern stare.
Sven holds up his hands and smiles, "Sure, sure. Now, concert's scheduled to begin in...a few minutes. Or I might just let the sardines bake for a few more minutes. It's all a part of the Sven experience. The Svenxperience! It really rolls of the tongue, doesn't it?"
Cassandra shrugs and stifles a grimace, "I'm sure it does. I should find Carla and see if she could use a hand, eh?"
Sven places his hand on her shoulder and gives it a firm squeeze, "Before you go, holders of VIP passes- like yourself- are usually allowed to bring a plus-1 along. Free of charge and all that jazz, of course. Do you have anybody whom you'd like to invite to this once-in-a-lifetime experience?"
The only person who immediately comes to mind is Amy. She does fancy rock music, so maybe she'd like to tag along. But then again, she might be busy or...this whole concert may very well end up turning into a dreadful flop.
Cassandra gulps and stammers, "Err...there may be someone, yeah. Can I go outside and give her a quick call, see if she's interested in coming along?"
Sven smoothly says, "By all means. And if you find Carla, tell her to go back to the hotel and get my bathrobe. Tell her that it's the silky red one with the 'K' monogrammed on the chest, NOT the aqua-colored one given as a gift from the Sheikh. She'll know what I'm talking about, heh-heh."
Cassandra sucks some air through her teeth, "Alrighty then. I'll make sure to pass on the message. Any--anything else?"
Sven still has his mind on the bathrobe, "I want to change into something more comfortable when the afterparty starts. Hmm? What was that?"
She stifles a sigh and forces herself to be polite, "Will there be anything else before I leave, Sven?"
Sven shakes his head and says, "Nah, I'm good. Enjoy the concert, OK? Because I am going to blow your fucking minds away! Socks, too! You'll need a new pair by the time I'm done, hahaha!"
While leaving the room, Cass mutters to herself, "We'll see about that."
She keeps walking till she finds Carla near the main stage. She's loudly ordering the sound techs to do a mic check. Once everything is to her satisfaction, she turns around and sees Cassandra.
"Oh, it's you. And your clothes are still on. I'm surprised, honestly. What do want? VIP seating's--"
"Sven told me to give you a helping hand."
"He--he did? Oh, well...I see. How...thoughtful of him."
"So, what do you need?"
Carla licks her lips and mulls things over, "There's a coffeeshop a few miles away. Go there and grab me the tallest possible cup of caramel macchiato. Make sure that they use goat's milk instead of cow's milk, got it?"
