"Hmm. Yes. But you must be prepared for anything."
Gregory takes his place in the backseat of the Lexus and idly watches the city going by in a blur of bright colors, flashing lights, rainfall, and throngs of people seeking to evade the weather.
At a traffic jam, their attention is drawn to a bright sign on the side of a building. Cassandra grunts and slowly edges the car forward.
Gregory's face twists as he stares at the sign in question. It's some kind of advert encouraging people to vote Conservative. There are a few things that Greg strongly despises in this world.
The Thatcher government (and the subsequent horrors that followed) are fairly high up on his list. He despises the Tories with a burning passion befitting of a Toreador. Even till now, he resents the Tories who are the face of austerity, barbaric slashing of welfare, a disregard for the underprivileged, poisoning of the body politic, and the dulling of public consciousness (amongst other things).
That's not to say that fucking Labor is any better- they are, if nothing, the enablers of such horrendousness. Both are equally bad, in his opinion. 2 cheeks of the same arse.
(And don't get him started on the fringe parties like the bloody UKIP. As for the Lib-Dems and Greens, they are essentially non-entities to him.)
Cassandra, more or less, shares the political sentiments of her Domitor. Over the years, she has become somewhat disillusioned with how rapidly to the right Labor has been lurching, making it easy for the Tories to gut the NHS and privatize everything under the sun, moon, and stars.
And the ruthless cutting of benefits (especially disability-adjacent ones) always hurts her greatly. Add to that the foul demonization of benefits claimants, the increasing unemployment, and the concurrent gutting of council houses, and then one has a rather dire situation brewing.
Cassandra puts her foot down on the gas pedal and asks, "Do you still vote, Greg?"
"No. And even if there was anyone to vote for, my identification would undoubtedly incur the wrong kind of attention and raise many an eyebrow."
"Hmm. I--I don't even recognize Labor these days, yeah? Many of me mates barely recognize what it's turning into or--or the direction it's taking. It was shocking when a twat like Tony fucking Blair won. Cor blimey!"
"Yes. But you have to remember 1 thing."
She looks up at the rare view mirror and prompts her Domitor to continue, "Go on."
Gregory pointedly reminds her, "In 2002, Margaret Thatcher- that old axe-faced harridan- unambiguously boasted that it was Tony Blair and New Labor that were her greatest achievements. THAT should've been your wake-up call, Cassandra."
The Ghoul merely nods her head up and down. The remainder of the drive is silent and devoid of conversation.
Alistair lives in a neighborhood where the houses are practically stuck together. Wrought iron gates, flower beds, and big trees line the way. Some have small and modest gardens out in the front. Others have a stone path.
The streetlamps seem to be in good working order. Nice, neat, and tidy. So far.
Cassandra pulls over and watches as Gregory gets out. The rain is slightly harder now. The Toreador opens his umbrella and briskly walks off to his destination.
Once he's inside, Cassandra starts driving towards her next destination. Greg told her everything that she needed to know, nay an iota more than that.
The Ghoul soon finds herself in a spot frequented by uni students- and those who fancy themselves young enough to pass as such. Shops, small venues, clubs, record stores, etc. Most places have a warm and inviting aura emanating from the walls themselves.
Cassandra was instructed to wait at a restaurant with sparse and cozy indoor decorations and comfy wooden furnishings. Varieties upon of varieties of freshly baked cookies are for sale at prices deemed somewhat reasonable. Cassandra peruses through the handwritten menu while waiting for the Osirian named Callum to show up.
Greg merely said, "You'll know him when you see him. And he'll know you when he sees you." When questioned for clarity, Cass only received vague half-answers and dry shrugs from him.
Him speaking cryptically or having that withdrawn (some may say "jaded") attitude is nothing quite new, of course. It's been like that ever since Cassandra started regularly sipping Blood from Gregory's wrists.
After a minute or 2, a young lad in his 20s walks into the establishment. It's like the whole place lights up when he sets foot inside. There is something undeniably familiar and pleasant- almost calming, soothing, or something to that effect- emanating from him.
He has shaggy black hair, porcelain skin, a big nose, sharp green eyes, and a rather lithe frame. His lips are dry and chapped. He politely nods over to the plump lady behind the counter, greets a couple, and makes his way over to Cassandra.
His backpack is lazily flung across a bony shoulder. His clothing consists of a t-shirt that looks way older than him, corduroy trousers, and a patchy open jacket that's more threads than actual cloth. In 1 of the side pockets are dozens of coins rattling together as he sits across her.
Cassandra checks to make sure that he is who he says he is by slowly asking, "Callum?"
The Osirian vampire nods his head and sets his backpack on the floor. He then folds his fingers and stares intensely into the other party's hazel eyes.
Cassandra smiles a little, "Children of Osiris?"
"The very same," Callum responds with a nasally, Scouser accent.
Cassandra's eyes dart around the area briefly. Lively conversations, a mounted telly playing some old footie match, laughter from children in another corner of the joint, servers hurrying and skipping and jumping between customers.
The usual.
Callum looks over his shoulder and chuckles, "Yeah. I usually come here to study. I've been told that their cookies are good. So, you're Gregory's aide, hm?"
"Ghoul."
"Same thing in my eyes. So, you got the stuff?"
Cass nods her head, "It's in a safe place. I was given strict instructions to hand it over to you lot- Greg said that you'd know what it all is, what to do with it, where to move it to, and so on? Right?"
Callum shrugs, "Eh, yeah. Sure. Any reason why the Toreador sent you in his place? Is he busy?"
"Sort of, yeah. I've been authorized to act, speak, and negotiate on his behalf."
Callum seems impressed, "You 'ave? Wow. He must really trust you if he put YOU up for all of that, mate." He smacks his lips, reaches inside a backpack, and procures a worn notebook.
Cassandra stares at it and inquires, "So, you're a student, eh? What do you do?"
The Osirian snickers, "I've been a uni student for the past 15 years. Me graduation's right around the corner- again. So far, nobody's been any the wiser. But the Masquerade's getting quite tricky to uphold in this new decade, I'll tell you that much! And what's your story?"
Cassandra narrows her eyes, "Greg never talked about me with you lot?"
"Nope. Strictly business, that one. But your Domitor is a cordial and understanding man all the same."
"Fair enough. Uh, yeah, I'm his Ghoul and he's me Domitor. There's nothing more to it than that, really."
Callum nods slowly, "Word has been travelling far and wide of a Toreador Ghoul who inflicted Final Death--"
Cassandra groans and completes his sentence, "On a Wight. A Brujah Elder named Winston. Yes. I--I did. No, I'm not exactly proud of destroying him."
Callum regards her with great interest, "How intriguing. Back in my...err...Fledgling nights, I knew of Ghouls who went around destroying Kindred for the usual reasons: fun, sport, a desire to be free of their tyrannical Domitors, to feed their fatal addiction to the Vitae of our kind, and so forth. And yet you've not quite followed your predecessors into that dark and bloody abyss."
"Everybody's different- circumstances, outlooks, situations, preferences. You can't exactly paint in broad strokes and lump all Ghouls into 1 category. Do you want the items or not?"
Callum extends his cold hand, palm facing outward, "Patience. I just want to know more about you. A friendly curiosity, that's all. What do you know of the Osirians, hmm?"
"Quite a bit, actually. You're an interesting lot, actually. You can't Embrace or make any Ghouls because your Blood's been cursed or something like that, yeah? So, you're stuck with mostly recruiting Thin-Bloods, Caitiffs, and other castaways to keep your numbers steady."
Callum's eyebrows shoot up in genuine surprise, "How impressive! You seem to know quite a bit about our Bloodline. Did your Domitor teach you all of this, hm?"
"Eh, not exactly him. But he paid for me lessons. How do you fit into all of this, anyway? What's your role within...you're not a cult, are you? You're not trying to recruit me OR GREG into any sort of weird cult, are ya?"
Callum smiles and plainly says, "Not at all. That's more the Ministry's style. And if you know about the Serpents, then you have a fuller picture of the extent of our...struggles."
Cassandra mumbles, "Yeah, it sounds more like a 1-sided slaughter. How the 'ell are you lot still kicking? How are your Temple leaders- the ones who are on the path to mastering Bardo- able to keep tabs on everything?"
Callum chuckles slowly, "Wow. You know about the Discipline of Bardo, too. We manage? Why? How? Because we have the Light inside of us. The very same Light that the Setites Abhor. And therein lies the source of their hatred for us and all who bear the Blood of Osiris."
He leans closer and asks, "Are you familiar with the old song known as 'This Little Light Of Mine'?"
Cassandra lightly scoffs, "Never went to Sunday School growing up. Or church, for that matter. I'm an atheist. But I've heard of that song in passing. Why? What does it have to do with anything?"
Callum expounds, "Prior to my Embrace, I was of...born into a different persuasion than yours. At school, we'd sing that little song almost daily. Anyways, things change. The world moves on and I...went along for the joyride, you could say. I am now of a different persuasion- in terms of faith and the like- but that little song from my childhood days always comes back to me."
Cassandra tilts her head and follows up, "Yeah? I can see why it comes back. All that talk of light and letting it shine, yeah?"
"Not just any light. But THE Light. The Light that our enemies Abhor..."
He lowers his voice to a whisper, "The Light that is capable of destroying them on the spot. It is said to be as bright as the sun itself."
"Sounds...err..."
Callum leans back and takes a look at the menu. He tosses it aside and shrugs, "Hm. Prices seem to have shot up. Again. Now, you were saying?"
Cassandra gulps and notes, "Sounds a bit destructive, innit? The power to eradicate a whole Clan with...however this 'Light' manifests itself through you lot. Then how are you any better than the Setites so keen on eradicating your kind? It doesn't sound right or fair to me."
She puts in, "I--I mean, I get that you're being hunted down and aren't on a level field here, but still. Trying to eradicate an entire Clan as powerful as the Followers of Set doesn't sound very nice or just, yeah?"
Callum nods, "I will concede that the methodology is flawed in certain aspects. But the goal is noble, nonetheless: erase the devious Typhonists with the very Light that they Abhor. They don't just Abhor bright lights, mind you. The Light within us Osirians is a Final Death sentence for them."
"Cor blimey. It's proper complicated, innit? I dunno, though..."
Callum observes, "You have doubts. It would be my pleasure to illuminate you, Cassandra."
Cassandra softly chuckles, "I'm sure it would. What's your role in all of this? You haven't been a Kindred for that long; so, it's safe to assume that you haven't exactly mastered Bardo like the rest of your counterparts. Right?"
"True. Indeed, I haven't. But you should understand that mastering the Bardo Discipline isn't about memorizing tomes' worth of ancient glyphs and stringing them all together to form incoherent babbles. It's far simpler than that, really."
Cassandra rolls her shoulder, "Oh?"
"Mm-hmm. It's about keeping our Humanity high and denying the Beast within. 'Beasts we are, lest Beasts we become.' Isn't that the common refrain among the other Clans and Bloodlines? For us, it's all about the Light."
Cassandra fixes him with a sympathetic look, "How do you manage? You, what? Don't drink blood from unwilling people? You find other means to--"
Callum vaguely responds, "We have our ways. Our Gangrel friends at this city's zoo are kind enough to provide us with animals which prove to be just as nourishing as human vessels- and far less messy, haha!"
Cassandra concedes, "You've got a good point there, yeah. I suppose that's far more ethical. What if someone- all dazed and confused like- came up and offered their wrist to you? Would you drink from them?"
Callum's smile fades, "You're talking about Blood Dolls, aren't you? Wherein the lines between willing and unwilling become blurred. Hm. Yes. That's an ethical and moral gray area that's riddled with all kinds of pitfalls. It's hard to tell whether they're eager or hopelessly addicted to the Kiss. Eh, for me, it's an absolute 'no.'"
Cassandra queries, "What do you hope to achieve or--or get out of all of this. From what I've learned, you don't seem interested in Golconda or any of that."
Callum clasps his cold hands together, "This is no mere pursuit undertaken for laughs. After all, I myself am planning a pilgrimage to Egypt."
Cassandra's jaw drops, "What? How? Egypt is the heart of Setite territory! How are you planning on sneaking into their Domain, anyway?"
The vampire simply smiles, "The Light of Osiris guides me. It illuminates the path before me. Oh, and I'll be going on foot."
"Not even bicycle?"
"Not even bicycle."
The Toreador Ghoul exhales and remarks, "Cor blimey! And--and no one's forcing you into this, yeah? You're doing this of your own volition?"
Callum's eyes drop to the faded notebook on the table, "Yes. No coercion. My mates at the Temple strongly believe that I am ready for such an undertaking. I am to leave in another few nights. I will have to hunt, seek my own lodgings before the sun rises, and so on. But I have every hope that the journey itself should go well."
Cassandra's brows knit together and she gets a tad concerned, "What are you going to do when- or--or if- you reach Egypt proper?"
The Osirian softly sings, "This little light o' mine, I'm goin' let it shine / Let it shine, let it shine, let it shine / Ev'rywhere I go, I'm goin' let it shine..."
After he finishes singing, he chuckles, "I'm not a wind-up bomb being sent on a 1-way suicide mission. Relax. But I have my sacred duties to carry out."
Cass further queries, "Aren't you worried about attracting attention to yourself? The wrong kind, in particular?"
Callum snickers, "What? 'White boy walking to Egypt from England'? I suppose the tabloids would go mental if they ever caught word of this, hahaha! I, like any other sensible Kindred, uphold the Masquerade. It's the only true thing that binds us and keeps us all together regardless of Clan, Sect affiliation, Bloodline, or Generation."
"Even your arch-nemeses?"
"Even our arch-nemeses."
Cassandra changes the subject, "What are you currently studying these nights, anyway?"
Callum opens his notebook and jabs his thumb at several pages, "Uhhh, economics, a bit of calculus, some art, music, and some psychology on the side. The nights are long...and productive. But my calling is my topmost priority."
Cassandra incredulously asks, "You got time for...ALL of that?"
Callum responds, "You make the time, Cassandra. And it helps that I am now in a position where I'm fully able to fund my own education. I wish that were the case for others who are in my perceived age."
He gazes out the window and lets a shadow of sadness engulf his pale features, "Education, night by passing night, is increasingly becoming a luxury that the offspring of the commoner can no longer afford. Like a lot of other things, it increasingly falls into the purview of the obscenely wealthy."
Cassandra's expression turns morose, "Mm-hmm. All those pointless budget cuts over the past few years. Forcing a lot of kids out into the job market before they're even fully prepared. And there are barely any jobs to--to speak of."
Callum looks at her and nods slowly, "Indeed. Unlike the Ministry which preaches retreat from the world, the Osirians DO contend with the world and its many contradictions. To stiffen one's neck and look away from the current state of affairs would be the height of dishonesty, inhumanity, cruelty, and...laziness."
He adds, "The British Empire might no longer exist in name- but her demise has had grave and far-reaching consequences. And more so for her former subjects than for us. I'll be seeing a lot of it firsthand- I've only read books and seen documentaries. But to see it up-close would be a completely different experience."
Cassandra exhales, "I can tell that you're looking forward to your trip, yeah."
Callum stifles a wince, "Well, I am still a condemned nocturnal predator who forever subsists on the blood of living creatures and has to hide himself from the blazing hot sun. And let's not forget the extreme temperatures, haha!"
"Or the wood. Are you ready for this?"
"Of course, I am. There are just a few more arrangements that need to be made and I shall soon be taking off."
Out of concern for the lad, Cass prods, "You got a--a bag packed? Clothes? Cash? You--you 'ave some sort of a plan, right? You're not making it up as you go along, I--I hope."
"Far from it, Cassandra. Far from it. The Light guides my path, but I'm no fool. I have a competent plan in place. I will have a supply of animal's blood for emergencies, various translation apps installed on my iPhone, trustworthy contacts in the various Domains that I shall be passing through, and...a bit of faith, if you will."
Cassandra bites her lip, "I can't help but get this feeling that all is not as it seems."
Callum shrugs, "It's probably just nerves, then. If anything, I'm more worried about my uni's Egyptology professor."
"What makes him so special?"
"He is...of our Blood, in a way. And a damn good Egyptologist. 1 of the foremost experts in his field. My Temple tasked me with keeping an eye on him. I've done well for this past decade since we met. Or so I'd hope. After I leave, the other Osirians will have to find a suitable replacement to watch over him without drawing any undue attention."
Cassandra scrunches her face and questions, "Why were you tasked with looking after him, anyway? He--he can't take care of himself?"
Callum patiently explains, "The threats against our kind are numerous. Mortals, other Kindred, those werewolves in London, androids, Mages, Wraiths, and so much more. The list hardly ever ends! No offense, but even Ghouls like you have it out for...the Blood, more so than the individual in whose body it resides."
Cassandra blinks and feebly chuckles, "Yeah, I--I suppose so. What makes that particular bloke so bloody special, then?"
"Like I said: he's a damn good Egyptologist. Officially, the Ministry- as a whole- has declared a Blood Hunt for him. He's a virtual pariah and is practically untouchable within the confines of this Domain, thank Osiris. But outside its borders? He's fair game, I'm afraid."
Callum rubs his tired eyes and continues, "Our Temple also feels that he is rather close to uncovering the actual location of Set-Apep's resting place. If we find where the Setite Antediluvian's true resting place is, then maybe we can end things without too much...bloodshed. And maybe then, the Undying King will smile upon us."
A twinkle of hope flicks across his dead eyes.
Cassandra tilts her head and presses her lips into a thin line, "I reckon, yeah. Sounds better than turning the brightness up on a bunch of people- even if they are wankers- and watching them turn into a pile of ash, innit?"
Callum's chuckle is brief, "The desirable outcome, yes. And along the way, I'll help transport some of those valuable goods that your Domitor has entrusted us with."
"Any idea what, why, where, when, and how they'll be used?"
"I know as much as you do, I'm afraid. But I can assure you that they'll be used to bring Light into an increasingly...muddled world. I'd like to confirm that you do indeed have the goods before informing my fellow Osirians. Do you mind showing them to me?"
Cassandra nods and gets up from her seat, "'Course. It's in me Lexus. You mind if I order a few things before we head out?"
Callum equably says, "By all means. I'll wait for you outside." The coins in his pocket jingle when he rises up and leaves the establishment, his notebook tucked securely under his cold arm.
Cassandra licks her lips and decides to order an assortment of cookies- blueberry, choco-chip, red velvet, and drop sugar. She also inquires if any of them contain nuts, because Amy has a nut allergy. The smiling server confirms that none of them do.
After paying with her card, she thanks the server with a broad smile and takes the large bag with her, stepping out onto the wet street.
Callum is on the other side, bending over and dropping a few coins into the chipped tumbler of a homeless boy who's sitting on a small blanket. As Cassandra nears, she picks up on a fragment of their hushed conversation.
"Get yourself a warm place, mate," the vampire gently tells the unhoused mortal.
Out of pity, Cassandra reaches into her bag and gives the poor boy some cookies along with some spare quid, "Here. This should also help for a while."
All the boy does is smile with his broken teeth and nod. The sign next to him reads: "MUTE. Kicked off benefits suddenly :-( Could use some spare £. Bless u."
He timidly waves goodbye and dives into the food as Callum and Cassandra make their way to her parked car.
Cass wipes the tear away from her eye and grimaces, "It's rough out there, innit?"
"Sadly so."
The Ghoul opens the door of her car and ushers the vampire into the backseat. He gets in and settles himself in, wiggling around a fair bit. Given the rain, Cassandra opts to drive to a more secluded locale rather than simply hand the goods off over here. She starts the car and they take off.
Callum stares out the window, an inscrutable look playing on his features. Cass rounds a corner and finds a spot in an underground parking structure that has this bright greenish light, rough concrete walls, a handful of parked cars, and an unusual amount of smoke coming from 1 of the vents on the far side.
She stops, turns the ignition off, and fishes her phone out of her purse. A text from Amy, which simply reads: "Hey, luv. Just came back frm work. Going for a bath now, will b thinking about u throughout :-)"
Cass smiles softly and texts back: "Think about me all u want, sweets <3"
After sending that text, she sends another: "Got some cookies 4 u. No nuts in any of them, promise, I checked. Fancy sharing them sometime soon?"
Amy responds: ":-O sounds like a proper treat! And I would love to do far more than...share cookies with u. Maybe we can 'share' 1 together, eh?"
Cassandra bites her lower lip and texts back: "Well, aren't u cheeky. TTYL, bye!"
"You're just as cheeky, u minx. CYA! MWAH! Kissies!"
Cassandra puts her phone away and looks at the rearview mirror, "Sorry about that. Err...a--a friend of mine."
Callum shrugs, "You owe me no explanation, Cassandra. May I now see the goods?" His tired eyes seem to have rekindled with an uncharacteristic gleam.
Cassandra pops the trunk and steps out into the lit structure; Callum pushes the door open and follows suit. His eyes immediately fall upon a bulky tome with the Eye of Ra on its cover, a black folder containing several pages, and a strange, elongated metallic rod with an ornate jewel at 1 end and several more jewels embedded along the sides.
He picks up the bulky book and begins thumbing through it, softly mumbling something or the other as his look slowly shifts from approving to distressed. His jaw goes a bit slack, too.
Cass squints, "What's wrong?"
"Our...Egyptologist would be over the moon with this stuff, no doubt about it. But it's also concerning. The Setites are said to be developing a new Discipline called 'Corruption'. It's a foul and dark perversion of Presence."
The Osirian mumbles on, "So, the rumors are true, then. This doesn't bode well for...us." His eyes wander from the book to the metallic rod, and then to the folder.
He carefully takes the rod out and gives it a close examination, a small smile slowly creeping across his face. He nods firmly and sets it down before taking the folder into his hands.
He opens it up and begins going through the pages, his face flickering between suspicion, intrigue, and something else entirely. With every page that he flips through, his expression changes accordingly.
Cassandra interrupts his train of thought by asking, "You're OK with acting as couriers for--for all of this stuff?"
While still having his eyes glued to the pages, Callum responds, "Why wouldn't we be? We owe your Domitor greatly? By the way, tell him to pop by our ceremonies sometimes- he knows the addresses very well."
He looks up from his perusal and fixes Cass with a sympathetic look, "And the invitation is cordially extended to you as well. On my behalf. And...no, it doesn't quite involve a lot of spiritual stuff. Also, I have to reiterate this: we're not a cult and we're most certainly not looking to sell you on some...scheme or the other. You can even come by our Temple unaccompanied and be treated just as fairly as you would in any other place."
All the Ghoul can say as way of response is, "I'll think about it, yeah."
Out of concern for the Osirian, Cassandra asks, "You're not the only one who'll be moving this stuff around, right? And it isn't too risky?"
Callum shrugs and turns a page, "The bulk of these goods will be transported in larger caravans where they'll be hidden away amongst more...conventional fare. I'm hardly the only Kindred courier in play. Risks come and go, but the Light illuminates our way and banishes all those who seek to do us harm."
Cassandra raises her eyebrows, "Cor blimey. And it's all over the world? Not just Egypt?"
"Mm-hmm. Us Osirians are everywhere on nights such as these. Worry not, the Light within us- honed by practice of Bardo- is the same Light that our foes greatly Abhor because it would obliterate them on contact. I myself have been trained in harnessing only a fraction of it, so I'm fairly safe from the threats that I shall inevitably come across."
A chill goes down Cassandra's spine as she proceeds to ask, "What about other threats? SI? Non-Ministry Kindred? Garou? Your walking pilgrimage is hardly risk-free, innit?" She winces a bit and looks around the structure.
"Come what may, I shall reach my intended destination with my head still attached to my body. I already told you: I've planned this out. But I do appreciate your concern. It's a sign that your Humanity is intact." Callum's eyes crinkle a bit as he grins.
Cass gesticulates and queries, "Why not just bike over there? It's a lot faster, yeah?"
The Osirian chuckles, "What? Like those YouTubers? Nah. I know another member of ours who decided to drive to Egypt- in her station wagon from the 1950s. The story of her journey is quite riveting. But I've made my choice- I'm walking there. The Blood and the Light are sufficient. And a traveler's card, haha!"
"And she made it there in--in 1 piece?"
"It happened a decade ago- when things were a lot different than they are now. But yes, she's well-adjusted and...back in the comfort of the Gloughchester Temple. By 2020, she plans to do another pilgrimage."
Cass is a bit awestruck, "Wow."
"Yes, indeed!" Callum shuts the black folder and firmly nods.
From there, the 2 of them make small talk and go over a few more things before parting ways. Callum says that he'll get an Uber to his current location through an app installed on his iPhone- quite the resourceful lad!
(One wouldn't want to be a Setite encountering the blinding Light which has been harnessed to obliterate their kind, specifically).
By way of farewell, he tells her, "May the Grand, Resurrected, Undying King illuminate your path and provide clarity like none other in times of uncertainty, doubt, and confusion. May the Light surround you in its bliss. And take care."
At Alistair's Haven...
The interior of the former doctor's 2-storey house is fairly demure and modest. Carpeted flooring, wallpaper from last century, sturdy (but old) wooden furniture, all bathed in a soft amber light.
Alistair is dressed in a long robe of pure black and has a lit pipe hanging from his mouth. He puffs away and asks his fellow Toreador, "So, old boy, how else has everything been on your end?"
Gregory shifts around on his plush armchair and stifles a groan, "The usual. I have Cassandra doing what needs to be done- on my behalf and with my full authorization, of course. So far, she is proving to be quite effective in her work. I tell her only what she needs to know and leave her to it."
"Hmm," Alistair emits some smoke through his nostrils, "She seems like a decent sort. You know, old boy, work has been...depressing as of late."
"I can imagine. All those blasted cuts to our NHS- our crowning jewel. It's a fucking travesty. How are you even holding on?"
Alistair's chuckle is dry, "That's the thing! We're not! You mightn't be privy to this, but maternal mortality rates have shot up! Shot up! In a developed nation like ours! Funding shortfalls, staff shortages, burnouts- all of these contribute to the maternal mortality rates. Ghastly!"
The ex-doctor shudders and fiddles with his pipe, "I see it all, old boy. The baggy eyes of those hard-working nurses- bless their hearts. The doctors whose tempers are getting shorter and shorter than that of a Brujah's and don't even get me started on how we have to go and grovel before this and that vulture for funds."
Gregory sighs deeply, "I can only imagine. These things don't just happen on their own, Alistair..."
Alistair wags a finger at Greg, "I know what you're going to say, old boy. And having seen it all, I wholeheartedly agree with your sober assessment. This is the work of malign agents from all across the ol' political spectrum."
Gregory mumbles, "It's hardly a spectrum when they're all varying flavors of conservativism." He says that last word with unmitigated disgust and contemptuous disdain. His nostrils flare up, too.
For a few moments, a silence lingers along with the acrid, pungent, and smoke-laden air. Gregory gazes up at the wall, where there are several pictures of a black cat.
He breaks the silence by asking, "Alistair? Why the pipe? It does nothing to us either way."
Alistair chuckles feebly, "Call it my Aesthetic Fixation of the week, old boy. I caught glimpse of it on the telly and I...simply had to have it, if you catch my drift. I had to. I like the way it looks, and it certainly smells a lot better!"
He leans closer and taps the side of his nose, "It takes a Toreador to know the struggles of the Blood, don't you agree?"
Greg dryly responds, "Yes. I concur."
Alistair's smile broadens, "Speaking of. You really ought to do some mingling with the other Toreadors here, old boy. What's the worst that could come out of it. Yes, yes, I know- you withdrew your application from the Gloughchester Bridge Party. But that's no excuse to withdraw yourself entirely! Our Clan is a vibrant lot, and our special Coterie finds new purposes in these trying times."
(Gregory is not too fond of many fellow Aesthetes for a mélange of reasons that vary from legitimate to downright petty).
Regardless, he says, "I'll give it some consideration. After coming here from London, I didn't see much of a point in keeping in contact with any of them, aside from a select few exceptions."
Alistair smacks his lips and offers, "Perhaps I can...introduce you to some of the prominent ones in the Toreador social scene?"
"If it's all the same to you, I'd very much prefer to stay away from those exact types. Gloughchester was me- and Cassandra- getting away from the pointless mind games, centuries-old feuds, and powerplays that the others are oft wont to indulge in. The kinds that usually end up with people dying, getting harmed...or worse."
Alistair blows out some smoke and wags his pipe at Gregory, "Right you are! I quite understand what you're talking about, old boy. And in a Domain as London, I can only imagine how intense it all was."
Gregory grimly notes, "But the Garou wiped that all away in mere weeks. Centuries of accumulated power, wealth, and influence all rent asunder by...werewolves."
Alistair sighs and laments, "Oh, great and mighty City of London. Londinium! City of kings, queens, nobles, and commoners alike. Of diplomats, bankers, bureaucrats, emissaries, and those who they're obliged to work with. The City of Villages Where Royalty Lives. Now under the occupation of Gaia's strongest creatures- or so I'm told."
Gregory briefly chuckles at his friend waxing poetic, "All the plays you've attended over the decades have certainly paid off. I can't even remember the last time I attended a play and actually...liked it. The last one I went to failed to rouse my spirits or captivate my attention, sadly."
Alistair slowly sighs, "Ah. I can relate. I myself went through a bit of that ol' dry spell back in the 1990s. But I found ways to reinvigorate the experience- wholly ethical, decent, and somewhat humane measures, for what it's worth."
"I'll...take your word for it."
"Would YOU be interested in those, by any chance? Perhaps something small-scale but not without its fair share of theatrics? Far be it from me, of all people, to push somebody into doing something that they don't want to do."
Gregory gives a tight-lipped smile and says, "Perhaps I'll give it some thought over the coming nights."
Alistair suggests, "And you really should get to know the others around here, old boy. I know that you and Cassandra haven't been around for THAT long, but still. You have to fit in. Eventually. Both you and your faithful Ghoul. She is...devoted to you, isn't she?"
"I believe that she is, yes. Granted, owing to my Generation and my lack of interest in pursuing any Disciplines, I would surmise that my bond with my Ghoul tends to be a bit looser than some other Kindred would like."
Alistair exhales some pungent pipe smoke through his nostrils and blinks. He waves his pipe in a semi-circle and states, "What Generation were you again?"
"12th. Which only, amongst other things, serves to make Embraces virtually impossible, if not downright risky."
"Oh, yes. Of course. As it is, your Blood's Thinned...unless..." Alistair gives his fellow Artisan a cheeky and knowing look.
Gregory shakes his head, "Absolutely not. I have not committed Diablerie. And I don't intend to. My allegiance, since the time of my Embrace, has always been to the Ivory Tower and its Traditions."
Alistair stifles a yawn and changes the topic by asking, "And why don't you want to learn a Discipline, hmm?"
"Because I see it as a waste of time and resources. And I suppose that I lack the motivation for it, or at least so I'm led to believe. For now, there is only 1 thing at the forefront of my mind."
"Hmm? Only 1?"
Gregory plainly nods, "Yes. Getting my affairs in order. And I intend to do it via Cassandra."
Alistair nods understandingly, "Quite an undertaking on her part, isn't it?"
"She is...adequately prepared for what may come her way. Thus far, she has proven herself beyond capable of handling what has come her way- while always remaining humble and cognizant of the fact that she is but a Ghoul within the Camarilla's structure."
Alistair congratulates him, "Splendid, splendid!"
He then puffs his pipe again and says, "I myself have been busy- on many fronts, really. Might I interest you in a wholesome game of bridge tomorrow night? No pressure to join in, of course. Bring your Cassandra along if you wish. She can mingle with the other Ghouls in attendance, haha!"
Gregory narrows an eye and lifts his chin, "What's the worst that could happen at such an event?"
"The worst thing that can happen is losing your temper and violating the Masquerade! The Prince will be in attendance, but we all know that he's harmless as far as Princes go."
Greg grunts softly, "He's a child for god's sake. 16-years-old at the time of his Embrace. What the hell was his Sire thinking?"
The NHS admin leans in closer, looks around, and lowers his voice, "Between you and me, old boy, the Prince doesn't quite run the show in Gloughchester. He may be of our Blood, but our Blood...are the ones with their hands on the actual reins, so to speak."
Alistair holds out his hands, "Anyway! Err...forget I--I said that. Or try not to give it much thought, eh? The point is, whoever has their hands on this Domain's levers and important buttons, they've all kept the peace for this long and have protected us from any major threats."
Gregory sighs harshly, "It still doesn't excuse the fact that a child- forever 16 years of age- runs this city. Your implications are disturbing, to say the least. Why would the real rulers of this Domain have a boy as a figurehead? What purpose does that serve?"
Yet again, Alistair lowers his voice.
His lower lip trembles when he confesses, "The--the real rulers of Gloughchester have eyes and ears everywhere. A simple game of bridge is hardly ever a--a simple game of bridge. Humble invitations from Ancillae like...her can bring out things that one wouldn't otherwise wish to be dragged out."
Gregory's face scrunches in confusion, "What the hell are you on about? What do you mean by 'her'? Who are you referring to?"
Alistair sucks some air in through his teeth and stammers, "Oh--oh dear! Is--is that the time? I have some...err...rounds to complete before sunrise. I'd love to keep chatting, but I'm afraid that this is where we must part ways for the night, o--old boy."
Gregory's brows knit together and he side-eyes Alistair. In a deeply suspicious voice, he says, "Right. Of course. As you wish."
Cassandra's Domitor stands up with a groan and is hastily (but not impolitely) ushered out of the Haven. The moment he steps outside, Alistair bids him a goodnight and shuts the door behind him.
He takes a large breath and pushes his hair back before picking up his pipe again. He brings it to his mouth and shakily takes a puff from it. His entire body starts trembling, and he quickly moves to turn the lights off before scurrying upstairs.
The former doctor pulls the curtain aside and watches Gregory retreating into the distance. He closes his eyes and leans against the window frame, his entire body quaking.
He mumbles to himself, "You could've been a part of the--the inner circle- the true rulers of--of this Domain! Why the hell did you go and withdraw y--your application, old boy? And what does that kind of disrespect spell for not only you but also your Ghoul? They don't tolerate such things!"
Alistair leans closer to the glass, his forced breaths becoming heavier and more pronounced, "The--the real rulers of this city don't take too kindly to people who waste their 'precious' time. And to think that all it takes is--is a few innocuous games of bridge to decide and--and determine this city's fate for--for years to come!"
The NHS admin grabs the windowsill and leans even closer to the glass, the stress (and pressure) of it all driving him quite mad. He knows things. He is privy to information that he'd very much NOT be privy to.
But complicated and vested interests prevent him from divulging such disturbing secrets to every soul (damned or otherwise) that he comes across.
Alistair uneasily takes a puff from his pipe and blows it all out through his nostrils. The former doctor grimaces at the thought of...her (that Ancilla, whoever she is) finding out about what was spoken on this night.
He truly believes that Gregory would be put through the wringer- his words being twisted around until they say something completely different than what was actually said.
Alistair shudders at the very thought and tells himself, "For his sake- and hers, but mainly his- I sincerely hope that the 2 of them NEVER cross paths. Ever!"
After shuffling into bed, Alistair finishes smoking his pipe and grabs a book from his collection. He groans while peering at the cover and internally curses his insatiable need for Aesthetic Fixations.
In the coming weeks, Mister Gregory best be careful about who he knocks around with! He better watch out!
Elsewhere...
A tall building made of reflective glass and steel is illuminated from within by countless electric lights. They emit a harsh glow that lights up the whole interior, allowing those inside to see clearly.
Any acquired schematic or architectural plan would say that this structure- located right in the heart of Gloughchester- has 15 floors. At least that's what most see from the outside. Most folks don't really stop to think that something might be amiss here.
The interior is stark, sleek, and modern. Reflective tiled flooring, leather furniture, glimmering metal decorations, glass panes all over, and digital screens broadcasting news from across the world are amongst the various elements that help define this place's character.
Official records and documentation would say that numerous business (and tech-related enterprises) occupy a vast majority of this particular building. They wouldn't be entirely wrong.
And yet...there's another floor right at the top. A floor invisible to the naked eye. A floor that the Sleepers aren't permitted to see for a myriad of reasons- reasons that they'll never comprehend. 1 of which is the very nature of this "extra" floor.
The 16th floor is fortified, electronically shielded, and inaccessible by any means- mundane or otherwise. Full body scanners are installed on either side of the front door. These scanners ensure that no "undesirables" get in here.
And the multitude of armed security guards and other security measures prevent said intruders from going too farther in.
At a fully air-conditioned control room, rife with bright monitors and sleek panels, a handful of people sit behind rows upon rows of computers. They're all wearing headsets and typing away on their keyboards while streams of data pass by on the screens.
Words are barely exchanged. Eyes scan across the screens and fingers hit the keys at blistering speeds. Anything suspicious is flagged and sent higher up the chain of command for further analysis. 1 thing's for sure- this operation runs like a well-oiled machine.
Captain Mark Meadows stands at the forefront, chin held high, hands clasped behind his straight back. He is dressed in a pair of high-waisted slacks (sans belt), a tucked in shirt, and combat boots. All somewhat professional for a man of his stature.
The leader of this Branch 16 operation looks at the man next to him. He's 30, on the shorter side, balding, has glasses, wears a white dress shirt with an orange tie, and carries a tablet with him. He taps on the tablet, nods to himself, and looks up at the Captain.
Captain Mark looks straight ahead, "Norman. This is...quite the building. 16 floors in a 15-storied structure. You people...frighten me."
Norman softly chuckles, "We are on the same team, Captain Meadows. We want the same thing: the eradication of the blank body threat in the United Kingdom. With you in charge of B16 affairs and me lending support, I'm confident that we'll get the job done."
Mark shakes his head and huffs, "What I'm interested in is results. It's what my superiors are expecting, and I won't let them down. And as far as we are concerned, YOU answer to me, magician--"
Norman politely interjects, "I prefer the term 'Mage'. Calling us magicians is...crass, sir. Not to mention, horribly reductive."
Mark continues, "And I expect to see a report detailing how we're going to neutralize the blank body threat...with your complete cooperation. Do I make myself clear, Mage?"
The Awakened Mage plainly nods, "Crystal, sir. Since you're here, might I raise a small concern with you? A quick and brief one, if I may."
The Captain grits his teeth and looks down at him, "Make it quick."
"Ahem. Yes. The auxiliary guards- Gen4 Security. As it is, we're having issues maintaining...err...Consensus with this many people around. This structure has only 15 floors, yet they walk around on the 16th floor. It's not easy to keep all of their doubts...subdued, so to speak."
"Yes. Your magics strain credulity. What do you propose?"
"Can we perhaps cut down on the Gen4 personnel? Say about 20%"
Captain Mark scoffs, "You've lost your damn mind, Mage. 10%"
Norman scratches his lower lip with his thumb, "Meet halfway through, then? 15%"
Captain Mark stifles a sigh, "Very well. But I trust that the other security protocols we have in place will be...adequate?"
Norman replies, "Above and beyond, sir. Might I ask why you chose these operatives in particular?"
Mark peers down at him, "I am familiar with their operational histories and protocols. And my daughter- Ashleigh- is an employee of theirs. She's always been her own girl, that one. She runs private security for very important persons."
Norman's eyebrows go up, "Ah. A familial connection. Intriguing. Far be it from me to overstep my bounds, but I have to ask: will you let that which is personal get in the way of your stated mission objectives?"
The Captain blinks a few times and glances around, "Operation Brightburn is my life, now, Norman. I live and breathe it day in and day out. Nothing else matters in comparison to that. I've come too far and sacrificed too much to just...make a dent in these...enemies of all that is good, honorable, and decent in our nation."
Norman smiles thinly, "We'll make this city a bastion of safety. Too bad about Birmingham, eh? From what I've gathered, they left you a little present. Impaled with rebar and exsanguinated. My, my. What a sight."
Captain Mark tersely responds, "Our foes have no honor."
The Mage shrugs, "We are here- ready and willing- to assist you. All you need to do is ask and we shall be more than happy to deliver."
Mark assertively nods, "And so shall I. But you don't do this for me personally. You do this for Queen and Country. Do you understand, Mage?"
"We are far more alike than you think, sir. We- of all people- understand what is at stake here."
Mark tells him, "Very good. Indulge me for but a moment, if you please?"
"Yes?"
"Why can't you Mages just snap your fingers and turn all of the blank bodies into ashes. Your powers over reality- disturbing as they may be- are useful in situations like these. What holds you back from taking such a measure?"
Norman nods while bringing up his tablet, "I understand where you're coming from. But reality, more often than not, tends to bite back. Broadly speaking, we refer to that as 'Paradox'. In theory, we could, say cause a worldwide sunrise that would burn every vampire out and about. But the Paradox Backlash would be nothing short of catastrophic."
Mark plainly says, "In other words, your magic has constraints and isn't as all-powerful as it seems."
Norman notes, "True Magick is equal parts art, equal parts science. When in doubt, best not to cast anything lest one accidentally cause...the end of things as we know it."
Captain Mark laughs softly, "If only you could make my job a easier with a snap of your fingers. Then, I would take my retirement and live out the remainder of my days in peace. Buy a farmhouse here, a country house there..."
The Mage chuckles along, "A good reason as any to push for results sooner rather than later. But it's still best to take it all in stride. Oh, a phone call should be coming for you...any...second...now!"
And just as the Captain is about to say something, his secure cell phone starts ringing. It's the kind of ringtone that people would use when they want to be certain that their phone rings. He narrows his eyes, a look of disbelief and confusion asserting itself on his face.
He answers the call and clears his throat, "Hello?"
The stern woman on the end of the line skips the pleasantries and demands, "Situation report, Captain Mark Meadows."
Captain Mark stands straighter, licks his lips, and informs her, "Ma'am! Branch 16 operations successfully up and running on the 16th floor of...err..."
Norman silently mouths, "The Blythe Building."
The leader of the operation continues, "The Blythe Building. We have made contact with the local...Mages and integrated their expertise into our overall security networks. Several leads have already been identified, and we are prioritizing those that may pose the greatest risk to the Royal Family, as per our updated operational protocols."
She continues to be terse and blunt, "Very good. Operation Brightburn has always been your brainchild. As such, we expect results soon. Are we clear?"
"Yes, ma'am."
(Captain Mark Meadows is a very "yes, ma'am!" type of fellow).
He pauses for a moment before asking, "Will there be anything else, ma'am?"
She straightforwardly asks, "Are your assets ready to begin hacking through the phones?"
"We can commence operations at any time, ma'am. Analysts are on standby to go through hours upon hours of voicemails, text messages, and phone calls. Our keyword list has been updated thanks to our primary contact."
"Ah. Him. Keep that one on a short leash, Captain."
Captain Mark sighs a little, "Even now, I--I still do not know how he possesses such vast, intimate, and oddly peculiar information about the blank body threat. He is rather curt with me, ma'am. He won't give me the whole picture. I am--"
She interrupts him, "But his intel has proven valuable in the confirmed killings of numerous blank bodies across the United Kingdom. Results, Captain Meadows. Results. You would do well to remember that."
"Yes, ma'am. I will continue to work with him...and these...Mages."
Norman smiles politely.
Captain Mark walks away to a secluded corner and lowers his voice, "Ma'am, are we sure that such people can be trusted?"
"We wouldn't be working alongside them if we weren't certain about their loyalty to Queen and Country, Captain Meadows. But we do have certain protocols in place. Should they either prove too volatile or outlive their uses to us, then we'll simply terminate their status as an 'ally' and let the rest be handled from there."
Mark asks, "T--targeted assassinations, ma'am?"
"Yes. We have the names of their families, friends, loved ones, lovers, children, grandchildren, close associates...anyone that could be used against them should such measures ever be required."
"Is the integrity of Operation Brightburn being threatened, ma'am?"
"What do you think, Captain?"
Captain Mark looks uncertain and it reflects in his tone, "I still do not trust reality-benders, ma'am. Such powers ought not to be in anybody's hands. The kinds of things that they could do...they--they're at odds with--with EVERYTHING, if you will."
"Hmm. While you raise valid points, we have agreed to enter into this alliance for the good of Queen and Country. Britain needs all the protection it can get. These...Willworkers are a necessary evil, and it's up to us to keep them from getting out of control."
Captain Mark does a low bow even though no one can see him, "As you wish, ma'am. What does B16 leadership have to say about working with these Awakened Mages, ma'am? This...Mr. Norman seems confident in his own abilities."
She answers, "Many of Branch 16's top brass share your concerns about entering into such complex alliances. Over the next few months, we'll convene and meet to determine whether or not these measures were worth taking."
"Very well, ma'am."
"Speaking of measures worth taking, how goes your interrogation of that prisoner?"
"Ah. Him. Even after all these years, he is proving uncooperative. For a blank body, he has a rather high pain threshold. But his mind is--is truly something else, ma'am. I feel guilty every time we have to waste bags and bags of blood on that creature. But the intel which we 'get' out of him has proven to save British lives, ma'am."
"If it proves too much of a budgetary constraint, then you may have to liquidate him."
Captain Mark lifts his chin and asserts, "I shall do so with the utmost pleasure, ma'am. Will that be all?"
"Yes. God save the Queen."
"God save the Queen!" he echoes, putting all of his conviction behind those words.
With that, the call ends. Captain Mark slowly exhales, scrolls through some messages on his phone, smiles faintly, and heads back to Norman. The Mage is swiping his finger across his tablet's screen when he senses that Captain Mark has returned.
"Your call go swimmingly, sir?"
"Yes. Just fine."
"Hmm. For what it's worth, I do very much look forward to working together on a common goal. A rather ambitious one at that."
Captain Mark raises an eyebrow, "Operation Brightburn isn't merely 'ambitious', Mage. It is a necessity. And you will answer to me and only me."
"It's your call, Captain. I trust that my own years of service have been useful to the Crown thus far. What of the boots on the ground? I could provide some from my end, but you were quite reluctant to accept them."
"I stand by my previous statement, Norman. I want MY people to deliver the killing blows. You people are merely the emergency button behind the glass. The glass which, hopefully, shall remain unbroken."
Norman chuckles slowly, "Yes, yes. We have, let me see...1 second..."
He taps on his tablet and brings up dossiers upon dossiers of personnel files to the large screens in front of him. Captain Mark folds his arms and starts scanning through all of this information.
He mumbles, "Ex-SAS, Royal Marines, MI5, MI6...Trojans, all well-seasoned operatives. Yes, my kind of crowd. When they arrive, ensure that they have the best possible lodgings."
(War criminals and scum drenched in innocent blood, the lot of them).
Norman comments, "It shall be done, sir. As a matter of fact, many more from your native Hereford are scheduled to arrive within the next few hours, sir."
"Good. Ensure that they are fully briefed on the situation. I do not want Britain's finest warfighters walking into a situation such as this with subpar intel, got it? Any information pertaining to the blank body threat must be fully disclosed to them. If I hear that you've failed to divulge--"
Norman clicks his tongue, "With all due respect, threats are beneath you, sir. Don't push us around and treat us like your butlers. We know what's at stake here, and we know how to achieve our common mission directives- we are not amateurs at this sort of thing. Like yourself, we were commissioned to serve Her Majesty."
Captain Mark stares straight ahead, "I will be the judge of that. Dismissed."
The Mage glares at the Captain as he turns on his heel and strides off to oversee the operations. Once the Mage is out of sight, Captain Meadows breathes a small sigh of relief and lets his tense shoulders sag ever so slightly.
The forces of the SI are in town. How will the true rulers (tm) of Gloughchester react to the threat posed by Branch 16? Will the existing power structures be shaken up? Merely stirred? Violently destabilized? Something else entirely unforeseen?
Only time will tell!
Cassandra is back in the ever so familiar coziness and comfort of her cabin, the aromatic scents of various outside perfumes from flowers, leaves, fruits, and more wafting about. She dozes off after washing up and changing into a simple pair of loose shorts.
The bra hugs her voluptuous chest just right, though she could go without wearing it. But she was far too tired to unhook it. As Cassandra sleeps, there are all sorts of fantastical images dancing in her dreams.
Uh-oh.
Sven the Baali rockstar (and somehow a member of the Sabbat) invades the Ghoul's psyche by walking out of a wall of moving fog. He's dressed in a half-tucked shirt, designer jeans, and a black leather jacket slung over his shoulder.
He greets her excitedly, "Yo, yo! Miss me? 'Course you did. Reminder: you're gonna be seeing a LOT of me, so you better get used to it now. Alright? Alright. How was your night, Cassie? Oh..."
The Infernalist pauses for dramatic effect and scans the frozen Ghoul's face, "...lemme see. I already know! Hahahahahaha!"
Cassandra is helplessly stuck. Shivers run down her spine as Sven paces a tight circle around her, guffawing and gloating in equal measure. The Baali vampire is very pleased with himself. Despite being on the run from this Domain's authorities.
Sven stops laughing and rubs his palms together, "I can't believe the Sheriff threatened you like that. But damn, is she hot! Is she hot, Cassie? Is she your type? Ah, anyway, let's see...oh you met with some scrawny dude who's gonna walk to...freakin' Egypt! Well, OK. Whatever, I guess."
The area around them is oppressively misty. Sven leaves a small trail behind him as he walks back and forth, casually gesturing with his hands.
A look of sympathy washes over his face, "Oh, poor you. You--you think I'm...bad? A 'posh wanker'? Hey, that kinda hurts my feelings. Look, I'm not bad, OK? In a way, I'm just like you, hopelessly and helplessly addicted to the high. Y'know...the high?"
He asks it in such a way that sends tingles down Cassandra's spine. Sven steps back and grins knowingly.
The Demon Worshipper looks at something over his shoulder and shudders, "And you can say that we're not alike. But I know Ghouls, Cassie. You don't get your sippy-sip and it's game over in the most horrific way imaginable. Damn. Talk about walking a tightrope, huh?"
A high-pitched howl emanates from all over. It sounds like the screeching of a million bats being poked with a billion tiny needles. Sven's eyes widen and he gulps a little.
He hurries up his talking, "I--I'm addicted to the high of...not the Blood, per se. But the success, legions of my adoring fans, the money, the fame, the guys and the gals, the afterparties, the TV appearances, the...music, and...and...the other stuff that comes with being wildly successful. And it's not easy being this good-looking, y'know?"
Sven smirks and puts in, "And oh boy, the amounts of married couples I've had threesomes with. It'd make the most hardened of porn stars blush. So yeah, I'm addicted to the high. Fatally? Hopelessly? Who's to say, hmm? And I've got my...buddies tagging along for the ride..."
Yet again, he fearfully glances over his shoulder, "I gotta go. But I'll be back. Since you're gonna go and complain to Sheriff Alice, tell her to stop trying to find me- she won't. Ever. And let's both hope that she doesn't point that big fuck-off pistol of hers at your face again, yeah? Hahahahahahahahaha!"
Sven takes a few steps back and merges with the fog. Numerous spindly (and slimy) limbs emerge from the ground and make their way towards Cassandra. She can't move, can't scream, can't do anything but watch in abject horror as a bunch of tendrils shoot up and ensnare her.
